which would need to be separately established or adopted for any domain
where it is wanted. Lower instantaneous hazard corresponds qualitatively
to greater expected longevity, all else equal; the exact expected
remaining lifetime depends on the future hazard trajectory, not merely
its instantaneous value at t, and requires a specified survival-function
model to compute precisely --- two containers with identical
Ω\_hazard(t\_0) can have very different expected remaining lifetimes if
ones hazard falls rapidly afterward and the others stays elevated. This
document does not commit to a specific survival-function form, and the
qualitative, instantaneous relationship above is what the diagnostic and
comparative uses §10.5b puts Ω\_hazard(t) to actually require, not a
claim about expected lifetime as a direct, computable function of
instantaneous hazard alone.

\textbf{Axiom C3 (Verticality is Rigid).}

For any container C, as verticality V(t) increases beyond a
domain-specific threshold Θ\_V ∈ (h\_min, 1), the container undergoes a
phase transition from a semi-rigid matrix toward a closed-circuit
configuration, characterized by Π\_f(t) → 0 (feedback permeability
collapses), D\_op(t) → 1 (official-operational divergence maximizes),
d\_A(t) → 0 (adaptive capacity contracts), and R\_g(t) → 1 (rigidity
functional reaches maximum, §10.4).

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.0.1 (Verticality and the High-Entropy
Regime).}}

In the canonical high-entropy configuration, characterized by deceptive
shielding (Definition 10.5b.13b.8) --- ordinary predatory or
self-concealing high-entropy dynamics, the case this document treats as
default throughout --- a container is characterized by V(t)
\textgreater{} Θ\_V and D\_op(t) ≫ 0, with correspondingly low Π\_f(t).
Its elevated verticality is the structural counterpart of its
self-sustaining, extractive, and blind dynamics: the official layer
appears calm (σ\_o low) because rigid structure has frozen the official
messaging in place, while the operational layer churns chaotically
beneath it (σ\_p high). This conjunctive characterization is not a
universal defining property of the high-entropy regime as such, which
the regime classifier (§10.5b.1) establishes independently through
σ\_o/σ\_p alone: Definitions 10.5b.1.1--1.2's Type A and Type B
full-alignment cases are genuinely high-entropy by that classifier
(operational is entropic, σ\_p high) while carrying low D\_op, since
official and operational have converged --- accurately in Type A,
falsely in Type B --- rather than remaining divided. This proposition
describes the ordinary, divided case; the aligned exception is
Definitions 10.5b.1.1--1.2's own subject and is not additionally
governed by the D\_op ≫ 0 clause here.

\textbf{Axiom C4 (Horizontality is Fluid).}

For any container C, horizontality W(t) is maximized (approaching, but
never reaching, 1 − h\_min) as Π\_f(t) → 1, I(t) → 1, D\_op(t) → 0, and
resources are sustainable (dR/dt ≥ 0, with long-run balance per §7.9.2).
In this state the container exhibits bounded stress (S(t) \textless{}
Θ\_S), high adaptive capacity (d\_A(t) ≈ d\_A\^{}max), low rigidity
(R\_g(t) → 0), and short, bounded recovery time (τ(t), §10.10).

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.0.2 (Horizontality and the Low-Entropy
Baseline).}}

A container in the low-entropy baseline regime (§10.5b.1) is
characterized by W(t) \textgreater{} Θ\_W and D\_op(t) ≈ 0, for some
threshold Θ\_W ∈ (0, 1−h\_min). Its elevated horizontality is the
structural expression of open, negentropic, self-accurate systemic flow
--- the semi-rigid matrix state toward which recovery trajectories tend,
per Definition 10.5b.0.4, without ever reaching full structurelessness.

\textbf{The 90° inversion and tipping outcomes.}

Rather than an operator with an ambiguous, one-to-many output, the
relationship between a container's structural orientation and its
tipping outcome is best stated directly as a labeled transition, since
§10.5b.9's M\_tip thresholds already determine which outcome occurs ---
a separate ``inversion operator'' would only relabel that same
determination.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.5 (Structural Transition Labels).}}

A container beginning in a low-verticality configuration (V(t) ≈ h\_min,
a horizontal field) that undergoes a shock-driven phase transition
(Axiom C3) moves to a high-verticality configuration. From there,
resolution of a subsequent tipping event (§10.5b.9) is labeled:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  terminal lock (previously ``180° flip''): V(t) remains elevated; the
  container remains in a rigid, closed configuration and cannot return
  toward h\_min without external anchoring (§10.5b.13);
\item
  full reorganization (previously ``360° flip''): V(t) relaxes back
  toward h\_min via the ordinary alignment-driven dynamic of Definition
  10.5b.0.1, once I(t) recovers. This outcome is not merely a favorable
  value within the high/medium-entropy dichotomy that produced the
  tipping event in the first place --- it is a genuine exit from that
  dichotomy entirely, landing the container on the low-entropy baseline
  as a categorically different mode, not a better-scoring position still
  defined by the same two-regime framework it just left.
\end{itemize}

Which label applies is fully determined by the M\_tip-versus-threshold
comparison of §10.5b.9; the labels above are descriptive shorthand for
that outcome's structural consequence, not an independent mathematical
object.

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.0.1 (Mass Weighting, Coherence, and
Structural Outcome).}}

In the high-entropy regime, a container's tipping outcome is governed by
the coherence factor κ(t) = 1/(1+D\_residual(t)) of §10.5b.8: residual
divergence outside the converging pair dissipates part of the driving
force as internal friction, but Flip Condition A structurally admits no
full-reorganization route under either of its causal sub-routes
(§10.5b.5), so the momentum that survives dissipation determines only
whether the terminal lock is reached at all (M\_tip ≥ M\_lock), never
which structural outcome results. In the medium-entropy regime, by
contrast, the outcome is governed by fluid-mass weighting (§10.5b.7):
because μ\_p \textgreater{} μ\_o structurally in this regime, the same
external launching force more readily crosses into the
full-reorganization range when the majority (operational) mass is what
converges.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.6 (Ultimate Convergence).}}

A container C undergoes Ultimate Convergence on an interval when:

\emph{V}(t) → h\_min, W(t) → 1−h\_min, B(t) → B\_open,

where B\_open is a boundary operator permitting maximal admissible
exchange consistent with remaining a bounded container --- open,
bidirectional exchange toward open counterparts, with extractive
exchange inadmissible (Definition 10.5b.13b.1), so that the healthiest
boundary is maximally selective rather than maximally open --- while
membership continuity is maintained throughout: χ(t,t′) ≥ χ\_min
(Definition 9.3). This is a thermodynamic transition from a high-hazard,
closed configuration to a low-hazard, open one (Definition 10.5b.0.4)
--- not dissolution, and not a transition to formlessness.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.7 (Identity Persistence, complete
criterion).}}

Combining Definition 9.3 (Part 9) with the semi-rigidity floor
established here: a container C maintains full identity across {[}t′,
t{]} if and only if both (a) χ(t,t′) ≥ χ\_min (membership continuity is
maintained) and (b) χ\_supp(C,s) ≥ χ\_supp,min for all s ∈ {[}t′,t{]}
(§10.5b.0's geometric support ratio, structural base breadth relative to
height, remains above its own admissibility floor throughout) --- i.e.,
structural change alone, however large in V(t) itself, does not end
identity provided both membership continuity (a) and a genuinely
non-collapsing support base (b) hold throughout. Condition (b) is not
V(s) ≤ 1 for all s, which would add no independent content, since V(t)
is defined as h(t) ∈ {[}h\_min, 1{]} (Definition 10.5b.0.1) and is
therefore bounded above by 1 for every admissible container as a matter
of definition, not as a condition any real trajectory could fail.
χ\_supp remaining bounded away from collapse is a genuinely independent,
sometimes-false condition instead: a container can satisfy membership
continuity while its structural base narrows toward a single point of
failure (Ω\_hazard's own κ\_Ω·V\^{}ψ/χ\_supp form already shows why this
matters --- hazard diverges as χ\_supp → 0 regardless of what V(t) is
doing), and this is precisely the loss of bounded-container structure
the criterion needs an independent condition to rule out. Ultimate
Convergence (Definition 10.5b.0.6) is the case where both conditions
hold while V(t) trends toward its floor: identity persists, hazard
falls, longevity increases. Dissolution is the case where (a) fails ---
χ(t,t′) falls below χ\_min because membership has scattered into
unrelated clusters --- regardless of what V(t) is doing at the time; a
container can dissolve while structurally rigid (fragmentation under
sustained high hazard) or, in principle, while structurally relaxed
(gradual absorption into a neighboring cluster). The two failure modes
are structurally distinct even though both end identity, and §8.8a.v's
``complete fragmentation'' outcome and §8.8a's locked-node dynamics are
instances of the former.

\textbf{Relationship to the entropy regime classification.}

Verticality/horizontality (this subsection) and the entropy regime
classification (§10.5b.1, via σ\_o, σ\_p) answer different questions and
should not be expected to jointly triangulate one another from a single
V/W reading. V(t) measures overall structural rigidity --- a property
shared, to varying degree, by both non-baseline regimes, since both
arise from the same shock-branching mechanism (§10.5b.2) departing from
the same baseline. σ\_o and σ\_p measure something orthogonal: which
layer, official or operational, is carrying the apparent volatility. A
high-entropy and a medium-entropy container can therefore present with
comparable V(t) --- both departures from baseline elevate structural
rigidity relative to the semi-rigid matrix floor --- while being sharply
distinguished by σ\_o versus σ\_p, which is precisely why §10.5b.1 uses
the variance signature, not V(t), as the classification criterion.
V(t)'s role is to explain why departure from baseline carries a hazard
consequence (Definition 10.5b.0.4), not to re-derive which departure a
container has undergone.

\hypertarget{b.0.8-sustained-horizontal-matrix}{%
\subsection{10.5b.0.8 Sustained Horizontal
Matrix}\label{b.0.8-sustained-horizontal-matrix}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.8 (Sustained Horizontal Matrix).}}

A container C undergoing Ultimate Convergence (Definition 10.5b.0.6) is
sustained on an interval {[}t′, t{]} when the convergence conditions ---
V(t) = h\_min, W(t) = 1−h\_min, B(t) = B\_open, χ(t,t′) ≥ χ\_min ---
hold throughout the interval rather than only momentarily. Where
sustained, C acquires one further property beyond Definition 10.5b.0.6's
own content: immunity, not merely resilience, to tipping-momentum
accumulation. For any incoming Drive\_B(j,t) or propagated kick
K\_\{j→k\}(t) (§8.8a, §8.8b) directed at C during the sustained
interval, C's own contribution to M\_tip is forced to zero, regardless
of the striking force's magnitude. This is a difference in kind from
Z(t)'s resilience (§10.5b.9a), which lowers the cost of a striking event
that still occurs; a sustained container's striking events do not occur
in the sense of accumulating momentum at all --- there is, as this
document's own imagery for the state has it, nothing there to rock.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.0.3 (Stress Non-Accumulation, Already
Entailed).}}

No new mechanism is required for a sustained container's memory to
remain honest and undiminished while contributing nothing to
informational stress. Axiom C4 already establishes Π\_f(t) → 1 as
horizontality is maximized; §6.7's existing formula, S(t) =
∫K(t,s)(1−Π\_f(s))D(s)ds, already sends the stress contribution of any
given interval to zero as Π\_f(s) → 1 within it, independent of D(s)'s
magnitude. A sustained container's memory operator M\_t continues
recording every event that reaches it, per §9.2's unmodified definition
--- nothing is hidden, nothing is filtered at the point of encoding.
What the existing (1−Π\_f) gating already ensures is that this
honestly-recorded content does not translate into stress accumulation
while Π\_f(t) remains at its sustained-state value. Definition
10.5b.0.8's tipping-immunity is the one genuinely new claim in this
subsection; the memory and stress behavior surrounding it was already
correct once Axiom C4 and §6.7 are read together.

\hypertarget{b.0.9-glimpse}{%
\subsection{10.5b.0.9 Glimpse}\label{b.0.9-glimpse}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.9 (Glimpse).}}

A container touches, without sustaining, Ultimate Convergence when
Definition 10.5b.0.6's conditions hold at some instant or brief interval
failing Definition 10.5b.0.8's duration requirement. Where this occurs,
tag the corresponding segment of M\_t with a glimpse marker g(t) = 1
(g(t) = 0 otherwise) at the moment of encoding --- an ordinary,
undistorted memory-write per §9.2, distinguished only by this marker
rather than by any special content.

\emph{\textbf{Provenance is inherited, not assumed.}} Because g(t) is
set only when Definition 10.5b.0.6's conditions are actually, formally
satisfied, a glimpse is not tagged from a container's own belief or
interpretation that a moment was healthy --- it is tagged from the same
observation and measurement architecture (D\_om, D\_op) already
governing every other diagnostic in this document, subject to the same
accuracy limits. A glimpse recalled from a segment whose original
D\_om/D\_op readings were themselves inaccurate carries that inaccuracy
forward into F\_glimpse's restorative force; no additional grounding
check beyond what already governs D\_om and D\_op at the moment of
encoding is introduced here, because none is needed beyond what the rest
of this document already requires of those same readings.

\emph{Domain instances.} Ecological memory: a brief recovery leaves a
seed bank or propagule legacy that a later disturbance activates; an
organization's brief period of genuine reform, recorded and recalled
under later pressure.

\hypertarget{b.0.10-glimpse-recall}{%
\subsection{10.5b.0.10 Glimpse Recall}\label{b.0.10-glimpse-recall}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.10 (Glimpse Recall and Its Two
Effects).}}

Where a subsequent perturbation --- an external knock, or internal
pressure from entropic-aligned metamessengers --- raises D\_om(t) or
D\_op(t) toward a destabilizing level, glimpse-tagged memory segments'
recall (weighted disproportionately to their recency, via a fitted
glimpse salience κ\_glimpse \textgreater{} 1 applied within K(t,s)'s
existing kernel) produces two separable effects.

\textbf{Immediate effect.} A restorative forcing term, generalized to
aggregate over the full glimpse history rather than a single fixed lag:

F\_glimpse(t) = κ\_glimpse · ∫₀ᵗ K\_glimpse(t,s)·g(s) ds · (D\_om(t) −
D\_om,floor)₊,

using a glimpse-specific kernel K\_glimpse(t,s) within §9.2's existing
memory architecture rather than a new mechanism, entering §6.4's
landscape alongside F\_ext,fb and F\_ext,supp (§10.5b.18.11) as a
further restorative --- not distorting --- contribution, pulling the
container back toward its healthy target band specifically because the
recalled memory carries evidence that the categorical split producing
the current perturbation is not, in fact, load-bearing.

\textbf{Cumulative effect, explicitly conditional.} Repeated glimpse
recalls accumulate into a separate residue quantity, Γ\_glimpse(t),
dΓ\_glimpse/dt = κ\_G · g(t) · (1 − Γ\_glimpse(t)), structurally
parallel to Z(t)'s accumulation but tracking glimpse exposure rather
than survived knocks. Γ\_glimpse(t) alone does not lower h\_target(I(t))
or produce any permanent change in V(t)'s resting configuration; it is a
necessary but not sufficient contributor, gated by conditions external
to Γ\_glimpse(t) itself --- meaningfully favorable Ψ\_total(t)
(§10.5b.18.14), sustained access to healthy external coupling (φ\_fb,
Γ\_supp, §10.5b.18.9--10.5b.18.10), and grid conditions this document
does not claim to fully specify. Where those gating conditions hold,
rising Γ\_glimpse(t) contributes to a gradual lowering of
h\_target(I(t)) over time; where they do not, Γ\_glimpse(t) rises
without producing permanent structural change, and the container's
glimpses remain a source of momentary stabilization only.

\hypertarget{b.0.11-control-versus-influence-and-the-do-no-harm-principle}{%
\subsection{10.5b.0.11 Control Versus Influence, and the Do No Harm
Principle}\label{b.0.11-control-versus-influence-and-the-do-no-harm-principle}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.11 (Control-Shaped and Influence-Shaped
Intervention).}}

An intervention on a container --- internal (§6.1's own representational
apparatus) or external (an adapter or engine term acting on Φ, §6.4) ---
is \textbf{control-shaped} when it forces P toward one predetermined
target regardless of whether that target is where the container's own
unforced dynamics would arrive, foreclosing the range of states
genuinely reachable from where the container currently stands. It is
\textbf{influence-shaped} when it supplies pressure, resources, or
corrective feedback that shifts the conditions or likelihood of the
container's own trajectory without fixing a specific predetermined
endpoint in advance, leaving which state the container actually reaches
an open question its own dynamics resolve.

Every forcing term already built into this document's landscape falls
cleanly into one category or the other. F\_conf, F\_boom, and F\_demand
are control-shaped: each pulls P toward one specific target --- a
confrontation resolution, a false-signal valley, official's demand ---
independent of whether that target is the container's true signal, and
each, per Axiom C1 (§10.5b.15), requires sustained forcing to hold open
and relaxes away once withdrawn. The external feedback and support
channels (φ\_fb, Γ\_supp, §10.5b.18.9--18.11), glimpse-recall
(F\_glimpse, §10.5b.0.10), and emergency firewall reinforcement
(F\_emerg, §10.5b.14.2) are influence-shaped: each restores
permeability, resource stability, or memory of possibility that lets the
container's own dynamics find its own signal, without dictating in
advance what that signal turns out to be.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.0.5 (Control Undermines the Health It
Targets).}}

The formal criterion separating the two categories is their effect on
d\_A(t) (§10.3), this document's own measure of adaptive possibility,
which §14.7 already identifies as constitutive of system health rather
than merely correlated with it. A control-shaped intervention forecloses
which states remain reachable by construction --- that foreclosure is
what distinguishes it as control-shaped in the first place.
Reachable-set foreclosure and a decrease in the particular scalar
d\_A(t) = d\_A\^{}max · exp{[}−β\_S S̃ − β\_R δR̃ − β\_F F̃{]} are not the
same claim on their own --- the exponential form requires stress,
resource debt, or fragmentation to actually rise, which
possibility-foreclosure alone does not automatically produce. The
coupling is not left implicit: Definition 10.5b.0.12's Forcing Tax
(M\_forced) accrues directly into δR(t) whenever control-shaped forcing
is active, and Definition 10.5b.0.13's Forced Rebellion (F\_rebel) adds
directly to divergence pressure feeding S(t), both firing specifically
because the intervention is control-shaped rather than as a separate
assumption. Together these give every control-shaped intervention a
real, derived path to increasing at least one of d\_A's own inputs, and
therefore to contracting d\_A(t) directly --- not by definitional fiat,
but through the two concrete mechanisms already built for this purpose,
independent of whether the specific target the intervention forces
happens to be a healthy one. An influence-shaped intervention does not
foreclose reachable states; where it affects d\_A(t) at all, it does so
only indirectly, by relieving the stress, resource-debt, or
fragmentation terms that §10.3's formula already ties to adaptive
capacity. This holds regardless of where the intervention originates: an
externally-authored control-shaped term does not act as a neutral nudge
from outside the container's own dynamics, because it becomes part of
the same landscape Φ the container's own feedback operator ℱ runs on
(§6.4) --- there is no version of ``control from outside'' that avoids
contracting the container's own adaptive possibility, since the forcing
enters the identical equation regardless of who authored it.

Fundamental Theorem 10.5b.0.5.1 (The Do No Harm Principle ---
Conditional, Not Moral: If the System's Continued Adaptive Capacity Is
the Goal, Force Is Harm). There is nothing paradoxical about holding
influence as a goal --- supplying conditions without fixing an endpoint
is a perfectly coherent objective, not a self-contradiction. The actual
content is a straightforward, near-unconditional claim: control-shaped
force is harm, because it contracts d\_A(t) by construction, in every
case except one --- a container genuinely at the verge of imminent
terminal collapse, where forcing it back from that edge is the one
circumstance in which control-shaped intervention is justified rather
than harmful. Named for what it actually says, not for a philosophical
tension that was never really there.

Proposition 10.5b.0.5.2 (Safety Redefined as Attuned Timing, Not
Control). A direct positive consequence of the theorem above, worth
stating in its own right rather than leaving implicit: since
control-shaped force forecloses the adaptive capacity it is meant to
protect, genuine safety cannot consist in achieving or maintaining
control at all. What the theorem's own influence-shaped alternative
actually requires is attuned observation of a container's real, unforced
trajectory -\/- including the genuinely rhythmic, recurring patterns
already established elsewhere in this document (repeated cycling,
§10.5b.9a.3; the B1/B2 branch point's own recurrence) -\/- and
intervention timed to that trajectory's real shape, rather than an
attempt to fix its outcome in advance. Such a pattern's general shape
can be genuinely predictable -\/- that a cycle recurs, roughly how it
tends to resolve -\/- without its specific timing or outcome being
forceable; attempting to force the outcome anyway is precisely what
activates the Forcing Tax and Forced Rebellion above, converting a
knowable rhythm into a damaged one. Safety, on this reading, is the
skill of moving with an unforceable rhythm at the right moment, not the
elimination of the rhythm's own uncertainty.

Derivation, stated explicitly. This theorem is not an independent
assumption; it follows necessarily from Axiom 9 (Health-Seeking Is the
Default Orientation, Part 3) together with §10.3's own formula for
d\_A(t) = d\_A\^{}max·exp{[}−β\_S S̃ − β\_R δR̃ − β\_F F̃{]}, via three
concrete, already-built mechanisms rather than by assertion:
control-shaped forcing's own definition (§10.5b.0.11) forecloses
reachable states by construction; Definition 10.5b.0.12's Forcing Tax
(M\_forced) accrues directly into δR(t); and Definition 10.5b.0.13's
Forced Rebellion (F\_rebel) adds directly to divergence pressure feeding
S(t). Given Axiom 9's own designation of health as the preservation of
adaptive possibility, and given d\_A(t)'s own dependence on exactly the
two inputs these two mechanisms increase, the contraction of d\_A(t)
under control-shaped forcing is not a value judgment layered on top of
the mathematics --- it is what those axioms and definitions, taken
together, already entail. A framework that asserted this as a fresh,
independent axiom rather than deriving it would be asserting something
already provable from what it had already assumed, which is precisely
the redundancy a minimal, coherent axiom system is supposed to avoid;
the theorems real strength is that it does not need that assertion.

Sustained Ultimate Convergence (Definition 10.5b.0.8) cannot be reached
by a control-shaped intervention, internal or external, without
contradiction. If Ultimate Convergence is genuinely the container's own
unforced fixed point, forcing toward it was never necessary --- the
container's own dynamics reach it once whatever currently obstructs them
clears, per Axiom C1. The single exception, and only one mechanism
actually qualifies. External, anchor-based intervention is never this
exception: an anchor that forces a container open before it is ready
cannot, by that same act, be a trustworthy anchor to it --- trustworthy,
steady support and force toward a predetermined state are mutually
exclusive by what those words mean, not merely in practice. Permeability
flooding (§10.5b.13.2) is not an exception at all, on either count: it
is not caused by the anchor to begin with (the anchor's own role there
is containment of a flood already underway from real, internal causes,
never production of it), and even if it were, force from an external
party could never be the trustworthy support the Do No Harm Principle
would need to set aside its own prohibition for. The one genuine
exception is Medium-Entropy Systems Override (§10.5b.14.3b)
specifically: operational's own intact, genuine intervention on its own
system, either preventing hull breach or steering an already-occurring
collapse toward reset, gated on the container already facing a worse
alternative without it. This is self-action, not an external party
forcing anything, which is why it can be the exception where
anchor-based force cannot. High-Entropy Systems Override (§10.5b.14.3a)
is not an exception either, despite sharing the name: it is
control-shaped, self-serving rather than self-preserving in the relevant
sense, and remains a harmful mechanism under the Do No Harm Principle
like every other high-entropy control assertion. Control-shaped force
remains harm in every case except a healthy system's own genuine action
on itself to reverse an immediate collapse already underway; it is never
a general license for an outside party to force a good-looking outcome,
however the outcome is described.

Definition 10.5b.0.12 (Forcing Tax). Independent of and additional to
Proposition 10.5b.0.5's possibility-foreclosure argument, a
control-shaped intervention imposes a real, distinct resource cost on
the container experiencing it, structurally parallel to M\_decep's own
self-deception tax (§10.5b.18.4): M\_forced(i,t) = κ\_forced ·
F\_control(i,t), κ\_forced \textgreater{} 0 fitted, where
F\_control(i,t) is the magnitude of control-shaped forcing currently
acting on i, summed across whichever control-shaped terms (§10.5b.0.11)
are active. M\_forced(i,t) accrues into δR(t) exactly as M\_decep does
(§7.9's convention). This is a real, second cost distinct from
foreclosure: possibility-foreclosure narrows which states remain
reachable directly, by construction; the forcing tax is a real resource
drain the container incurs simply from being forced, independent of and
prior to whatever the forced outcome turns out to be. Both apply
simultaneously to any control-shaped intervention; neither substitutes
for the other.

Definition 10.5b.0.13 (Forced Rebellion). A further, distinct, real risk
beyond both of the above: a container subjected to control-shaped
forcing can respond with active, chaotic pushback against the forcing
itself, rather than merely absorbing the possibility-foreclosure and
resource tax passively. Define a real rebellion-response term feeding
directly into the container's own divergence pressure: F\_rebel(i,t) =
κ\_rebel · F\_control(i,t) · (1 − Trust(source,i,t)), κ\_rebel
\textgreater{} 0 fitted, where Trust(source,i,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} is the
forced container's own genuine trust in the forcing source, lowest for
an outside party asserting force and by definition unavailable to any
anchor relationship, since a source that forces cannot simultaneously be
the trustworthy anchor Trust(source,i,t) would require. F\_rebel(i,t)
enters the hull-breach inequality's own left-hand side (§10.5a) as an
additional contribution to divergence pressure, capable of triggering a
genuine tipping event that would not otherwise have occurred at that
time --- forcing does not merely tax or narrow the container, it can
itself become the proximate cause of a real collapse. Critically, a
tipping event triggered this way resolves through this document's own
existing, independent B1/B2 mass-weighting machinery
(§10.5b.7--10.5b.9), not through any mechanism favoring a good outcome:
the forcing party's good intentions have no bearing on whether the
resulting event resolves as reorganization or terminal lock. This is the
formal content of forcing toward health being genuinely dangerous rather
than merely inefficient --- it can provoke the very collapse it was
meant to prevent, with a real chance of resolving into pure collapse
rather than the reset it may have been intended to produce.

Definition 10.5b.0.13a (The Bracing Threshold: Duration and Reserve as
Independent Factors). Self-directed control-shaped bracing (a
container's own forcing applied to itself in response to an external
threat, distinct from §10.5b.0.11's forcing from an outside party) is
not uniformly costly regardless of intensity: a brief, self-directed
bracing response to a genuine, momentary threat can be a net-positive
short-term stabilizer, while the identical response sustained against a
chronic or repeated threat becomes the self-damaging pattern this
document's forcing machinery otherwise describes. Two factors
independently determine which regime a given instance falls into, not
one: duration/repetition of the triggering threat (τ\_threat(t),
cumulative or repeated exposure rather than a single momentary event)
and the container's own current resource reserve (R(t)/R\^{}max, §5.6)
at the time bracing is sustained. Bracing tax,
reserve-and-duration-dependent: M\_brace(i,t) = κ\_brace ·
F\_control\^{}self(i,t) · {[}τ\_threat(t)/τ\_ref{]} · {[}1 −
R(t)/R\^{}max{]}, κ\_brace \textgreater{} 0 fitted, τ\_ref a fitted
reference duration below which self-directed bracing is not charged this
tax at all -\/- the two bracketed factors are independent multiplicative
contributors, not substitutes for each other: a well-resourced container
can sustain longer bracing before the tax becomes significant, and a
depleted container is vulnerable to the same tax even from a
comparatively brief threat, and either factor alone, at a sufficiently
extreme value, can drive M\_brace(i,t) high regardless of the other
factor's own value. This is the formal content of a defensive response
that is adaptive at low duration or high reserve becoming actively
pathological as either duration lengthens or reserve depletes, without
requiring the underlying threat itself to have changed in kind -\/- the
same bracing mechanism, mechanically unchanged, crossing from adaptive
to self-damaging as its own two real cost drivers move.

Definition 10.5b.0.13b (Impact-Force Regime and the Rigid/Fluid Response
Flip). A third, independent dimension, orthogonal to duration and
reserve above, governs which response mode -\/- rigid (bracing,
solid-like resistance) or fluid (yielding, actively redirecting incoming
momentum rather than resisting it) -\/- actually minimizes damage for a
single, acute impact event: the peak force or velocity of that event
itself. Below a domain-specific threshold F\_c, rigid response minimizes
damage, which is why bracing is the evolved, intuitive default response
across biological systems -\/- historically, most survivable impacts a
given lineage encountered fell below F\_c, making rigid response the
correctly-calibrated default rather than a design flaw. Above F\_c, the
optimum flips: fluid response -\/- yielding into the direction of
applied force and actively redirecting momentum, rather than resisting
it -\/- produces measurably less structural damage than rigid resistance
to the same event, precisely because rigid resistance at high force
concentrates the full impact at the point of contact, while fluid
redirection distributes and converts that force into distributed motion
away from the vulnerable structure. This is not a difference of degree
but a genuine regime flip: the same mechanism (internal structural
resistance to an external force) that reduces damage below F\_c actively
increases it above F\_c, and a system whose response mode does not
switch with the regime -\/- one still bracing reflexively at force
levels above F\_c -\/- suffers worse structural damage than one that
does not resist at all.

This is the same underlying principle already established for a fluid
medium under §Axiom V2's horizontality, run in the complementary
direction: a liquid, ordinarily the more fluid of the two phases, itself
flips to solid-like behavior under a sufficiently high-velocity,
near-perpendicular impact (a high-velocity dive, a wave striking a fixed
structure at a steep angle) precisely because the impact timescale is
too short for the fluid to flow and displace before the collision is
already complete -\/- the medium's own capacity to redistribute is
exceeded by the rate at which force is being applied, the same
F\_c-crossing logic stated for the opposite material starting point. A
part-solid, part-fluid system (any organism, and many non-living
composite structures) sits between these two starting points, and the
same crossing governs it in the direction relevant to its own survival:
below F\_c, acting more solid (rigid, resistant) minimizes damage; above
F\_c, acting more fluid (yielding, redirecting) does, which is the
formal content of why a genuine, deliberate override of the reflexive
bracing response -\/- consciously yielding and redirecting rather than
resisting -\/- becomes the correct, healthier response specifically once
impact force crosses F\_c, rather than being simply weaker or more
passive than bracing.

Proposition 10.5b.0.13c (Fluid Response Is Active Redirection, Not
Passive Absorption). The fluid-mode response above F\_c is mechanically
continuous with Mid-Collapse Steering (§10.5b.14.3b.ii) rather than a
separate mechanism: in both cases, an already-occurring, unstoppable
process (an impact already in motion; a collapse already past
hull-breach) cannot be prevented at this point, and the genuinely
healthy response is not passive submission to it but active steering of
its resolution toward the least damaging outcome available -\/-
redirecting the body's own motion in the direction of an already-folding
joint, converting a concentrated, localized force into distributed,
controlled motion that removes force from the vulnerable structure
entirely, is the same shape of intervention as steering an
already-occurring Flip B trajectory toward B2 rather than B1: neither
prevents the triggering event, both determine, through active
participation rather than resistance or inaction, which of the available
resolutions is actually reached.

Proposition 10.5b.0.13d (Two Independent Mechanisms, Not Three, and
Their Convergence). Definitions 10.5b.0.13a and 10.5b.0.13b are
genuinely independent mechanisms governing different questions, not two
instances of one underlying rule, and stating their relationship
explicitly resolves what would otherwise look like three separate cases
into two orthogonal dials. §10.5b.0.13b alone governs a single, acute
impact event: whether that one event's peak force crosses F\_c,
independent of how long any resulting response is sustained.
§10.5b.0.13a alone governs a sustained response's own affordability:
whether its duration and the container's own reserve level keep it
affordable, independent of how large the originating force was. A
response can therefore fail along either dimension independently, or
along neither: brief bracing against a sub-threshold force is doubly
adaptive (correct response mode per §10.5b.0.13b, affordable duration
per §10.5b.0.13a); bracing against a force that has already crossed F\_c
is maladaptive immediately, regardless of duration, since the response
mode itself is wrong from the first instant (§10.5b.0.13b's failure
alone); and bracing against a sub-threshold force that is then sustained
too long, or attempted with too little reserve, becomes maladaptive
despite the response mode having been correctly chosen at onset
(§10.5b.0.13a's failure alone, arising later than §10.5b.0.13b's own
regime-flip failure would have). Both failure modes converge on the
identical corrective action despite arising from independent causes:
release the brace and shift toward fluid response, whether because the
response mode was wrong from the start (§10.5b.0.13b) or because a
correctly-chosen response has outlasted what §10.5b.0.13a's own
duration-and-reserve accounting can sustain. This convergence is not a
coincidence requiring separate explanation: both mechanisms are
measuring the same underlying quantity, the actual, current cost of
continued resistance relative to its benefit, from two different real
inputs (instantaneous force; accumulated duration and depleting reserve)
that happen to share a single corrective response because releasing
resistance is what addresses excess cost regardless of which input
produced it.

Three falsifiable predictions, one per mechanism, stated explicitly
rather than left implicit in the mechanisms alone. Resource depletion
(Definition 10.5b.0.12): systems under sustained control-shaped forcing
should show measurably faster resource-debt (δR(t)) accumulation than an
otherwise-identical, unforced counterfactual, holding all other inputs
constant. Divergence amplification (Definition 10.5b.0.13): forced
interventions should show a real, measurable rate of triggering the very
collapse they were intended to prevent, with resolution outcomes at
matched B1/B2 mass-weighting indistinguishable from collapses that
occurred without any forcing present at all. Counterfeit convergence
(Definition 10.5b.0.9a, Forced Flattening): systems that appear to
improve under sustained forcing should show rapid relapse to their
pre-forcing state, or worse, once the forcing is withdrawn, at a rate
distinguishable from the ordinary decay profile of a genuine, unforced
Paradoxical Convergence (Definition 10.5b.0.9b). Each prediction is
independently checkable against real trajectory data; a domain where
none of the three holds would be real evidence against this theorem's
mechanisms as stated, not merely against its presentation.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.9a (Forced Flattening).}} Where
observed convergence persists only because of ongoing forcing, it is not
genuine Ultimate Convergence at all, but Forced Flattening --- a
propped-open false state, indistinguishable from genuine convergence by
data alone, that relaxes away the moment the forcing is withdrawn. This
is the operative reason no adapter or engine instantiation should encode
Ultimate Convergence as an explicit optimization target: doing so does
not merely risk failing to reach the state, it risks manufacturing
Forced Flattening, a forcing-dependent counterfeit of it, contracting
the container's own adaptive possibility in the act of attempting to
produce what adaptive possibility is supposed to look like at its
healthiest. Wanting a good outcome does not exempt an intervention from
being control-shaped, and does not exempt it from this consequence.

Definition 10.5b.0.9b (Paradoxical Convergence). The real, positive
counterpart Forced Flattening's prohibition implies but never states.
Genuine convergence is not reached by forcing two divergent layers into
false agreement; it can be reached by a container ceasing to punish
itself for whichever layer's honest signal currently diverges from the
other, allowing real divergence to persist unforced rather than papering
over it. This operates in opposite directions by regime, since each
regime punishes the opposite spike:

\emph{Domain instances.} A control system that stops overriding an
accurate sensor it had been fighting; an organization that stops
punishing internal whistleblowing and lets the honest signal through.

Definition 10.5b.0.9c (Convergence Authenticity Diagnostic). A real,
distinct question genuine Paradoxical Convergence raises but does not by
itself answer: how to tell it apart from a system performing the
appearance of convergence rather than achieving it, given that both can
present as official-layer claims of improvement. The two are
distinguished by trajectory shape, not by the claim itself at any single
instant: genuine Paradoxical Convergence shows the gap between official
claim and operational conduct narrowing over time, D\_op(t) trending
downward as real healing progresses. Fabricated convergence ---
self-interested disinformation operating on the system's own
trajectory-narrative rather than on a discrete event or memory
(§10.5b.18.4's existing account of the latter) --- shows the opposite
signature: official-layer claims growing more extreme, more
credentialed, or more performed specifically in proportion to how far
operational conduct continues to diverge, D\_op(t) itself trending flat
or upward while the official layer's own self-description escalates to
compensate. Define Auth(i,t) = −d(D\_op(i,t))/dt as a real, checkable
authenticity signal: Auth(i,t) \textgreater{} 0 (narrowing) supports
genuine convergence; Auth(i,t) \textless{} 0 sustained alongside
escalating official-layer claims (a rising rate of self-description,
adapter-tracked where available) is the formal signature of fabrication
masquerading as convergence. This gives an actual test rather than only
a warning to be suspicious of grand claims.

Definition 10.5b.0.9d (Official Identity Inversion). A further, specific
pattern distinct from ordinary high D\_op: rather than official simply
overstating or diverging from operational reality, official's stated
identity can be the specific inversion of operational conduct ---
claiming the trait most opposite to what operational actually does,
rather than an exaggerated or vague version of the truth. Define
Inv(i,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the degree to which official's own stated
identity claims and operational's actual conduct occupy opposite poles
of the same trait dimension (adapter-scored per claimed trait, e.g.
stated egalitarianism against measured self-interested extraction, or
stated restraint against measured coercion), rather than merely
differing in degree along it. High Inv(i,t) is a sharper, more specific
diagnostic than high D\_op(i,t) alone: a container can diverge from its
own official claims by simply falling short of them, which Inv(i,t) does
not flag, or by actively inhabiting their opposite, which it does ---
the latter being the more severe and more deliberate-reading pattern of
the two.

Proposition 10.5b.0.9e (Inversion Scales With Verticality). Inv(i,t) is
not independent of regime severity; it is driven by the same underlying
quantity that already governs how much genuine function remains
available to a container. Define Inv(i,t) as a function of V(t)
directly:

Inv(i,t) = 1 − exp{[}−κ\_inv · (V(t) − V\_0)₊{]}, κ\_inv \textgreater{}
0 fitted, V\_0 a fitted floor below which no inversion pressure exists,

approaching 1 as V(t) approaches its own ceiling and approaching 0 as
V(t) approaches V\_0, rather than holding Inv(i,t) as a quantity
unrelated to how vertical the container has become. The mechanism this
formalizes: official's cover role (§10.5b.18.1) becomes harder to
sustain with an honest, moderate gap as genuine function disappears ---
a moderate divergence still leaves a visible seam between claim and
conduct for scrutiny to find, while claiming the complete opposite of
what is actually happening removes any point of comparison an outside
observer could orient around. The less function survives beneath the
form, the more the cover must overcorrect into full inversion
specifically to remain effective as cover, not merely proportionally
worse for its own sake --- which is why the most rigid, most
Form-dominated containers should show the sharpest, most complete
inversions between official identity and operational conduct, not merely
the largest ordinary divergence.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Medium-entropy direction.} A medium-entropy container's
  official layer already treats its own entropic spikes as evidence of
  dysfunction (§10.5b.2.12's self-attributed-dysfunction pathway).
  Ceasing that self-punishment --- registering an entropic spike without
  the accompanying M\_decep-style suppression response --- allows the
  spike itself to dissipate through ordinary relaxation (§6.9) rather
  than compounding into sustained divergence, drifting the container
  naturally toward its own already-intact operational calm rather than
  fighting it.
\item
  \textbf{High-entropy direction.} A high-entropy container's
  operational layer, per §10.5b.13.2's forced-percolation mechanism,
  actively fears and suppresses negentropic spikes reaching it from
  official --- the same operational layer that, per §10.5b.2.19's
  Orientation Valence, holds contempt for what it perceives as
  official's weakness. The parallel positive path here is the reverse of
  the medium-entropy case: not ceasing punishment of entropic spikes,
  but ceasing suppression of negentropic ones, allowing genuine
  negentropic content already present in official (§10.5b.2.13's
  frozen-but-intact official layer) to register in operational without
  triggering the suppression response that currently meets it.
\end{itemize}

d(divergence)/dt ⊇ −κ\_para·𝟙{[}spike registered without suppression
response{]}, κ\_para \textgreater{} 0 fitted, the sign and triggering
spike-type flipped by regime as described above --- both directions
reduce real, measured divergence despite the disagreement itself
persisting through the spike, which is the actual mechanism: acceptance
calms oscillation without requiring the layers to agree first.

\emph{\textbf{Implementation constraint.}} No adapter or engine
instantiation of ℱ, Φ, or P\_C(t) may include Definition 10.5b.0.8's
convergence conditions, or any other specific state, as an explicit
minimization target, weighted term, or optimization objective aimed at
forcing convergence to a predetermined outcome. Glimpse-recall
(§10.5b.0.10), the external feedback and support channels, and emergency
firewall reinforcement remain permitted and encouraged as
influence-shaped, passive or conditions-restoring mechanisms, per
Definition 10.5b.0.11; what is excluded is only control-shaped forcing
toward a predetermined endpoint within the engine's own forcing or
evaluation functions. This constraint is carried forward into Part 15's
adapter contract as a standing prohibition, alongside its existing
warnings against conservative-default misreadings.

Anticipated objection: isn't preserving adaptive possibility itself a
target? If health is defined by d\_A(t), and this document's own
mechanisms are oriented toward not contracting it, that can look like
optimization toward a goal under another name, apparently reintroducing
exactly what the Do No Harm Principle excludes. The distinction that
resolves this is between trajectory control and constraint preservation,
and it is a real, not merely verbal, difference. Specific-state
optimization constrains which trajectory a container is pushed along,
fixing where it must end up regardless of what its own unforced dynamics
would do. Preservation of adaptive possibility constrains only the
conditions under which a container's trajectory remains open, without
fixing which trajectory, among those the conditions leave available, the
container actually takes. The former forecloses reachable states by
construction; the latter's entire content is refusing to foreclose them.
This is why d\_A(t) can be this document's measure of health without
functioning as a control-shaped target in Definition 10.5b.0.11's sense:
nothing in preserving conditions for openness specifies in advance which
open state gets reached.

\textbf{10.5b.1.3 Receptivity, Distinct From Ψ\_total(t)}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.1.3 (Receptivity).}}

Ψ\_total(t) (§10.5b.18.14) measures how much of a container's population
is genuinely negentropic in orientation. It does not measure whether
that population's own feedback can find any purchase. Define 𝓡(t) ∈
{[}0,1{]}, a container's receptivity to corrective feedback, as
decreasing in how completely official, operational, and the
metamessenger population have independently converged on the same
self-model, accurate or not:

𝓡(t) = 1 − (fraction of \{official, operational, metamessenger
population\} currently in mutual agreement on the container's own
valence).

A genuinely large, genuinely negentropic population (high Ψ\_total(t))
can coexist with 𝓡(t) ≈ 0: if official, operational, and even a majority
of the metamessenger population have all independently, and sincerely,
converged on the same false belief (Type B, above), there is no
remaining internal disagreement anywhere for negentropic feedback to
attach to and correct, regardless of how much genuinely negentropic
population exists to attempt it. Population size and receptivity are
independent quantities; a large negentropic population that cannot find
purchase is functionally equivalent, for corrective purposes, to a small
one.

\textbf{10.5b.1.4 Unsteady Official Chaos as a Health Signal}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.1.4 (Instability as Feedback, Not
Disorder).}} For a medium-entropy container, a rise in σ\_o(t)
(§10.5b.1's variance-of-changes measure) that is correlated with a rise
in Ψ\_total(t) should be read as evidence of active negentropic feedback
reaching official from operational, not as evidence of official-layer
disorder for its own sake. More of a container's metamessenger
population agreeing with its own clean operational core is, by
construction, less internal unanimity in maintaining official's captured
story --- an official layer that is unsteady in this specific,
feedback-correlated sense is \emph{more} negentropic overall, not less,
than one rigidly and steadily maintaining its chaotic self-presentation
with no internal pushback at all.

\emph{\textbf{Corollary (Why the Firewall Becomes a Membrane After a
Successful Reset).}} This supplies the causal mechanism §6.1 has stated
without previously explaining it: that the low-entropy baseline's
meta-representation layer is characteristically permeable,
ω\_meta\^{}up(t) ≈ 1, functioning as a membrane rather than a firewall.
Once a medium-entropy container's B2 reorganization succeeds and
official genuinely realigns with an operational core that was never
corrupted, there is no remaining divergence left to manage --- nothing
for a firewall to separate. The meta-layer does not dissolve, however,
and official and operational do not merge: they become meshed, still
distinct and still doing different work at different times, but in open,
bidirectional exchange. The metamessenger population's own function
changes accordingly, from protective barrier to honest instrument: what
the barometer-not-auditor framing (§6.1) calls a reading of systemic
self-deception becomes, once there is no more self-deception to read,
simply an accurate reading of health. A high-Ψ\_total(t) medium-entropy
container is accordingly doubly advantaged relative to a low-Ψ\_total(t)
one: more able to perceive external danger accurately (§10.5b.2.3--2.4),
and more able to reach a clean B2 reset if it eventually tips anyway,
since more genuine negentropic material is already present for that
reset to reorganize around.

\textbf{10.5b.1.5 Marker-Concealed Harm}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.1.5 (Marker-Concealed Harm, Two
Subtypes).}} An official-layer marker read as sufficient proof of a
container's internal state fails in two ways that call for opposite
responses from an external observer (R\_class(t), §16.9.2). Subtype A
(concealed active harm): the marker's official-layer signal (wealth,
professional standing, social integration) functions as active cover for
extraction or predation the container itself is conducting --- the
marker is not merely wrong about the container's wellbeing, it is
structurally useful to the harm, since it reduces the likelihood that an
external observer looks past it. This is the high-entropy pattern in its
starkest form (the regime account above), and the correct external
response is investigation and intervention against the container itself.
Subtype B (concealed suffered harm): the marker conceals genuine crisis
being experienced by the container, with no extraction occurring in
either direction --- a container in acute distress whose official-layer
marker (a stable family structure, continued employment, apparent
functioning) actively signals to observers that nothing needs checking,
precisely when checking might matter most. Here the container is not the
source of harm but its site, and the correct external response is
support and intervention for the container, not against it. Both
subtypes refute ``the marker proves the internal state'' equally, but in
opposite directions --- one hides a harm the container is doing, the
other hides a harm the container is undergoing --- and treating them as
a single undifferentiated case risks directing an observer's response
the wrong way entirely: intervention against a container that is Subtype
B, or support extended to a container that is Subtype A, both compound
rather than correct the original marker failure.

\textbf{10.5b.1.6 Membrane Persistence}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.1.6 (Membrane Persistence).}} No healthy
container's layers merge. Adaptive capacity requires that official and
operational remain distinct enough to be compared, since a divergence
between them can only be registered, and so corrected, where there are
still two things to diverge. A container whose layers had fully merged
would lose the capacity to detect its own drift from operational
reality, and with it the capacity to adapt in response; its adaptive
possibility d\_A(t) (§10.3) would be bounded by whatever its fused state
happened to be, with no internal mechanism left to revise it.
Compartmentalization is accordingly treated as functional in every
container, not as a defect that health removes: distinct layers carry
out distinct work on distinct schedules. The principle holds across
domains. In a eukaryotic cell, the nucleus, cytoplasm, mitochondria, and
lysosomes each carry out separate work, cycling through distinct phases
with checkpoints between them, while exchanging material continuously
through selectively permeable membranes; the nuclear envelope's pores
pass traffic in both directions without the nucleus ceasing to be
distinct. In a human nervous system, regenerative processing proceeds
while conscious operation is offline. In neither case would merging the
compartments produce a healthier system; it would remove the system's
capacity to regulate itself. Relaxation toward the Sustained Horizontal
Matrix (Definition 10.5b.0.8) is therefore a meshing of layers, not a
fusion of them.

Formally, define the container's comparison sensitivity

s\_cmp(t) = ∂D̂\_op(t) / ∂D\_op\^{}true(t),

the response of the divergence the container itself registers between
official and operational to a change in their true divergence. Where the
layers are distinct, s\_cmp(t) \textgreater{} 0. Where they have merged
--- P\_o ≡ P\_p by construction rather than by agreement --- the
registered divergence is identically zero whatever operational reality
does, s\_cmp(t) = 0, and every forcing term driven by registered
divergence (§6.4's feedback operator, F\_conf of §10.5b.3) receives no
signal: correction capacity is lost, not merely unused. Represent the
meta-layer's state by the triple (ω\_meta\^{}up(t), ω\_meta\^{}down(t),
s\_cmp(t)). A firewall is low ω\_meta\^{}up with s\_cmp \textgreater{}
0; fusion is s\_cmp = 0; and a container approaches the Sustained
Horizontal Matrix as ω\_meta\^{}up(t) and ω\_meta\^{}down(t) both
approach 1 while s\_cmp(t) ≥ s\_min \textgreater{} 0, s\_min fitted ---
the meta-layer becoming a fully bidirectional, valve-like membrane,
while the layers themselves persist throughout.

\textbf{10.5b.2 Regime Genesis (Developmental Branching)}

A container does not begin in either non-baseline regime; it arrives
there through a shock event acting on a previously low-entropy
container. Let S\_shock(t) denote a large, discrete, exogenous
perturbation to D(t) or R(t) --- structurally, an extraction event per
§8.6a, a resolution kick per §8.8b, or an equivalent one-off
environmental disturbance.

Define an ambient signal v\_gen(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, sourced either directly
from the container that inflicted S\_shock (transmitted during the shock
itself) or diffusely from the surrounding network's regime composition
(a stochastic, grid-level field). Branching at a shock event resolves
as:

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.1 (Parasitic Verticality, the Content
of v\_gen(t)).}}

v\_gen(t) is not left uninterpreted: its content, regime-generally
rather than tied to any single domain, is entropic feedback recruiting
the developing container into believing it is entitled to seek
verticality --- to extract from and displace onto other systems from a
position of assumed rank --- a vertical script, not merely damage. A
developing container can be harmed by extraction or displacement without
this recruitment; the recruitment specifically is what v\_gen(t)
measures. This gives a structural account of why high-entropy containers
tend toward predation later, rather than only observing that they do:
v\_gen(t) crossing v\_threshold means the container was not merely
damaged during formation but actively recruited into the extractive role
itself.

\emph{\textbf{Domain instantiation, and a corrected name.}} Prior
revisions named this content Parasitic Entitlement, a human-flavored
name for a definition whose own text states it is domain-general.
Corrected here to Parasitic Verticality, consistent with the
verticality/horizontality axioms of §10.5b.0 and their source in Shaking
Bowls' original verticality-is-entropic, horizontality-is-negentropic
distinction. In human and institutional systems specifically, this
vertical script is what §10.5b.2.12 and surrounding text call
entitlement scripting, and the recruitment it drives is entitlement
recruitment: the human-domain name for the same general mechanism, not a
separate one. Other domains take the same content under whatever their
own adapter-supplied vocabulary names an analogous rank- or
hierarchy-seeking signal; nothing in this definition is human-specific.

\emph{\textbf{Differentiated delivery within one source's coupled
neighborhood.}} A single corrupted source is not required to deliver
v\_gen(t) uniformly to every container it is simultaneously coupled to.
One configuration of a small, coupled sub-grid: one node receives
parasitic-verticality recruitment (high v\_gen(t), branching
high-entropy) while another, coupled to the same source at the same
time, receives extraction or displacement without that recruitment (low
v\_gen(t), branching medium-entropy if shocked, or simply damaged
without branching if not). This is a statement about differentiated
delivery from a shared source, not a claim that one node's treatment
causally funds the other's; the two outcomes are correlated by shared
origin, not necessarily linked by direct transfer.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  v\_gen(t) \textgreater{} v\_threshold: the operational layer absorbs
  the corruption --- a discontinuous jump in D\_op --- and the container
  transitions to the high-entropy regime. The official layer remains
  untouched, which is precisely what allows it to continue presenting as
  stable while operational reality diverges underneath it.
\item
  v\_gen(t) ≤ v\_threshold: the official layer absorbs the corruption
  instead --- a discontinuous jump in D\_om --- and the container
  transitions to the medium-entropy regime. The operational core remains
  untouched, pristine, and calm; only the declared/self-presented layer
  becomes visibly chaotic.
\end{itemize}

This is also the structural reason medium-entropy containers reach Full
Reorganization (B2, §10.5b.7--§10.5b.9) more readily than high-entropy
containers do: an intact operational core is something to reorganize
\emph{around}. B1/B2 mass-weighting favors whichever layer holds the
greater functioning resource mass, and a medium-entropy container's
operational layer, having never absorbed the original corruption, is
positioned to be that mass. A high-entropy container has no equivalent
intact layer to weight toward --- its operational core is precisely what
the corruption occupies --- which is why terminal lock, not
reorganization, is its structurally favored outcome.

\emph{Domain instances.} The founding culture of an extractive firm; a
colonial administration trained into extraction; in development,
oncogenic signaling that recruits a tissue into growth at its neighbors'
expense.

\textbf{10.5b.2.2 Degree of Capture and External Threat Perception}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.2 (Degree of Capture).}}

For a medium-entropy container j, define κ\_capture(j) = v\_threshold −
v\_gen(j, t\_0), evaluated at j's own branching moment t\_0 --- how far
below threshold v\_gen(t) fell at genesis, not merely that it fell below
it. This magnitude, previously discarded once the branch itself was
resolved, sets a persistent bias on j's own Ψ\_total(j,t) trajectory
going forward: Ψ\_total(j, t\_0) = 1 − κ\_capture(j)/v\_threshold
(normalized default; adapter-overridable), a container whose official
layer was almost totally captured beginning life with very little
negentropic-aligned population left, and one that barely crossed into
medium-entropy beginning with substantially more.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.3 (External Threat Perception).}} For j
assessing a coupled or potential predator i, j's accurate perception of
i's actual danger uses the observational-depth machinery already built
(§10.5b.18.9.1) rather than a new primitive:

Perceived\_danger(j,i,t) = A\_acc(j,i,t) · Danger\_true(i,t),

where Danger\_true(i,t) is i's own current extractive intensity
(default: D\_op(i,t), i's own official/operational gap, or ℱ\_i(t) where
available). This is perception only --- whether j registers the threat
at all --- and is bounded by the same accuracy ceiling that governs any
container's read of another's true state.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.4 (Low-Confidence Override).}} Whether
j \emph{acts} on Perceived\_danger(j,i,t) is gated separately, by j's
own current Ψ\_total(j,t):

Effective\_perceived\_danger(j,i,t) = Perceived\_danger(j,i,t) ·
g(Ψ\_total(j,t)), g increasing in Ψ\_total,

producing two distinct failure severities on one continuous scale,
rather than two separate mechanisms:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Ψ\_total(j,t) \textless{} θ\_perceive: perception itself fails.
  Perceived\_danger(j,i,t) is gated to zero --- there is no channel left
  through which accurate signal about an external threat can register at
  all.
\item
  θ\_perceive ≤ Ψ\_total(j,t) \textless{} θ\_act: perception succeeds,
  but g(Ψ\_total(j,t)) is too small for Effective\_perceived\_danger to
  cross whatever threshold triggers protective action (decoupling,
  resistance) --- the danger is seen and not acted on.
\item
  Ψ\_total(j,t) ≥ θ\_act: full perception and action.
\end{itemize}

\emph{Domain instances.} Immune checkpoints: T cells register a tumor,
but checkpoint signaling gates their action, which tumors exploit; an
institution with weak internal confidence that fails to act on its own
risk reports.

\textbf{10.5b.2.5 Co-Occurring Developmental Mechanisms and v\_gen(t)
Magnitude}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.5 (Compositional v\_gen(t)).}}
v\_gen(t)'s magnitude, not only its presence, is a function of how many
distinct developmental corruption channels act on a container
simultaneously during its formative window, rather than a single
undifferentiated signal:

v\_gen(t) = v\_gen,base(t) + Σ\_k κ\_k · 𝟙{[}channel k active{]}, κ\_k
\textgreater{} 0 fitted per channel,

with channels including, at minimum, direct vertical recruitment
(Definition 10.5b.2.1's core content), inflated validation signals (in
human development, hyperpraise or idealization; in organizations, a
parent firm's guaranteed support that masks a subsidiary's real
performance), projection of the source's own unmet requirements onto the
developing container, and absorption of the developing container's
boundary into the source's own, foreclosing independent identity
formation (in human development, engulfment or enmeshment; in
organizations, a parent that never lets a subsidiary develop governance
of its own). A container receiving several of these simultaneously
should show v\_gen(t) magnitude well past threshold, not marginal
crossing --- this is the structural account of why some high-entropy
containers show extreme, near-total capture (§10.5b.2.2's κ\_capture
near its own ceiling) rather than ordinary branching, and,
symmetrically, why some medium-entropy containers show comparably
extreme κ\_capture on the official-layer side.

\textbf{10.5b.2.6 Anchors, Distinct From Bidirectional Coupling}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} External feedback and support (φ\_fb,
Γ\_supp, §10.5b.18.9--18.11) are described there only through their
effect on a receiving container, without formally distinguishing their
source from an ordinary coupled neighbor. That distinction matters: a
source supplying stabilizing feedback without entering bidirectional
coupling behaves, and risks, differently from one that does.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.6 (Anchor).}} An anchor relationship,
Anchor(k → C, t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, is unidirectional stabilizing feedback
from source k to container C, distinct from bidirectional coupling
Λ\_kC(t): k supplies corrective forcing reducing C's own D\_om(t) or
S̃(t) without C's own state correspondingly entering k's own resource or
divergence equations the way ordinary coupling requires. This is the
formal home for φ\_fb(i,j,t)/Γ\_supp(i,j,t) specifically when their
source is not a persistently coupled neighbor but is playing this
anchoring role instead --- an anchor is not merely weak coupling; it is
a structurally different relationship, one-directional by definition,
which is why it does not draw a receiving container into the parasitic
cycle (§8.6a) the way accepting ordinary coupling from an unknown source
risks doing.

\textbf{10.5b.2.7 Dormancy, Generalized Beyond High-Entropy}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} §8.6a.6's dormancy is built entirely from
high-entropy-specific machinery (M\_decep,ext) and does not apply to
baseline or medium-entropy containers. Dormancy is a genuine response
available at any entropy level, per Axiom 9's default orientation toward
coupling --- and whether a given dormant interval heals or harms a
container depends entirely on what happens during it, not on dormancy
itself being good or bad.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.7 (Generalized Dormancy).}} A container
at any entropy level is dormant at time t when it has ceased seeking new
\emph{bidirectional} coupling: Σ\_search(i,t) = 0 and no new Λ\_ij is
being actively pursued, a response to formation-stage or
later-trajectory corruption, not a default state (Axiom 9). This
suspends the pursuit of new bidirectional coupling specifically, not all
outward-directed contact: seeking an anchor (§10.5b.2.6) while dormant
is not a contradiction, since an anchor is unidirectional by definition
and was never the kind of relationship this suspension covers.

\emph{\textbf{Nesting with §8.6a.6.}} This is the general condition.
§8.6a.6's high-entropy dormancy is a strictly narrower special case,
requiring this condition \emph{and} the cessation of ongoing extraction:
Σ\_search(i,t) = 0 \emph{and} Σ\_j X\_\{i,j\}(t) = 0. These are
genuinely different state variables --- a container could satisfy the
general condition while still drawing on one existing, unpursued
extraction relationship, failing the high-entropy-specific case while
satisfying the general one. A high-entropy container is dormant in the
full, narrower sense only when both hold; a medium- or low-entropy
container, which has no X\_\{i,j\} to begin with, is fully characterized
by the general condition alone.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Medium-entropy, healthy path.} A medium-entropy container
  prone to persistent coupling with high-entropy predators pauses
  specifically to avoid that trap, then either seeks an anchor
  (Definition 10.5b.2.6 --- not bidirectional coupling, and therefore
  not itself a route back into extraction) or works purely internally,
  using its own negentropic-aligned metamessengers to slowly raise
  permeability between its own operational core and official layer with
  no external party involved. Succeeding at either heals the container's
  future coupling prospects; this is a healthy, deliberate use of
  dormancy, not merely damage limitation.
\item
  \textbf{Medium-entropy, unhealthy path.} A container that stays
  dormant indefinitely specifically to avoid re-exposure to entropic
  feedback does prevent new harm, but simultaneously forecloses the
  positive negentropic feedback that coupling would otherwise have
  supplied --- d\_A(t) contracts through absence rather than active
  damage, the same destination reached by a different route.
\item
  \textbf{High-entropy dormancy.} Serves the same purpose as the
  medium-entropy healthy path --- slowly raising permeability without
  triggering the flooding that produces collapse --- but carries a
  distinct, genuine irony: a container that succeeds in healing to
  medium-entropy through this process is not thereby made safe. Its next
  coupling attempt now exposes it to the opposite role: no longer the
  predator in the relationship, but the kind of container §8.6a's own
  targeting machinery (𝒱(j,t), Definition 8.6a.4) would assess as a
  viable target. Healing here changes which side of the predation
  dynamic a container occupies; it does not remove the dynamic itself.
  Full healing to low-entropy baseline takes substantially longer, and
  most containers do not sustain dormancy that long --- but one that
  does may avoid this specific trap entirely.
\item
  \textbf{Low-entropy, the false-isolation trap.} A healthy container
  may go dormant on a mistaken inference: observing that the containers
  it sees coupling in its network are predominantly entropic, it
  concludes coupling itself is the source of entropy, and that full
  withdrawal will let it settle toward greater stability. The actual
  result is the reverse. Isolation forecloses both the adaptive
  possibility genuine exchange would supply and the accurate external
  feedback needed to stay calibrated to changing conditions --- the
  container's official layer reports calm (nothing is currently
  threatening it) while its actual core drifts toward entropy anyway,
  ungrounded (§6.3.2) by anything outside itself. This is a second,
  genuinely distinct pathway into entropic status, alongside §10.5b.2's
  shock-and-capture genesis mechanism --- not caused by an external
  predator or corrupting signal, but by a healthy container's own
  withdrawal from what it needed to remain self-accurate, motivated by a
  plausible but false theory of what coupling itself does.
\end{itemize}

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \emph{\textbf{Formal entry point, not narrative alone.}} This pathway
  enters the existing regime-classification machinery (§10.5b.1) through
  the same σ\_o(t)/σ\_p(t) mechanism already used to classify every
  other container, not through a separate transition rule. Isolation
  removes external challenge to the official layer specifically --- with
  no coupling, nothing is currently testing or contradicting official's
  own self-report, so σ\_o(t) stays low, genuinely calm. Operational
  reality, ungrounded (§6.3.2) and uncorrected, continues drifting
  regardless, so σ\_p(t) does not stay correspondingly low. Sustained
  long enough, this produces the identical formal signature already used
  to classify high-entropy status --- σ\_p(t) ≫ σ\_o(t) --- arrived at
  by a genuinely different route (self-imposed withdrawal, not external
  predation or corruption) but registered as a regime transition through
  the same existing test, not a new one.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{10.5b.2.8 Grid-Level Extraction Dependence}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} §10.5b.18.25's structural extraction
dependence is stated for a single predator container losing access to a
single class of targets. The same logic applies at the scale of an
entire predator-prey relationship within a grid: a predator population
requires its prey population to retain genuine, ongoing adaptive
possibility, or the coupling collapses entirely rather than merely
locally --- a macro-scale instance of the same principle already
established for an individual parasite losing its host.

\emph{Domain instances.} Firms exiting a market and ceasing to seek
partners; seeds and microbes in dormancy, suspending exchange until
conditions change.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.8 (Grid-Level Coevolutionary
Requirement).}} Let d\_A\^{}prey(t) denote the aggregate adaptive
possibility of a predator's prey population within the grid. The
predator-prey coupling remains sustainable only where both of the
following hold, together, not either alone:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Threshold condition:} d\_A\^{}prey(t) stays above a fitted
  floor θ\_coevolve; if aggregate prey adaptive possibility collapses
  below this floor, the predator-prey coupling itself collapses, taking
  the predator population's own sustaining resource base with it --- the
  grid-scale version of §10.5b.18.25's ``no self-sustaining floor
  beneath it.''
\item
  \textbf{Ongoing forcing requirement:} sustained mutual adaptive
  pressure --- prey developing genuine countermeasures, predator
  adapting in turn --- must continue occurring, not merely have occurred
  once. This is Axiom C1's logic with its polarity reversed: where C1
  says a forced, false state decays without continuous energy to sustain
  it, this says a healthy coevolutionary coupling requires continuous
  adaptive effort, on both sides, to stay away from its own collapse
  boundary; absent that ongoing effort, the system drifts back toward
  θ\_coevolve over time even without any single triggering shock.
\end{itemize}

This is the formal content behind §8.5a's scope distinction (§10.5b.9d):
predation sustained as part of a genuine grid-level cycle is exactly
predation satisfying both conditions above; predation that forecloses
its own prey's adaptive possibility, permanently or by removing the
ongoing forcing that would otherwise renew it, is not distinguishable
from ordinary entropic destruction merely because it crosses a container
boundary --- it fails the same requirement, at a larger scale.

\textbf{10.5b.2.9 Anchor Fidelity}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} Definition 10.5b.2.6 establishes that
anchoring is structurally different from bidirectional coupling, but
says nothing about whether a given anchor relationship is actually
delivering what it claims to. An anchor supplying destabilizing rather
than stabilizing feedback is not a degraded anchor; whatever is
providing it should not be classified as anchoring at all, regardless of
why the feedback is destabilizing.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.9 (Anchor Fidelity).}} A relationship
claiming Anchor(k → C, t) \textgreater{} 0 is legitimate only if the
feedback it supplies actually moves C's own divergence measures
(D\_om(C,t), S̃(C,t)) toward alignment, not away from it. Where the
direction is reversed, two structurally distinct failure modes are both
possible, and require different reclassification, not one shared fix:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Predatory-shaped anchor.} Deliberate, extraction-motivated
  cover wearing an anchor's shape --- reclassifies directly as
  extraction (§8.6a), using machinery already built, once the direction
  of harm is confirmed.
\item
  \textbf{Accidental harmful anchor.} Not predation at all: a
  medium-entropy source genuinely attempting to help, projecting its own
  unresolved, self-deceptive justifications onto the container it is
  trying to anchor, causing real and potentially comparable damage with
  no extractive motive or benefit to itself. This case must not be
  routed through cover-and-lure machinery, which would misdescribe what
  is actually happening --- there is no concealment to detect, only
  well-intentioned harm. It still requires removal as an anchor: intent
  does not change whether the feedback delivered was destabilizing.
\end{itemize}

\emph{\textbf{Standing risk, distinct from current behavior.}} Anchor
legitimacy is not fixed by an anchor's present output alone. A
medium-entropy anchor can be genuinely good for another medium-entropy
system, \emph{conditional} on its own formation-stage corruption not
being reactivated by the interaction --- legitimacy here is contingent
on a trigger state that can change without warning. A high-entropy
anchor can also be genuinely good, specifically where its own
operational core draws something it needs from the anchoring role itself
(standing, acclaim, a sense of purpose) rather than from extracting the
specific system currently being anchored --- but such an anchor carries
real, standing risk toward \emph{any} system it touches, at any time,
because its underlying entropic nature was never actually healed, only
given a socially-rewarded outlet that happens not to require harming
this particular target right now. Current stabilizing behavior does not
retire this risk; it only means the risk has not yet activated toward
the container currently being anchored.

\emph{\textbf{Safety ranking.}} Low-entropy sources are the safest
anchors, and within that category, containers in the semi-rigid
horizontal state (§10.5b.0.8) are safest of all --- specifically because
a high-entropy container being anchored will actively attempt to draw
its anchor into extraction or displacement, and maximal resistance to
being tipped is exactly the property that defeats that attempt. Medium-
and high-entropy anchors are not categorically unsafe, per the
standing-risk distinction above, but neither should be treated as safe
by default the way a low-entropy anchor can be.

The branch is fully determined by which layer absorbs a shock, not by
the shock's magnitude alone, which is why two containers experiencing
comparably severe disturbances can end up in structurally opposite
regimes. Per §8.8b, a resolution kick K\_\{j→k\}(t) supplies both
S\_shock(t) and, via the resolving container's own outcome
(terminal-locked → high v\_gen; reorganized → low v\_gen), the
accompanying v\_gen(t) needed to complete this branching mechanism.

\textbf{10.5b.2.10 The Cross-Container Mirror: Externally Injected
Operational Capture}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} The mechanism above is self-generated,
internal to one container. The same injection can cross a coupling
boundary: a source container's own Inject(k → \{o,p\}, t)-equivalent
population can target a \emph{different}, coupled container's layers,
producing the same Type B confusion in a target rather than an
extractor.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.10 (Externally Captured Operational
Layer).}} For a medium-entropy container j coupled to a source i whose
own population manufactures and injects ``you are negentropic'' across
