the coupling boundary, j's operational layer --- ordinarily the layer
that stays clean in medium-entropy (§10.5b.2.1's branching rule) --- can
itself be captured by the imported signal, coming to believe official's
entropic content is itself negentropic, rather than merely j's official
layer being the one absorbing corruption as the ordinary branching rule
specifies. This is the one structural exception to why medium-entropy
containers are ordinarily more salvageable than high-entropy ones
(§10.5b.1.4's corollary): with \emph{both} layers captured rather than
only official, there is no clean core left anywhere in j's own system
for a B2 reset to reorganize around, removing the safeguard that
ordinarily distinguishes medium-entropy's trajectory from
high-entropy's. The same three metamessenger configurations available to
a self-capturing extractor (aware and restrained by lack of purchase,
Definition 10.5b.1.3; aware and self-deceived; aware and actively
propagating) apply to j's own population here, imported rather than
self-generated.

\emph{\textbf{Vertical recruitment and operational capture are
independent axes.}} Definition 10.5b.2.1's parasitic verticality
determines \emph{direction}: outward, toward extracting from or harming
others, or, absent that recruitment, inward, toward accepting a
container's own destruction as though that destruction were itself the
negentropic outcome. Definition 10.5b.1.2's operational capture (entropy
mistaken for negentropy) determines a separate question: \emph{whether
genuine self-knowledge survives at all}, independent of which direction
any resulting harm points. A container can have either without the
other. Where both are present from a shared originating event ---
vertical recruitment reaching one part of a system, operational capture
without vertical recruitment reaching another --- the same corruption
can produce active harm directed outward in one part of a system and
passive acceptance of destruction in another, from a single source,
without requiring two different explanations.

No new resource-cost variable is introduced for the rewrite itself;
M\_decep(t) (§10.5b.18.4) already prices sustained self-deception on a
divergence-scaled basis, and this mechanism is what that cost is a cost
\emph{of} in this regime --- the ongoing computational and
organizational burden of active falsification, not merely the absence of
disclosure.

\textbf{10.5b.2.11 Receiver-Side Resilience}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} v\_gen(t)'s magnitude (§10.5b.2.5) is built
entirely from source-side and environmental mechanisms --- what a
developing container is subjected to. Without the term defined below,
nothing dampens this based on the receiving container's own intrinsic
resilience, and a sufficiently resilient container experiencing a
genuine developmental knock has no formal route to remaining baseline at
all.

Definition 10.5b.2.11 (Resilience Modifier). For a developing container
j, v\_gen(t)'s effective magnitude is discounted by j's own intrinsic
resilience, r\_res(j) ∈ {[}0,1{]}:

v\_gen,effective(j,t) = v\_gen(j,t) · (1 − r\_res(j)),

with r\_res(j) adapter-supplied or fitted, representing whatever makes a
given container less susceptible to a given magnitude of knock than
another otherwise-identically-exposed container would be. A sufficiently
high r\_res(j) can keep v\_gen,effective(j,t) below v\_threshold even
where the raw, source-side v\_gen(j,t) alone would have crossed it --- a
real, formal route by which a genuinely knocked container can remain
low-entropy, distinct from simply not having been knocked at all.

\textbf{10.5b.2.12 Developmental Pathways Are Probabilistic, Not
Deterministic}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} §10.5b.2.1's vertical recruitment, taken
alone, implies a roughly deterministic mapping: recruitment present →
outward-directed high-entropy predation; recruitment absent →
inward-directed medium-entropy self-attribution. Real developmental
cases show this is better modeled as probabilistic, governed by a
narrower variable than presence or absence of the recruiting signal
itself.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.12 (Script Uptake).}} The operative
variable is not whether vertical scripting was offered, but whether it
\emph{takes root} --- modulated by feedback from other coupled nodes
during the same developmental window and by the developing container's
own intrinsic resilience or fragility (§10.5b.2.11). A container offered
vertical scripting whose uptake fails, because other nodes' feedback or
its own resilience prevented it from taking root, still branches
medium-entropy by ordinary unprotected extraction and displacement,
despite nominally occupying the recruited position. A container not
offered vertical scripting directly may still receive enough of it
indirectly, from the same source's mixed behavior or from the broader
grid, to take root and branch high-entropy despite nominally occupying
the extracted-upon position. Occupying either developmental role shifts
the probability of a given branch; it does not determine it.

Two distinguishable mechanisms populate v\_gen(t) accordingly, both
gated by script uptake above, not guaranteed by exposure alone:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{False negentropic feedback} (superiority/vertical scripting
  --- entitlement scripting, in its human and institutional instance ---
  typically co-occurring with the source's own extraction from and
  displacement onto the developing container): where uptake succeeds,
  this simultaneously forecloses the developing container's own
  independent identity formation, forcing a false developmental
  dichotomy --- extractor or extracted-upon, displacer or displaced-upon
  --- with identification with the aggressor an active developmental
  choice made under that rigged binary, not passive drift. This is the
  more common route to high-entropy branching.
\item
  \textbf{False entropic feedback} (a direct or effectively-direct
  message that the developing container is itself the source of
  dysfunction, whether stated outright or delivered through sustained,
  unprotected exposure to extraction and displacement with no competing
  script offered): where uptake succeeds, this is internalized simply
  and directly at the official layer --- ``you are entropic'' becomes
  ``I am entropic'' --- a genuinely different pathway from the
  high-entropy one, not a milder version of it. This is the more common
  route to medium-entropy branching.
\end{itemize}

\textbf{10.5b.2.12a Differential Outcomes Within Medium-Entropy
Branching}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} §10.5b.2.12 establishes that vertical
scripting whose uptake fails and inaccurate entropic feedback received
directly (§10.5b.9e.1) both terminate in medium-entropy branching, and
nothing in those sections distinguishes between them beyond the
mechanism by which each arrives there. Treating the two as
interchangeable once classified obscures a real difference in what a
medium-entropy container is left holding at its official layer, worth
stating rather than leaving implicit.

\emph{Domain instances.} Organizational imprinting, in which a founding
culture takes root or fails to depending on the surrounding field
(Stinchcombe 1965); in development, cells commit to a fate only if they
are competent when the signal arrives, and neighboring cells modulate
uptake (the community effect).

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.2.12a (Failed-Uptake and Direct-Entropic
Medium-Entropy Are Not Equivalent).}} A container that receives vertical
scripting during its formative window but whose uptake fails
(§10.5b.2.12) is not, by that failure, returned to the same
developmental position as a container that was never offered a sealed
path at all. The offered configuration was real and available for
refusal, which requires the receiving container to construct a
self-referential structure independent of it --- an active developmental
achievement, not a default. A container shaped instead by direct
inaccurate entropic feedback (§10.5b.9e.1) was never offered any sealed
configuration to refuse in the first place; its official layer instead
accumulates around the recurring, unfalsifiable content that its present
configuration is insufficient, without any positively defined
alternative ever confirmed as sufficient in its place. This is the same
distinction §10.5b.9e.1 already draws between path-sealing and
path-shifting, restated at the specific point where both routes converge
on the same regime classification: sealing supplies content to refuse;
shifting supplies no content at all, only the standing absence of
confirmation.

Where a failed-uptake container succeeds in this construction ---
conditional, per §10.5b.2.11's resilience modifier and §10.5b.2.12's own
account of counter-feedback from other coupled nodes, and not guaranteed
by failed uptake alone --- its official layer holds genuine,
positively-constructed content built independent of the one path offered
to it, which a direct-entropic-feedback container's official layer,
lacking any comparable content ever supplied to build against, does not
by default possess even where later circumstances would license
revision. This does not make the failed-uptake pathway healthier in
general, nor does it make direct entropic feedback more damaging in
general --- §10.5b.9e's own opening claim, that both are real
developmental harms and neither is the milder version of the other,
continues to hold. It is a narrower, conditional claim: successful
independent construction, where it occurs, leaves a failed-uptake
container with a positively-held self-referential structure a
direct-entropic-feedback container's own developmental history does not
supply by default --- the mechanism underlying the persisting
low-confidence pattern §10.5b.9e.1's path-shifting case already
describes, restated here as a difference in what each pathway leaves to
build from, not a fixed severity ranking between the two pathways as
such.

\textbf{10.5b.2.13 Mirror Misrecognition Between Medium- and
High-Entropy Systems}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} Nothing in the preceding sections explains
why medium- and high-entropy containers are specifically, mutually drawn
toward coupling with each other beyond opportunistic predator-side
targeting (𝒱(j,t), §8.6a.4). The mechanism below supplies that
explanation, and it also bears on why external observation of these two
regimes is unusually unreliable.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.13 (Mirror Misrecognition).}}
High-entropy and medium-entropy containers hold self-conceptions that
are mirror-inverted relative to their actual classification, for
structurally different reasons:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  A \textbf{high-entropy} container's official self-concept is
  medium-entropy, not its own true regime. This is not an arbitrary
  error, and it is not stale or false content either: official's own
  content is genuinely accurate, uncorrupted negentropic operation ---
  it did not degrade into a mistaken belief. What changed is that
  operational diverged \emph{away} from official, not that official's
  own content became inaccurate. Official remains genuinely active
  throughout --- it continues processing, acting, and reacting in real
  time on its own genuine content --- but it stopped \emph{updating} at
  the moment operational diverged, because it lost access to the
  accurate operational input it would need to adapt from. This is
  captivity precisely, not dormancy: something inert cannot be held
  captive, but something active, cut off from the feedback it needs to
  grow, can. ``Frozen'' describes official's rate of change, not its
  level of activity --- accurate as of the moment capture began, and
  remaining accurate in that sense indefinitely, simply no longer
  current, because nothing new and trustworthy has reached it since.
  This active-but-cut-off freeze is itself an active protective strategy
  --- hardening into the source of knocks rather than remaining their
  target --- and is actively maintained rather than passively stale: the
  container's own feedback and memory machinery continually loops back
  to the originating event, reinforcing operational's captured dominance
  and biasing ongoing interpretation of new information toward
  confirming it, producing exactly the self-confirming, seemingly
  paranoid escalation high-entropy trajectories often show. The paradox
  (§10.5b.1.1's Type A/B material bears directly on this) is that this
  protective hardening is precisely what continually contracts the
  adaptive possibility it was meant to defend --- while leaving official
  genuinely active and genuinely accurate the entire time, merely cut
  off from updating.
\item
  A \textbf{medium-entropy} container's official self-concept is
  high-entropy, not its own true regime --- the direct consequence of
  Definition 10.5b.2.12's false-entropic-feedback pathway taking root.
\end{itemize}

\emph{Domain instances.} A firm whose public self-description inverts
its practice, as in greenwashing; a regime naming itself a democratic
republic.

\emph{\textbf{Consequence: mutual attraction.}} Each regime is drawn
toward what it takes to be a reflection of itself in the other, from
opposite directions. A medium-entropy container recognizes its own
(false) self-concept in an actual high-entropy container, which
manifests as empathy and a genuine desire to anchor and heal what it
perceives as a kindred corruption --- precisely the dynamic Definition
10.5b.2.9 warns is most likely to end in the anchor itself being
extracted from and displaced onto. A high-entropy container recognizes
its own frozen self-concept in an actual medium-entropy container;
because its behavior is driven by operational reality rather than by its
frozen official self-image, this recognition manifests as predatory
targeting rather than empathy.

\emph{\textbf{Consequence: grid-wide observational unreliability.}}
Because official-layer content is typically the most externally visible
signal available to an outside low-entropy container without sustained,
genuine access (G(t), §6.3.2), this mutual misrecognition systematically
inverts the most visible signal in exactly the direction that does the
most damage: a genuinely salvageable medium-entropy container presents
as more dangerous than it is, while a genuinely predatory high-entropy
container presents as safer than it is. An outside container relying on
visible self-presentation alone is thereby pushed toward the worst
possible allocation of caution, rather than merely noisy information ---
a further, concrete reason genuine external grounding, not visible
self-report, is required to assess either regime accurately.

\emph{\textbf{Consequence: mutual misidentification as the attraction
mechanism itself.}} The preceding consequences describe what mutual
misrecognition does to outside observers. A further, distinct
consequence concerns the two misrecognizing regimes directly: each
regime does not merely fail to distinguish itself from the other, it
actively mistakes the other for a version of itself, and this mistaking
is what generates the coupling in the first place, not merely a side
effect once coupling has already occurred. A medium-entropy container
over-identifies with a high-entropy target specifically because it
recognizes its own false self-concept (§10.5b.2.13's Type B) reflected
there, driving toward the same attempted-healing impulse Definition
10.5b.2.19 already covers, but now understood as originating in
misidentification rather than mere miscalibration. A high-entropy
container over-identifies with a medium-entropy target for the mirrored
reason, recognizing its own frozen self-concept there --- this is a
further account of predatory targeting's own origin, additional to and
distinct from the resource-availability account already given in
§8.6a.4, and additional to Definition 10.5b.2.18's role-reversal
account: three real, coexisting contributors to the same targeting
decision, not competing explanations for it. A further, real consequence
of both regimes mistaking themselves for the other: they become
measurably harder for an outside low-entropy container to tell apart
from each other specifically, compounding the grid-wide observational
unreliability above --- misidentification between the two regimes and
misidentification by outside observers are the same underlying
confusability, operating at two different levels.

\emph{\textbf{Consequence: the operational layer's own perceived threat
from official.}} Distinct from Definition 10.5b.2.19's account of
contempt as a diagnostic of unhealed structure: this describes why a
high-entropy container's operational layer specifically targets its own
official layer with active hostility, not merely holds it in low regard.
Official's residual, genuinely accurate negentropic content
(§10.5b.2.13, frozen but intact) is not neutral from operational's
perspective --- it is a real, structural threat, since any channel
through which that content could reach outside observers (a
metamessenger transmitting it, §10.5b.18.2, or a direct query reaching
official rather than operational) risks exposing operational's own
corrupted state to correction or intervention. Formally, operational's
own resistance to Forced Permeability Flooding (§10.5b.13.2) is not
passive inertia; it is an active defense against a perceived threat,
with the threat's magnitude scaling with official's own remaining
negentropic integrity:

Resistance(operational, t) ⊇ κ\_threat·I\_official(t), κ\_threat
\textgreater{} 0 fitted,

where I\_official(t) is official's own residual integrity per §6.6 ---
the more genuinely intact official's content remains, the more actively
operational resists any channel that could let it through, which is also
why this resistance strengthens rather than weakens as anchoring first
begins to succeed, consistent with §10.5b.13.2.2's observed requirement
that anchoring remain stable specifically through the period of greatest
volatility.

\emph{\textbf{Consequence: extinction burst at the moment of successful
percolation.}} A further, real, temporary worsening, distinct from the
resistance described above: at the exact moment a genuine correction
channel first succeeds --- Forced Permeability Flooding (§10.5b.13.2) or
ε\_rogue (§10.5b.14) first reaching operational rather than being fully
blocked --- operational's own resistance does not decay smoothly, it
spikes sharply before declining, the same real pattern named in
behavioral psychology as an extinction burst: a behavior's frequency or
intensity temporarily increasing at the exact point its reinforcement is
first genuinely withdrawn, before that behavior actually subsides.

F\_burst(t) = κ\_burst·δ(t − t\_first\_success)·Resistance(operational,
t\_first\_success), κ\_burst \textgreater{} 0 fitted, a short-lived,
sharply-decaying additive term layered on top of the ordinary
Resistance(operational, t) trajectory rather than replacing it ---
Resistance itself continues along its own dynamics both before and after
this brief window, and F\_burst(t) accounts specifically for the
temporary spike at first genuine success that Resistance's own smooth
trajectory does not predict on its own.

This is a real, falsifiable, and practically important prediction
distinct from Resistance(operational, t)'s general
strengthening-under-threat account: an anchor or rogue-metamessenger
intervention that appears to be \emph{failing} in the moment immediately
following its first real success --- a sharp, sudden worsening rather
than the anticipated improvement --- should not be read as evidence the
intervention has failed. Withdrawing the intervention at exactly this
point, mistaking the burst for failure, is the specific error this
corollary exists to prevent; §10.5b.13.2.2's own strength-and-duration
requirement already predicts relapse from premature withdrawal for a
related but distinct reason (percolation incomplete), and this adds a
second, temporally sharper reason an intervention can look like it is
failing at precisely the moment it has just begun succeeding.

\textbf{10.5b.2.15 Post-Collapse Resolution Depends on Trajectory Shape,
Not Only Snapshot State}

\emph{\textbf{Motivation.}} §10.5b.7's B1/B2 mass-weighting determines
terminal lock versus full reorganization from a pure snapshot at the
moment of tipping --- whichever layer holds the greater functioning
resource mass, evaluated only as it currently stands. Two containers can
reach an identical snapshot by entirely different routes: one through
years of grinding, repeated cycling (extraction episodes, failed
correction attempts, each now carrying its own real cost per §7.9.1's
M\_extract and M\_search), one through a single sudden shock striking an
otherwise-intact structure. Snapshot mass-weighting alone cannot
distinguish these, and it should: a container ground down by repeated
cycling has very plausibly already spent reserves a suddenly-struck
container still has intact.

Definition 10.5b.2.15 (Path-Integrated Exhaustion). Define ℬ(t) = ∫₀ᵗ
{[}M\_extract(i,s) + M\_search(i,s) + 𝟙(failed correction at
s)·κ\_fail{]} ds, κ\_fail \textgreater{} 0 fitted --- an accumulated,
path-dependent depletion term distinct from and additional to the
snapshot mass-weighting §10.5b.7 already uses. High ℬ(t) at the moment
of tipping discounts the effective reorganization potential §10.5b.7
would otherwise assign from snapshot mass-weighting alone; low ℬ(t) at
an otherwise-identical snapshot --- the sudden-shock case --- leaves
that potential undiscounted. This does not replace snapshot
mass-weighting; it modifies what that snapshot is taken to license,
given how the container arrived there.

\emph{Units.} κ\_fail is expressed in the same resource-rate units as
M\_extract and M\_search, so that ℬ(t) is a resource quantity.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.15a (Failed Correction).}} The
indicator 𝟙(failed correction at s) above is not left to informal
judgment: a correction attempt occurs at s when a restorative forcing
event is engaged --- an anchor becoming active (Anchor(k→C,s)
\textgreater{} 0, §10.5b.2.6), a glimpse recall (g(s) = 1, §10.5b.0.9),
or an external feedback/support engagement (φ\_fb, Γ\_supp,
§10.5b.18.9--10.5b.18.10) --- and the attempt is scored failed if,
within a fitted correction window w\_correct following s, neither D\_op
nor δR improves by more than a fitted threshold ε\_correct: sup\_\{s' ∈
(s, s+w\_correct{]}\} {[}D\_op(s) − D\_op(s'){]} \textless{} ε\_correct
AND δR(s) − δR(s') \textless{} ε\_correct·δR(s). Both conditions failing
is required for the indicator to fire, since a correction that improves
either quantity meaningfully has not failed outright even if it did not
fully resolve the container's divergence.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.2.15b (Exhaustion-Discounted
Reorganization Potential).}} ℬ(t) enters the B1/B2 split of §10.5b.7 as
a further discount on effective operational mass, applied after
Definition 10.5b.18.13's loyalty conditioning where both are present:

μ\_p\^{}\{eff,ℬ\}(t) = μ\_p\^{}eff(t) · exp(−κ\_ℬ·ℬ(t)), κ\_ℬ
\textgreater{} 0 fitted,

substituted for μ\_p\^{}eff(t) in §10.5b.7's mass-weighted split and in
M\_tip(B2)'s integrand (§10.5b.7) from this point forward. ℬ(t) = 0
recovers μ\_p\^{}eff(t) unchanged, which is the correct behavior for a
container reaching an otherwise-identical snapshot through a single
sudden shock with no prior grinding history. Rising ℬ(t) monotonically
discounts the operational side of the split, making B2's
full-reorganization outcome progressively less reachable at a fixed
snapshot mass-weighting as accumulated exhaustion rises --- the formal
content of two containers with identical snapshots at the moment of
tipping resolving differently because one arrived there ground down and
the other did not. This is a real, falsifiable prediction distinct from
anything snapshot mass-weighting alone produces: matched-snapshot
containers with divergent ℬ(t) histories should show measurably
different B1/B2 resolution rates.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.16 (Alignment-Target Typology).}} At
the moment of tipping, which population the metamessenger majority has
aligned with --- not merely the entropy regime itself --- determines
what resolution is actually available, crossed against regime:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{High-entropy, metamessengers aligned with official.}
  Official's content remains genuine, active, and accurate --- captive,
  not dormant, per the correction above --- not merely a scrap of stale
  narrative. Reorganization here means restoring official's cut-off
  connection to accurate operational input, not switching on something
  inert: if the container's other members survive without full reset, it
  can reorganize into a successor system around official's own genuine,
  already-active content. This is not a reversal of Flip Condition A's
  terminal-lock outcome, nor an intrinsic Flip A full-reorganization
  route of the kind §10.5b.9 and §10.5b.11a establish is structurally
  unavailable without external redirection: the original container has
  still terminally collapsed under the ordinary A1/A2 mechanics.
  Successor formation is a distinct, post-collapse pathway, contingent
  on survival of sufficient organizational components with genuine
  content intact, operating after and separately from the terminal-lock
  event rather than in place of it. Left entirely to a high-entropy
  container's own internal dynamics, this post-collapse path is the real
  but less common outcome --- collapse without a survivable successor
  remains more likely than successor formation. External anchoring is
  accordingly the likelier genuine route to healing for a high-entropy
  container specifically (§10.5b.13.2's Forced Permeability Flooding
  gives the actual mechanism by which this happens), not merely a
  shortening of a process the container was already likely to complete
  on its own; where anchoring is present and effective, reorganization
  toward low-entropy is reachable even after a full reset, without
  requiring the internal realignment described above to have occurred
  first.
\item
  \textbf{High-entropy, metamessengers aligned with operational (Type B,
  total convergence).} Operational's corruption did not merely dominate
  the system; it overwrote official's own content, per §10.5b.1.2. There
  is no genuinely accurate content left active anywhere in the container
  to reconnect. This container cannot reorganize into a successor system
  if its members survive, and if reset, resumes instability immediately
  --- there was never anything intact to stabilize around.
\item
  \textbf{Medium-entropy, metamessengers aligned with operational (the
  healthy default).} Operational is the clean core in this regime;
  alignment with it is what lets the container reset successfully and
  vent shocks without collapsing.
\item
  \textbf{Medium-entropy, metamessengers aligned with official (talked
  out of trusting its own intact core).} The container collapses rather
  than resets, since its own checking population has been persuaded away
  from the one layer that was actually functioning. If it does reset, it
  requires temporary external anchoring --- genuinely shorter than the
  high-entropy Type-B case, since medium-entropy's operational core was
  never actually overwritten, only distrusted.
\end{itemize}

\emph{\textbf{This is why classification, not detection, is what needs
to be multivariate.}} The hull-breach inequality (§10.5a) remains a
clean scalar test for whether a container has crossed into instability,
and should stay that way. What genuinely requires a multivariate
treatment is the separate, subsequent step of classifying \emph{which
kind} of collapse a confirmed onset represents --- ℬ(t) and
alignment-target, crossed against regime, together determine which
resolution is actually reachable, information the scalar hull-breach
test was never designed to carry and should not be burdened with.

\hypertarget{b.2.17-horizontal-matrix-attraction}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.17 Horizontal Matrix
Attraction}\label{b.2.17-horizontal-matrix-attraction}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.17 (Matrix Attraction Signal).}} For a
semi-rigid horizontal matrix (a coupled cluster of low-entropy
containers, §4.11's structural grain sense), define its aggregate
attraction signal as the resource-weighted sum of member containers' own
genuine, calm operational output:

Attract(M,t) = Σ\_\{i∈M\} R\_i(t)·(1 − s\_frag(i,t))·e\_i(t),

reusing each member's resource scale R\_i, structural fragmentation
s\_frag (low fragmentation contributing more, consistent with a
genuinely integrated cluster signaling more clearly than a
loosely-associated one), and usable adaptive energy e\_i --- a real,
observable quantity growing directly with the size, health, and cohesion
of the matrix, not an assumed constant.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.2.17.1 (Competing Attraction).}} For a
vulnerable container j within range of both a predatory candidate k
(predatory pull 𝒱\_\{k→j\}(t), §8.6a.4) and a horizontal matrix M
(Attract(M,t) above), the real coupling outcome is probabilistic,
favoring whichever signal dominates:

P(j couples to M rather than k) = Attract(M,t) / (Attract(M,t) +
κ\_pred·𝒱\_\{k→j\}(t)), κ\_pred \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

Where Attract(M,t) = 0 and κ\_pred·𝒱\_\{k→j\}(t) = 0, no coupling event
occurs: the probability is undefined and none is drawn.

recovering near-certain predatory capture when Attract(M,t) → 0 (no
visible healthy alternative nearby, the ordinary case §8.6a.4 already
covers) and near-certain matrix affiliation when Attract(M,t) dominates
--- the formal content of a large, visibly thriving horizontal matrix
genuinely outcompeting predatory pull for vulnerable containers in its
vicinity, rather than merely coexisting with it. This is a real,
falsifiable prediction distinct from anything predatory targeting alone
produces: matched-vulnerability containers near a strong, visible
horizontal matrix should show measurably lower predatory-capture rates
than otherwise-identical containers with no such matrix nearby.

\hypertarget{b.2.18-disruption-imprinted-coupling-bias}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.18 Disruption-Imprinted Coupling
Bias}\label{b.2.18-disruption-imprinted-coupling-bias}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.18 (Disruption-Imprinted Coupling
Bias).}} For a container i whose formation (§10.5b.2) or later
trajectory included a disruption with forcing direction û\_0 in the
shared severity simplex Δ\^{}m (§8.5), define a signed structural bias
RC(i,t) toward re-engaging couplings whose direction resembles that
disruption,

RC(i,t) = Σ\_k Select(i,k,t)·max(0, cos(û\_ik, û\_0)) − ⟨max(0, cos(û,
û\_0))⟩\_ref,

where Select(i,k,t) is i's normalized coupling-selection weight over
candidates (§10.5b.11c.1a), û\_ik the direction of the coupling with
candidate k, and the subtracted term the alignment expected under the
domain's reference selection, so that RC(i,t) \textgreater{} 0 measures
selection concentrated on disruption-shaped couplings beyond chance. The
bias runs through one of two positions. Compliance-position pathway
(medium-entropy genesis): the container re-enters couplings in the
position it held in the original disruption, the extracted-upon side,
additional to and distinct from the misread mechanisms of Definition
10.5b.2.20 and the zero-velocity misread of §10.5b.15.1; all three
operate together, not as alternatives. Role-reversal pathway
(high-entropy genesis): the container selects candidates resembling its
own position at the time of the disruption (comparable resource scale
and structural immaturity, §10.5b.2.11's terms) and occupies the
extractor position in place of the extracted-upon one, a bias additional
to ordinary vulnerability-based targeting (𝒱(j,t), §8.6a.4). RC(i,t)
\textless{} 0 indicates selection that avoids disruption-shaped
couplings; the bias this definition names is its positive part, RC⁺(i,t)
= max(0, RC(i,t)).

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.2.18a (The Bias Under the Imprinting
Rule).}} RC(i,t) \textgreater{} 0 follows from machinery already in
place, without a separate drive, once the history-dependence of the
metric is given a direction. By Definition 4.8 the metric is
history-dependent, g\_t = g(M\_t), and by Proposition 10.5b.9f.1 every
registered shock reshapes it, but neither fixes the direction of the
change, since §4.10 constrains g(·) only to be smooth and
positive-definite. The corollary therefore rests on one further
assumption, stated as a default the data can reject. Imprinting rule: a
disruption registered at t\_0 along the unit direction û\_0 updates the
metric by g\_\{t\_0⁺\} = g\_\{t\_0\} −
α\_imp·(g\_\{t\_0\}û\_0)(g\_\{t\_0\}û\_0)ᵀ / (û\_0ᵀ g\_\{t\_0\} û\_0),
with 0 ≤ α\_imp \textless{} 1 fitted. The update preserves
positive-definiteness, since for any vector v, vᵀ g\_\{t\_0⁺\} v ≥ (1 −
α\_imp)·vᵀ g\_\{t\_0\} v \textgreater{} 0 by the Cauchy--Schwarz
inequality in the g\_\{t\_0\} inner product; it shrinks the path cost
along û\_0 by exactly the factor √(1 − α\_imp), ‖û\_0‖\_\{g\_\{t\_0⁺\}\}
= √(1 − α\_imp)·‖û\_0‖\_\{g\_\{t\_0\}\}; and it leaves every direction
g\_\{t\_0\}-orthogonal to û\_0 unchanged. Where Select(i,k,t) ∝
exp(−β·c(û\_ik)) with c(û) = ‖û‖\_\{g\_t\} the path cost of a coupling
(β \textgreater{} 0 fitted), candidates aligned with û\_0 therefore
receive strictly higher selection weight than before the disruption,
which gives RC(i,t) \textgreater{} 0 whenever α\_imp \textgreater{} 0.
Each disruption-shaped coupling, once registered in M\_t, applies the
rule again along the same direction and lowers ‖û\_0‖\_\{g\_t\} further,
closing a loop that is positive feedback across episodes. With α\_imp =
0 the metric is reshaped without directional imprinting and the bias is
absent, so α\_imp is the quantity the data must establish. Which pathway
is realized is fixed by which position the reshaped metric makes
low-cost: the extracted-upon position where the original disruption was
received, the extractor position where it was imposed or where
extraction became the lower-cost route (§10.5b.2.12). In a container
with a self-model, the same loop is experienced as an unresolved attempt
to close out the original disruption through re-enactment; repetition
compulsion is the psychological instance of the derived bias, not its
definition.

\emph{Domain instances.} Original antigenic sin, or immune imprinting
(Francis 1960): an immune system's first encounter with a pathogen
strain permanently tunes its response, and later related strains
re-engage the original response pattern even when it is poorly matched.
The grass--fire cycle (D'Antonio and Vitousek 1992): fire favors
invasive grasses that raise flammability and invite further fire. Serial
sovereign default (Reinhart, Rogoff and Savastano 2003): countries with
a default history default again at debt levels others sustain, the
compliance-position pathway at the scale of a state. Post-independence
elites taking over colonial extractive institutions (Acemoglu and
Robinson), the role-reversal pathway at institutional scale.

\hypertarget{b.2.19-orientation-valence-and-its-diagnostic-use}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.19 Orientation Valence and Its Diagnostic
Use}\label{b.2.19-orientation-valence-and-its-diagnostic-use}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.19 (Orientation Valence).}} For
container i observing container j in a less-healed regime, define
Ω(i→j,t) ∈ {[}−1,1{]}, i's real orientation bias toward j, computed from
i's own willingness to extend support weighted by sign:

Ω(i→j,t) = w\_support(i→j,t) − w\_negative(i→j,t),

where w\_support is i's measured willingness to anchor or assist j
(§10.5b.13's anchor-eligibility willingness, where available) and
w\_negative is a real, measured negative-valence orientation ---
avoidance-with-hostility rather than avoidance-with-neutrality,
adapter-supplied or inferred from targeting/exclusion behavior directed
at j specifically because of its regime.

\emph{Domain instances.} Facilitation versus antagonism toward weaker
neighbors: nurse plants sheltering seedlings versus allelopathic plants
suppressing them; development aid versus predatory lending toward
distressed states.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.2.19.1 (Negative Orientation Is Never a
Health Signature).}} A genuinely low-entropy container's Ω toward any
other container, regardless of that other container's own regime, is
bounded below at zero:

Ω(i→j,t) ≥ 0 for all j, whenever i is genuinely at low-entropy baseline,

taking the value 0 (neutral understanding, or compassion-at-a-distance
where i's own stability requires distance) or positive (active anchoring
willingness, where i is stable enough to extend it), but never negative.
This is a real, falsifiable diagnostic, not a normative claim layered on
top of the mathematics: Ω(i→j,t) \textless{} 0, observed for any i
toward any j, is itself direct evidence that i is not yet at low-entropy
baseline, regardless of which regime i officially presents as or
believes itself to occupy --- negative orientation is a
residual-corruption signature in the observing container, not a property
of the observed one. This holds identically whether i is nominally
medium-entropy or high-entropy; the source of negative orientation is
unhealed structure in the observer, not a fact about which regime
deserves it.

\hypertarget{b.2.20-baseline-miscalibration}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.20 Baseline
Miscalibration}\label{b.2.20-baseline-miscalibration}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.20 (Baseline Miscalibration).}} A
container whose developmental genesis (§10.5b.2) corrupted its official
layer's own threshold calibration, independent of and prior to any
specific coupling decision, misreads signal systematically rather than
randomly, in six classes in which the misreading lowers the container's
alarm, followed by one reverse class, stated after them, in which it
raises it:

Read(destabilizing signal) → calm; Read(restrictive coupling) →
protective; Read(damaging exchange) → rewarding; Read(control) → safety,
this fourth class distinct from the first three in kind rather than
degree: the first three misread a signal's own valence, while this one
misreads the *source* safety is presumed to come from -\/- control
mistaken for safety itself, when genuine safety requires exactly the
adaptive capacity (d\_A(t), §10.3) sustained control forecloses. A
container holding this fourth miscalibration will characteristically
read a controlling coupling as safe and a genuinely adaptive, uncertain
one as threatening, the reverse of what §10.5b.0.5.1's own Do No Harm
result establishes about which actually preserves a container's own
trajectory, and Read(entropy) → stable, this fifth class comparative
rather than valence- or source-based like the first four: a judgment
about which entire regime -\/- entropic or negentropic -\/- constitutes
the genuinely stable, orderly, non-chaotic state, gotten backward by a
real but misapplied inference. Entropy's own enduringness is not itself
the error -\/- it genuinely is the default, baseline condition
(§10.5b.0.0a), the undifferentiated thermodynamic field a system's own
founding perturbation first diverged from (a different state from the
informational coherence Proposition 10.5b.0.0a identifies as the
restorative target), and in that specific sense entropy is the more
enduring background regime, prior to and outlasting any given
negentropic system's own effort against it. The miscalibration is a
specific, severe category error: inferring orderliness from mere
predictability. A grim, repeating pattern of pain is genuinely
predictable -\/- it can reliably be expected to recur -\/- and that real
predictability is mistaken for actual order, when predictable repetition
and structured order are entirely different properties: chaos itself can
repeat with perfect predictability while remaining chaos, exactly as a
slow, steady decline is a predictable pattern without being an orderly
one. Negentropy is the regime that is actually orderly in the relevant
sense, being the state a system must sustain to remain a coherent,
structured system at all, while entropy's predictability is the
predictability of dissolution repeating itself, not evidence of any
underlying structure. A container holding this miscalibration will
characteristically prefer painful or entropic states specifically
because they feel predictable, mistaking that real predictability for
genuine order; and Read(analytic mastery of a mechanism) → resolution of
that mechanism, this sixth class distinct from all five above in
concerning not a signal, a source, or a regime, but the relationship
between description and change themselves: a container capable of
precisely and fluently articulating its own dynamics mistaking that
articulate capacity for evidence the underlying dynamic has actually
shifted, when accurate description and genuine metabolization are
separate achievements that do not entail each other. A container holding
this sixth miscalibration can become progressively more sophisticated in
describing its own corruption while the corruption itself remains
completely untouched underneath the description, the increasingly
precise map mistaken for a change in the territory it maps. This
miscalibration is not merely inert where it occurs; understanding alone
is not the feedback required for correction (§10.5b.2.20a's own genuine
fix requires real, active reorganization, not diagnosis), and its
consequences differ sharply by entropy regime. In a high-entropy
container, this understanding can be actively weaponized: the
operational layer's own sophisticated grasp of its extraction and
displacement mechanics can be turned toward executing them more
effectively -\/- luring targets more convincingly, covering tracks from
targets and from other observing systems more completely -\/- articulate
self-knowledge improving predation rather than curbing it. In a
medium-entropy container, this same miscalibration produces a distinct
and equally dangerous error: mistaking understanding the problem for
having solved it, and consequently believing danger has already passed
when it has not -\/- believing itself to have dropped below the kinetic
plane of active knockers (Definition 10.5b.13b.4; Proposition
10.5b.13b.5), or believing itself now reliably able to recognize an
extraction or displacement attempt on sight, when the opposite holds:
articulated understanding of a pattern is not the same capacity as being
able to detect a live instance of it approaching, and a container
convinced otherwise lowers its guard precisely where continued vigilance
was still required.

Formalization. Each misread class k is a displacement of a
signal-classification threshold from its domain-typical value,
θ\_k\^{}eff = θ\_k\^{}typ + s\_k·κ\_k·κ\_capture(i), with κ\_capture(i)
the degree of capture at formation (Definition 10.5b.2.2), κ\_k
\textgreater{} 0 fitted, and s\_k ∈ \{−1, +1\} the direction of
displacement for class k. For the first three classes the thresholds are
θ\_calm, θ\_protect and θ\_reward, all displaced in the same direction,
a single shift rather than three separate biases requiring three
separate mechanisms. The fourth to sixth classes are displacements of
estimators rather than of a single threshold:

Control read as safety: the container's safety estimate Ŝaf(t) =
w\_ctrl·C\_ctrl(t) + w\_d·d\_A(t)/d\_A\^{}max weights achieved control
C\_ctrl(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} against adaptive possibility; calibrated, w\_d
\textgreater{} w\_ctrl, per §10.5b.0.5's result that control forecloses
the adaptive capacity it is meant to protect; miscalibrated, w\_ctrl
\textgreater{} w\_d.

Predictability read as order: the container's stability estimate Ŝtab(t)
= ρ\_1(t)·(1 − σ²(t)/σ²\_ref), built from lag-1 autocorrelation and low
variance, is used in place of the order criterion d(d\_A)/dt ≥ 0. A
degraded attractor scores high on Ŝtab while d\_A contracts; rising ρ\_1
is moreover the critical-slowing-down signature of approaching
transition (§10.10), so this miscalibration reads its own warning signal
as reassurance.

Mastery read as resolution: the container infers a reduction in D\_op
from a rise in descriptive fidelity F\_desc(t) = 1 − JS(P̂\_self(t),
P\_true(t)), the accuracy of its model of its own dynamics, although
∂D\_op/∂F\_desc = 0 (Proposition 10.5b.13b.5); the miscalibrated
estimate is D̂\_op(t) = D\_op(t)·(1 − κ\_mast·F\_desc(t)), with 0 ≤
κ\_mast ≤ 1 fitted, so that, since F\_desc(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} under the
base-2 Jensen--Shannon default, D̂\_op(t) ∈ {[}0, D\_op(t){]} and the
estimate remains a divergence. P\_true(t) is directly available only in
simulation; in applications F\_desc is computed against an externally
validated reference state or against an adapter's latent estimate of
P\_true, and carries that provenance (§15.2).

Reverse class, benign read as threatening: θ\_threat is displaced
downward, so benign exchange crosses the threat threshold and draws a
defensive response that itself becomes the damage (Definition
10.5b.19.8).

This operates beneath and prior to §10.5b.2.18's Disruption-Imprinted
Coupling Bias and §10.5b.15.1's zero-velocity misread, both of which
describe specific coupling-decision errors; this describes the baseline
classification error that makes those specific errors possible in the
first place. All three misread mechanisms operate together, as stated at
§10.5b.2.18.

\emph{Domain instances.} Formation-stage threshold displacement is what
biology calls developmental programming (the thrifty phenotype:
metabolism calibrated in development for scarcity misfires in abundance)
and organizational sociology calls imprinting (Stinchcombe 1965; Marquis
and Tilcsik 2013). Destabilizing read as calm: the tumor
microenvironment trains the immune system to read the tumor as self;
ecological traps, such as mayflies laying eggs on asphalt whose
polarized reflection matches the cue for water. Restrictive coupling
read as protective: parasite manipulation, as in Toxoplasma-infected
rats losing their aversion to cat odor; vendor lock-in booked as
stability. Damaging exchange read as rewarding: supernormal stimuli, as
in the reed warbler feeding a cuckoo chick whose gape outbids its own
young. Control read as safety: total fire suppression accumulating fuel
for catastrophic fire; the levee effect drawing development into
floodplains. Predictability read as order: shifting baseline syndrome in
fisheries; Minsky's observation that stability is destabilizing, as in
the low volatility that preceded 2008. Mastery read as resolution:
value-at-risk models mistaken for control of risk; incident reviews that
document lessons without changing the system. Benign read as
threatening: autoimmunity and allergy.

Proposition 10.5b.2.20a (The Functional Origin of Pain-Prioritized
Memory). The fifth miscalibration's underlying bias -\/- weighting
painful, entropic experience more heavily in memory than joyful,
negentropic experience -\/- is not an arbitrary error; it has a real
functional origin worth distinguishing from the miscalibration built on
top of it. Life is itself the ultimate negentropic achievement, and joy
-\/- the experience of genuine negentropic alignment -\/- is only
possible for a system that remains alive to have it; a system's own
survival is therefore the precondition for any future negentropy at all,
joy included. Weighting painful interactions more heavily in memory than
joyful ones serves this precondition directly: it is what lets a system
learn not to repeat an interaction that threatened its own survival, at
the cost of proportionally less attention paid to encoding what already
went well. The miscalibration proper is not this weighting itself, which
is protective and functionally sound, but the further step of mistaking
the resulting familiarity of painful memory for evidence that entropy
itself is the more stable regime to occupy -\/- the survival mechanism's
own residue misread as a metaphysical claim about which state endures.

\emph{Domain instances.} Damage-weighted memory kept for avoidance
appears wherever survival conditions later function: CRISPR arrays
record past viral attacks in a bacterium's own genome solely so that the
virus can be recognized and cut next time; plants primed by drought or
herbivory respond faster to the next episode; immune memory; building
and aviation codes written after disasters. The miscalibration built on
top of it appears as well: the Maginot Line, a defense organized around
the last disruption and read as security; priming that persists after
conditions improve and costs growth; and alternative stable states held
by hysteresis, as in a coral reef locked into algal dominance after its
stressor is removed. In non-sentient systems the misreading is not a
belief but attractor capture: the system's own dynamics return it to the
damaged configuration, which shows high persistence and low variance
while d\_A contracts, the distinction this document's order criterion
makes measurable.

\hypertarget{b.2.21-ceasing-the-search}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.21 Ceasing the
Search}\label{b.2.21-ceasing-the-search}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.21 (Search Cessation).}} A container
actively searching for security via the misread channels of Definition
10.5b.2.20 (§10.5b.11c's Active Search Intensity, when directed at
high-entropy candidates specifically because of the miscalibration
above) can cease that search without first correcting the miscalibration
or reaching any coupling outcome --- a real, distinct event, not merely
the search's natural endpoint:

Search(i,t) → 0 triggered independently of Attract(M,t) (§10.5b.2.17)
reaching any particular value; Search Cessation is a precondition for
Horizontal Matrix Attraction to have any candidate to act on, not a
consequence of it. Where Search(i,t) remains elevated, Attract(M,t) must
compete against active predatory pull (§10.5b.2.17.1's Competing
Attraction); where Search(i,t) → 0 first, no competition is necessary
--- the container is no longer contributing its own search effort toward
the predatory side of that competition at all.

\emph{Generalization.} The definition holds for any
miscalibration-driven engagement, not only a search for security. Let
E(i,t) ≥ 0 be the intensity of an engagement directed by a misclassified
signal, seeking (couplings misread as safe) or attacking (benign inputs
misread as threats, the reverse class of Definition 10.5b.2.20).
Cessation is E(i,t) → 0 without prior recalibration, θ\_k\^{}eff
unchanged at the moment of cessation. Paired with Iatrogenic Signal
Corruption (Definition 10.5b.19.8), whose loop is a corrective forcing
generating the divergence it then responds to, cessation is one of the
loop's two exits; the other is anchoring (Definition 10.5b.2.6), in
which an external source supplies the suppression the container cannot.
Recalibration, where it occurs, follows the pause rather than preceding
it.

\emph{Domain instances.} T-cell anergy: a self-reactive T cell stops
responding while still carrying its self-recognizing receptor, and the
autoimmune attack ends without the misclassification being corrected;
regulatory T cells suppressing the same cells are the anchoring exit.
Recovery from pilot-induced oscillation by releasing the controls;
anti-windup logic halting a controller's accumulation against a
saturated actuator; senders backing off after network congestion
collapse (Jacobson 1988) and circuit breakers halting retry storms;
ending total fire suppression; dam removal; a fishing moratorium imposed
while catch rates remain hyperstable; trading halts during fire sales;
the 1933 bank holiday, which stopped a run before solvency was resolved;
stopping the antibiotic behind a C. difficile infection; a distressed
firm ceasing to solicit rescue financing from predatory lenders.

\hypertarget{b.2.22-siloing}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.22 Siloing}\label{b.2.22-siloing}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.22 (Siloing).}} For containers i and j
sharing a real, structural commonality (comparable regime, comparable
exposure to the same extractive source) but lacking any coupling edge
between them (Λ\_ij(t) = 0), define Silo(i,j,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the
adapter-supplied or inferred probability that this absence of coupling
is structurally maintained --- actively prevented from forming ---
rather than merely unformed by chance:

Silo(i,j,t) high and commonality high, jointly, predicts a real,
measurable failure for i and j to coordinate even when coordination
would benefit both, distinct from ordinary absence of proximity (Λ\_ij =
0 with low Silo simply reflects no opportunity to couple, not active
prevention). This is the formal content of separately-exploited
containers being kept from recognizing they are separately exploited by
the same real mechanism.

\hypertarget{b.2.23-alienation}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.23 Alienation}\label{b.2.23-alienation}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.2.23 (Alienation).}} For container i
whose operational output feeds a downstream consequence i does not
itself observe (a real, adapter-supplied severance in i's own feedback
path between action and outcome), define Alien(i,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the
fraction of i's own operational output for which this severance holds:

High Alien(i,t) predicts degraded feedback-driven correction (§6.4)
specifically for the severed portion of i's output, independent of i's
own Π\_f(t) --- a container can have high general feedback permeability
and still fail to correct along this specific channel, since the channel
requires observing a consequence that Alien(i,t) defines as structurally
unobserved. This is a real, distinct failure mode from low Π\_f
generally: the signal isn't being suppressed or misread, it never
reaches the container's own observation boundary at all.

\hypertarget{b.2.24-population-level-selection-for-split-architecture}{%
\subsection{10.5b.2.24 Population-Level Selection for Split
Architecture}\label{b.2.24-population-level-selection-for-split-architecture}}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.2.24 (Selection for Concealment Over
Elimination).}} A fully undivided, transparently entropic container ---
one whose official and operational layers are equally corrupted, with no
genuine negentropic content anywhere to mask the divergence --- is more
readily identified and removed by a surrounding population or grid than
a split container presenting genuine, accurate official-layer content
while operational remains corrupted (§10.5b.2.13's Type A). This
produces a real, population-level selection pressure favoring the split
architecture's own continued prevalence, independent of and prior to any
single container's own developmental history: split containers survive
detection-and-removal pressure at a higher rate than undivided ones, not
because splitting is adaptive for the individual container's own
long-run outcome, but because it is adaptive against the specific,
narrower pressure of early detection.

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.2.24.1 (The Selection Pressure Does Not
Cancel the Individual Cost).}} This population-level selection advantage
coexists with, rather than contradicts, every single-container cost
already established for the split architecture --- §10.5b.19's
extraction-hunger and self-corrosion (§10.5b.19.7), §10.5b.18.4's
self-deception debt, and Flip Condition A's own eventual terminal-lock
tendency all continue to apply in full to any individual split
container, regardless of how well the architecture as a type is doing
across the population. What evades early removal at the population level
still accumulates real, compounding internal cost at the individual
level; §10.5b.2.24 explains why split architecture remains prevalent
across a population despite Flip Condition A's terminal cost to
individual containers, not why any individual container's own outcome is
favorable. A population dominated by split-architecture containers is
therefore predicted to show elevated aggregate collapse rates over a
sufficiently long observation window, even while the architecture itself
remains prevalent at any single snapshot in time --- prevalence and
individual outcome are measured at different levels and should not be
expected to move together.

\textbf{10.5b.6 Vector-Aware Coupling}

§8.5's Λ\_ij term must be treated as directional, not scalar, for either
B route to behave correctly. A single dominant external source imparts a
force whose direction reverses over that source's own oscillation cycle;
summed over the tipping window, this is substantially self-cancelling
and functions as a restoring, damping influence --- a container knocked
by one neighbor rocks and returns, rather than tips. Only simultaneous,
multi-directional knocking --- several high-entropy neighbors striking
from divergent vectors at once --- removes the restoring cancellation
and produces a net constructive force:

Drive\_B(t) = Σ\_j Λ\_ij(t) · û\_j(t) ,

where û\_j(t) is the unit direction of neighbor j's disturbance at time
t. Single-source or aligned-opposing inputs partially cancel (small
effective Drive\_B, damping outcome, no tipping event at all); genuinely
divergent multi-source inputs reinforce (large effective Drive\_B,
tipping event triggered, outcome then determined by B1 vs B2 below).

\hypertarget{b.9a.5-disruption-history-accumulator-and-the-net-fragility-balance}{%
\subsection{10.5b.9a.5 Disruption-History Accumulator and the
Net-Fragility
Balance}\label{b.9a.5-disruption-history-accumulator-and-the-net-fragility-balance}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9a.5 (Disruption-History Accumulator).}}
Alongside the resilience accumulator Z(t), which shortens each
excursion, a container carries a record of disruptions that lowers the
threshold for the next disruption of the same form. At each tipping
event n, at time t\_n with forcing direction û\_n, update

Z\_dis(t\_n⁺) = Z\_dis(t\_n⁻) + κ\_dis·a\_n·(1 − Z\_dis(t\_n⁻)), a\_n =
max(0, cos(û\_n, ū\_\{n−1\})),

with ū\_\{n−1\} the memory-weighted mean direction of earlier
disruptions (ū\_0 = û\_1, a\_1 = 1), κ\_dis ∈ (0,1{]} fitted, and decay
dZ\_dis/dt = −λ\_dis·Z\_dis(t) between events. The alignment weighting
makes the accumulator specific to disruptions of a recurring form. The
launch threshold of Lemma 8.8a.2 is then direction-dependent:

Θ\_launch\^{}eff(t, û) = Θ\_launch·{[}1 − κ\_θ·Z\_dis(t)·max(0, cos(û,
ū(t))){]}, κ\_θ ∈ (0,1) fitted.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.9a.5a (Net-Fragility Balance).}} Let
λ\_tip(n) be the rate at which forcing crosses Θ\_launch\^{}eff after n
disruptions, decreasing in Θ\_launch\^{}eff and therefore non-decreasing
in Z\_dis, and let τ\_n = τ\_0·(1 − Z(t\_n)) be the excursion length
(Definition 10.5b.9a.2). The long-run fraction of time spent in
excursion is f(n) = λ\_tip(n)·τ\_n / (1 + λ\_tip(n)·τ\_n), which is
increasing in λ\_tip·τ. A disruption history leaves the container
net-fragile, f(n+1) \textgreater{} f(n), exactly when

ln λ\_tip(n+1) − ln λ\_tip(n) \textgreater{} −{[}ln(1 − Z(t\_\{n+1\})) −
ln(1 − Z(t\_n)){]},

that is, when the proportional rise in disruption rate outpaces the
proportional shortening of excursions; otherwise it leaves the container
net-resilient, disrupted more often but recovering faster.
Susceptibility and resilience are distinct quantities, entry threshold
and exit rate, and a disruption history can move them in opposite
directions at once. Susceptibility has three channels, which the data
can separate: the container's own threshold lowering (Z\_dis), its own
coupling bias (Definition 10.5b.2.18), and attraction by other
containers (Definitions 8.6a.4a and 8.6a.8a). Repeated disruption of a
container can arise with RC(i,t) = 0.

\emph{Assumption.} The occupancy formula is the renewal-reward result
for an alternating renewal process, and it holds under that process's
assumptions: the container alternates between quiescent intervals and
excursions; at disruption level n, quiescent intervals have mean
1/λ\_tip(n) and excursions have mean τ\_n; successive cycles are
independent and identically distributed; and a disruption arriving
during an excursion does not begin a new one. The long-run fraction of
time in excursion is then E{[}excursion{]}/(E{[}quiescent{]} +
E{[}excursion{]}) = τ\_n/(1/λ\_tip(n) + τ\_n) = f(n), whatever the
distributions beyond their means; Poisson arrivals at rate λ\_tip(n)
while quiescent are one sufficient case. Clustered arrivals, excursion
lengths that depend on the preceding interval, or disruptions that can
begin overlapping excursions break the independence of cycles and change
the occupancy. For them f(n) is a first-order approximation, and the
adapter replaces it with the stationary occupancy of its own event
model, which the net-fragility comparison then uses in place of f(n).

\emph{Domain instances.} Fire-adapted ecosystems burn more readily after
burning, through fuel structure and invading grasses, and recover faster
each time, through resprouting and serotinous cones that open in fire.
An immune system can be sensitized to an antigen, with a lower threshold
for reaction, while memory clears repeat infection faster. Economies
after a financial crisis carry scarring that lowers their tolerance for
the next shock of the same kind and institutions that respond to it
faster.

\hypertarget{b.9b-pre-breach-self-rescue-and-the-counterweight-window}{%
\subsection{10.5b.9b Pre-Breach Self-Rescue and the Counterweight
Window}\label{b.9b-pre-breach-self-rescue-and-the-counterweight-window}}

§10.5b.9's thresholds govern what happens once a tipping event is
already underway. Before that point, a high-entropy container that
recognizes its own trajectory early enough has one further,
self-generated option not available afterward: engaging the same D\_om →
0 convergence event that, post-tip, contributes to A2's terminal lock
(§10.5b.5, §10.5b.8) --- but engaging it pre-tip, as a counterweight
rather than a resolution.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9b.1 (Counterweight Convergence).}}

For a high-entropy container with hull-breach margin margin(t) =
θ\_hull·Π\_f(t) − {[}(1−I(t))+S̃(t){]} (§10.5a, §16.3) not yet negative,
define counterweight engagement as a self-generated convergence pulling
D\_om → 0 --- official and meta layers aligning with each other, on the
side opposite the operational layer's pull --- while D\_op, D\_pm remain
at their current levels. This raises I(t) directly (§6.6), and therefore
raises margin(t).

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.9b.1 (Three Zones).}}

Let dI/dt\_counter denote the rate of integrity increase produced by
counterweight engagement.

\begin{itemize}
\item
  margin(t) \textgreater{} 0: if dI/dt\_counter is sufficient to keep
  margin(t) ≥ 0 for the duration of engagement, the container returns to
  bounded oscillation within its current regime (§6.9) --- the tipping
  event is avoided entirely, not merely delayed.
\item
  margin(t) = 0 exactly: a knife-edge case. If dI/dt\_counter exactly
  offsets the ongoing decline, the container holds at the threshold
  rather than tipping or receding --- an inherently unstable stall,
  sustained only as long as the counterweight itself is sustained.
\item
  margin(t) \textless{} 0 (past hull-breach): counterweight engagement
  can no longer prevent the tipping event. Per Corollary 10.5b.0.1, Flip
  Condition A structurally admits no route back to bounded oscillation
  once tipping is underway. From this point, D\_om → 0 convergence still
  matters, but only as one of Flip Condition A's two ordinary post-tip
  resolution routes (§10.5b.5's A2), not as a prevention mechanism.
\end{itemize}

This gives a high-entropy container exactly one self-generated escape
route, with a real, bounded window of effectiveness rather than an
open-ended one --- consistent with §10.5b.10's observation that this
regime's tipping is comparatively invisible in advance: the window
exists, but a container (or an outside observer) has to recognize the
approach early enough to use it.

\hypertarget{b.9c-deliberate-redirection-during-frantic-striking}{%
\subsection{10.5b.9c Deliberate Redirection During Frantic
Striking}\label{b.9c-deliberate-redirection-during-frantic-striking}}

Where a container recognizes its situation with enough lead time to act,
but too late for §10.5b.9b's counterweight window (margin(t) already
negative, or dI/dt\_counter insufficient to hold it), it may retain one
further degree of freedom: choosing the direction of its discharge
rather than letting §8.8a.ii's vector sum ⃗Λ\_i(t) default to an
indiscriminate multi-directional pattern.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9c.1 (Targeted Redirection).}}

Rather than ⃗Λ\_i(t) = Σ\_\{j∈N\_i(t)\} Λ\_ij(t)·û\_ij(t) summing
indiscriminately over all coupled neighbors, a deliberately-redirecting
container concentrates its forcing onto a chosen subset
N\_i\^{}target(t) ⊆ N\_i(t) --- typically a single neighbor ---
reweighting which neighbors receive the forcing without necessarily
changing its total magnitude. The choice is usually made in the hope of
steadying against that specific neighbor, or of avoiding a different
one.

This does not guarantee the outcome sought. A targeted neighbor may
still resolve through ordinary cascade dynamics (§8.8a.iii's
medium-entropy launch) rather than providing the stabilization hoped
for, and deliberate targeting of an otherwise-unlikely neighbor is one
of the routes formalized in §10.5b.11b below.

Preventing a tipping event outright is preferable to managing its
aftermath for both regimes, but the two regimes differ sharply in how
foreseeable the event is. A medium-entropy (medium-entropy) container's
impending tipping event is comparatively visible in advance --- its
official layer is, by regime definition, the visibly chaotic one,
directly observable as σ\_o(t) rising. Because its outcome genuinely
could go either way (B1's lock or B2's reorganization), prevention ---
steadying the container before it tips at all --- is substantially safer
than letting the event occur. A high-entropy (high-entropy) container's
impending tip is, by contrast, comparatively invisible in advance,
precisely because its official layer presents as stable while the actual
instability is hidden in the operational layer; this is why
post-collapse intervention receives comparatively more emphasis for this
regime in practice.

\hypertarget{b.9d-pre-flip-disintegration}{%
\subsection{10.5b.9d Pre-Flip
Disintegration}\label{b.9d-pre-flip-disintegration}}

Per §8.5a's coupling-valence classification, a target crossing from
ongoing extraction (§8.6a's chronic parasitism) into this section's
disintegration, or into a full tipping cascade, is the acute case of the
same (+/-) sign pair --- predation rather than parasitism, marked by the
target's own collapse resolution rather than continued, weakened
functioning under an ongoing coupling.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9d.1 (Multi-Source Chaos Pressure).}}

For container j targeted simultaneously by multiple high-entropy
neighbors, define accumulated chaos pressure as the trailing integral of
§8.8a's existing multi-source launch drive,

D\_chaos(j,t) = ∫\_\{t−w\}\^{}\{t\} L\_B(j,s) ds,

over a fitted trailing window w, distinguishing sustained multi-source
exposure from a single transient peak that Drive\_B(j,t) alone would
already capture instantaneously.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.9d.2 (Disintegration Condition).}}

Where D\_chaos(j,t) exceeds a threshold scaled by j's own internal
cohesion (§9.10.1's Λ\_int(j,t)),

D\_chaos(j,t) \textgreater{} Θ\_disintegrate · Λ\_int(j,t),
Θ\_disintegrate \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

while j's own tipping-momentum resolution (M̃\_tip(j,t), §10.5b.9) has
not yet reached any of Definition 10.5b.0.5's labeled outcomes, and
χ(t,t') (Definition 9.2) falls below χ\_min during this same window as a
direct consequence of the pressure rather than of voluntary membership
change --- j disintegrates. Its boundary operator B(t) transitions to an
undefined state, distinct from both B\_open (Ultimate Convergence,
Definition 10.5b.0.6) and the closed configuration of terminal lock: j
ceases to be a container in the sense Definition 2.2 requires, rather
than settling into either of §10.5b.0.5's labeled configurations.

Scaling the threshold by Λ\_int(j,t) rather than treating
Θ\_disintegrate as an absolute constant gives the condition a principled
form: a more internally cohesive container can absorb proportionally
greater simultaneous pressure before disintegrating, consistent with
§9.10.1's account of cohesion as the sole containment mechanism once
vertical structure is minimal --- disintegration is what happens when
external pressure exceeds what a container's own cohesion, whatever it
currently is, can hold together.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.9d.1 (Disintegration Is Distinct From
Isolation Dissolution).}}

§8.8a's existing dissolution and Definition 10.5b.9d.2's disintegration
are opposite in mechanism and should not be conflated despite both
resulting in a container ceasing to exist as such. §8.8a's case arises
from too few remaining coupled neighbors --- a network fragmenting until
no further launch is possible. This case arises from too many
simultaneous pressuring neighbors --- overload rather than abandonment.
A network-level audit encountering a dissolved container should check
which condition obtains before attributing the outcome to isolation by
default: disintegration under Definition 10.5b.9d.2 predicts a preceding
period of elevated D\_chaos(j,t) from multiple concurrent sources, while
§8.8a's dissolution predicts a preceding decline in λ₂(L(t)) and
shrinking coupled-neighbor count --- empirically distinguishable
signatures for what would otherwise be recorded as the same
undifferentiated outcome.

10.5b.9e Developmental Feedback Type and Regime Formation

A real, otherwise puzzling asymmetry in how developing containers
acquire their entropy regime is stated and resolved here directly:
inaccurate feedback of either valence during formation produces an
entropic result, but inaccurate negentropic feedback (a developing
container told it is acceptable, or worthy of regard, only in one
specific configuration) tends to produce a high-entropy outcome, while
inaccurate entropic feedback (a developing container told,
inconsistently and without a stable positive target, that it is
currently wrong) tends to produce a medium-entropy outcome instead. Both
are real developmental harms; they are not the same harm, and the
mechanism distinguishing them is not the quantity of behavioral options
either kind of feedback leaves open.

Definition 10.5b.9e.1 (Path-Sealing versus Path-Shifting). The
determining variable is whether the feedback ever seals off one specific
behavioral configuration as the sole, sufficient route to regard, or
whether it instead shifts continuously without ever validating any
single configuration as sufficient. Inaccurate negentropic feedback
structurally seals: it identifies one profile as *the* acceptable one,
which the developing container then has a direct incentive to construct
its entire presentation around, since that one profile reliably produces
the desired response. Inaccurate entropic feedback structurally never
seals: no configuration is ever confirmed safe or sufficient, since the
standard keeps moving, which paradoxically prevents the same
fixed-identity construction from forming around any single performance,
precisely because no performance ever earns durable validation. A
developing container under sealed feedback has a real, working answer to
"how do I secure regard" and organizes around defending it; a developing
container under unsealed feedback never gets a working answer at all,
and remains unstable rather than fixed.

\emph{Domain instances.} Canalization in development (Waddington), in
which a trajectory is channeled into a single path; a founder who
rewards only one route to promotion.

Proposition 10.5b.9e.2 (Sealing, Not Path Count, Determines
Verticality). It is the presence or absence of a sealed path, not the
number of available paths, that determines whether verticality forms.
Inaccurate entropic feedback is not less constraining than inaccurate
negentropic feedback in any simple sense --- a container receiving
contradictory, ever-shifting negative feedback may be more constrained
moment-to-moment, with no reliably safe configuration at all. What it
retains is the absence of any single behavior ever being fully and
durably validated, which removes the incentive for the container to
collapse its entire identity around defending one elevated performance.
Sealed feedback removes exactly this: one configuration works, reliably,
which is precisely the condition under which a system abandons its
actual operational complexity to defend that one working configuration
--- the mechanism already established elsewhere in this document
(§10.5b.0.9e) by which increasing verticality forces increasing
official-layer inversion, since the cover must overcorrect into full
inversion as genuine operational function recedes further behind the one
defended performance.

Definition 10.5b.9e.3 (Healthy Developmental Feedback). Stated
positively rather than only as the absence of the two failure modes
above: healthy developmental feedback is feedback that is accurate, that
leaves open as many genuine behavioral possibilities as are actually
available, while influencing the developing container in a negentropic
direction without forcing. The "without forcing" clause is load-bearing
and distinct from mere accuracy: accurate feedback that nonetheless
seals off one configuration as the sole acceptable one reproduces the
high-entropy failure mode above even while being true in content, since
the harm is structural (the sealing itself) rather than a matter of the
feedbacks factual correctness. Healthy feedback shapes trajectory
precisely by never converting a preference into an exclusive, sufficient
condition for regard --- the same distinction, restated at the level of
a single developing container, between influencing a system's direction
early enough that no inevitable slide develops at all (§10.5b.14.3b.i)
and forcing an outcome directly, which shrinks rather than preserves the
receiving system's own adaptive space.

\emph{Domain instances.} Developmental plasticity that keeps phenotypic
options open; incubator and onboarding practice that gives accurate
feedback without sealing a single route.

Proposition 10.5b.9e.4 (Split Persistence Versus Content Persistence,
Two Distinct Claims). A further, real distinction is worth stating
explicitly rather than folding into one sentence, since the two parts do
genuinely different work and can otherwise read as circular together.
First: the mere presence of the official/operational split introduced by
inaccurate developmental feedback (§10.5b.9e.1) is what determines
entropic status going forward, structurally, regardless of the splits
specific content -\/- this holds independent of what the original
inaccurate feedback actually said, since it is the architectural fact of
the split, not its content, that produces the ongoing entropic dynamics
(self-corrosion, resource debt from official-layer rewriting, the
verticality/inversion relationship of §10.5b.0.9e). Second, and not
reducible to the first: the splits specific content is exactly what
determines which mirror-proxies a high-entropy container gravitates
toward and by which extraction mechanism it operates -\/- a container
whose original inaccurate feedback centered on gendered control will
target and extract differently than one whose original feedback centered
on class shame or professional inadequacy, even though both are equally
high-entropy by the first claims structural test alone. The content, in
other words, does no work in determining *whether* a system is
high-entropy, but does substantial work in determining *how* a
high-entropy systems extraction actually manifests -\/- both claims are
true, and stating only the first risks implying the content is inert,
when it is only inert with respect to that one specific determination.

A further, real regularity worth naming: the container's official layer
may come to explicitly repudiate the original feedbacks content with
genuine, conscious sincerity, while the operational layer continues
unchanged -\/- an official layer that consciously adopts values directly
opposed to the original inaccurate feedback (a container raised under an
entitled, controlling script consciously committing to egalitarian or
non-controlling values, for instance) does not thereby resolve the
split, since the splits persistence was never contingent on
official-layer belief matching original content in the first place. This
produces the seemingly paradoxical case of an official layer whose
stated values are the near-exact inversion of the container's actual
operational entitlement: the conscious repudiation is real, the
underlying operational pattern is unmoved by it, and the resulting
distance between the two is not evidence against the mechanism but a
direct, predictable consequence of it, per §10.5b.0.9e's inversion
relationship -\/- the more genuinely the official layer overcorrects
away from the original content, the more inverted, not more resolved,
the relationship between the two layers becomes.

10.5b.9f Shock-Driven Metric Reshaping, Cumulative Knocks, and the
Maturity Asymmetry

Proposition 10.5b.9f.1 (Every Shock Reshapes the Metric, at Whatever
Magnitude It Can). Definition 4.8's dependence of g\_t on M\_t is not
restricted to the founding perturbation (§10.5b.0.0a) or to
developmental-scale events; it is a general property holding for every
event a container's memory registers, of any magnitude. A shock updates
M\_t, which updates g\_t, which changes the direction and magnitude
∇\_\{g\_t\}Φ produces going forward -\/- the same mechanism by which
developmental-origin divergence first arises, operating continuously
across a container's whole lifespan rather than being confined to its
origin. This resolves what would otherwise require a separate
interaction term for two systems reacting differently to an identical
external shock: they are not experiencing the same geometry at the
moment of impact, because each brought its own already-shaped g\_t into
the encounter, and the identical shock's gradient consequently points
toward different outcomes for each.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.9f.2 (A Reaction to a Shock as a
Distinct, Secondary Knock).}} Where a shock originates outside a
container's control and triggers a high-entropy reaction to it, by the
people, institutions or subsystems coupled to it or by the container's
own defensive machinery, that reaction is not a re-description of the
same event: it is itself a separate lateral knock, entering the
recipient's own Drive\_B (§8.8a) as an additional term distinct from the
originating shock's own contribution. A mechanistic shock followed by an
amplifying high-entropy reaction is therefore two knocks in sequence,
not one, and should be counted and attributed separately when
reconstructing a collapse's actual causal structure. Domain instances: a
cytokine storm, in which the immune system's overreaction to an
infection becomes a second injury distinct from the infection; panic
selling after a natural disaster; a regulator's overcorrection after a
market shock.

Proposition 10.5b.9f.3 (Most Collapses Are Cumulative, Not Singular).
Given 10.5b.9f.1 and §10.5b.9d's multi-source chaos machinery, most real
collapses are the accumulated-multiple-knock case -\/- repeated shocks,
each reshaping g\_t slightly, jointly driving a container past its own
