10.5b.13a.3, option (ii)) without protector-coupling or a turn toward
high entropy.

After contact: \emph{reopening}, the relaxation of β\_h(j,i,t) at rate
1/τ\_r once c(j,i,t) falls (Definition 10.5b.13b.6).

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.13b.8 (Shielding).}} Shielding is any
containment by which a container restricts what passes from its interior
across its boundary. It takes two structurally distinct forms,
distinguished not by the container's own assessment --- which reads its
shielding as protective in either case --- but by what is contained, and
by whether containment opens a gap between official and operational
layers.

\emph{Containment shielding} (constructive; negentropic; open) contains
resources. Define s\_c(j,i,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, the fraction of j's reserves
held out of reach of a counterpart not yet classified, relaxing as the
counterpart is classified open:

s\_c(j,i,t) = s\_c\^{}0(j) · c\_unres(j,i,t),

where c\_unres ∈ {[}0,1{]} is the degree to which j's classification of
i remains unresolved (1 at first contact, falling to 0 once c(j,i,t)
settles near 0). During assessment the extraction flux is computed on (1
− s\_c) · R\_j, so that τ\_X (Definition 10.5b.13b.3) scales as 1/(1 −
s\_c) and diverges as s\_c → 1: an advance arriving during assessment
finds nothing loose to take, and j remains below the plane even at
elevated τ\_h. Containment shielding acts on resource exposure alone:
∂D\_op/∂s\_c = 0, it is compatible with high G(t), and it is reversible.
The container's presence remains visible, and visibly protected.

\emph{Deceptive shielding} (destructive; entropic; closed) contains
reality: the official layer covers the operational core from
observation, external and internal alike, producing D\_op ≫ 0 at the
maintenance cost M\_decep(t) (§10.5b.18.4) --- the official-shielded
configuration (§10.5b.1). Formally, shielding is deceptive where it acts
on representation rather than on resource exposure, ∂D\_op/∂(shielding)
\textgreater{} 0, together with low G(t). Containment shielding is
containment; deceptive shielding is self-deception. Because deceptive
shielding is itself what suppresses the container's access to its own
operational state, a container shielding deceptively under low G(t)
experiences its shielding as constructive, which is why it persists.
Concentrating resources to insulate against a hostile grid is a
resource-level instance, protective in intent and converted by
Proposition 7.9.3b's concentration-liability flip into the container's
greatest vulnerability. Structural Self-Shielding (Definition
10.5b.11b.6) is, by contrast, a constructive act performed by a
high-entropy container in collapse, and retains its name under this
definition.

\emph{Cloaking} is the form that converts between the two. Define j's
visibility vis(j,t) ∈ (0,1{]} and cloaking depth c\_cl(j,t) = 1 −
vis(j,t). Cloaking deliberately dims j's official presentation below its
operational state, opening a deliberate gap D\_op\^{}cl(t) = κ\_dim ·
c\_cl(t), κ\_dim \textgreater{} 0 fitted, and scales every encounter
rate by vis: high-entropy search reaches j at vis · Σ\_search\^{}eff,
but open coupling is reduced in the same proportion, j's external
feedback and support (φ\_fb, Γ\_supp, §10.5b.18.9--18.10) entering at
vis · φ\_fb and vis · Γ\_supp. Cloaking begins as constructive: it is
protective, and fully grounded, since the container knows exactly what
it is concealing. Define its deliberateness α\_cl(j,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} ---
the degree to which the gap is held as a known tactic rather than as
self-model --- and its duration d\_cl(t) since onset. The internalized
fraction I\_cl ∈ {[}0,1{]} evolves as

dI\_cl/dt = κ\_I · c\_cl(t) · {[}(1 − α\_cl(t)) + (d\_cl(t)/T\_safe −
1)₊{]} · (1 − I\_cl), κ\_I, T\_safe \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

so that internalization is zero if and only if cloaking is both fully
deliberate (α\_cl = 1) and temporary (d\_cl ≤ T\_safe), and failure of
either condition alone suffices to drive it. Internalization has three
consequences: the gap survives the end of cloaking, a residual
D\_op\^{}res = κ\_dim · I\_cl · c\_cl\^{}max persisting after c\_cl
returns to zero; grounding falls, G(t) being reduced by the factor (1 −
I\_cl · c\_cl\^{}max) since the gap is now part of the container's
self-model rather than a known tactic; and the meta-layer shifts from
membrane toward firewall, ω\_meta\^{}up(t) entering at (1 − κ\_ω ·
I\_cl) · ω\_meta\^{}up(t) to maintain the dimmed surface's separation
from the operational core. As I\_cl → 1, cloaking has become deceptive
shielding, and the container has moved toward the medium-entropy regime
--- a permanent shrinking away from its own negentropic core. Cloaking
is accordingly warranted for a low-entropy container only while
Ψ\_total(j,t) \textless{} Ψ\^{}*(j,t) cannot otherwise be corrected, and
is to be dropped once containment shielding alone suffices to keep the
container below the plane. Internalized cloaking is a characteristic
developmental route into the medium-entropy regime: a container that
shrinks to avoid punitive knocks, absent any vertical script to adopt in
its place (§10.5b.2.12), and comes to take the shrinking as its own
nature, α\_cl having been low from the start.

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.13b.9 (Saturation Death by Isolation).}}
Let f\_sat(j,t) be the fraction of counterparts in j's coupling range
for which c(j,i,t) ≈ 1. Since open exchange is possible only between
open systems, admissible exchange is bounded by E\_exch\^{}adm(j,t) ≤ (1
− f\_sat(j,t)) · Ē\_open(j,t), where Ē\_open is the exchange available
from open counterparts in range. In a saturated grid (Proposition
10.5b.13a.3), f\_sat = 1: however low j's hardening latency, β\_h → 1
against every counterpart, E\_exch\^{}adm → 0, and §7.4's balance
reduces to

dR/dt = P\_prod − W(t),

with W\_def(t) included in W(t) through M\_add(t). Where P\_prod
\textless{} W, reserves are exhausted at

t\_dep ≈ R(t\_s) / (W − P\_prod)

after saturation onset t\_s, for approximately constant rates; once R
reaches zero, the clipping rule of §7.3 holds W at R, and unfunded
defense and maintenance accrue as resource debt. The container is
depleted not by strikes, which its hardening successfully deflects, but
by isolation; and since W\_def grows with the number of contacts held
hardened, prolonged defense itself shortens t\_dep. This is why boundary
defense, the most robust strategy at every grid intensity short of
saturation, is not on its own sufficient at saturation, and why
Proposition 10.5b.13a.3's remaining options take over there. The same
depletion follows short of saturation wherever miscalibration makes
hardening indiscriminate (β̄\_h\^{}open → 1, Definition 10.5b.13b.6),
which identifies the central hazard of prolonged survival in a hostile
grid: a container whose hardening becomes permanent and indiscriminate
preserves itself in the short term while losing the adaptive capacity
that defined its health.

\hypertarget{b.14-rogue-metamessengers-and-slow-correction-reversal}{%
\subsection{10.5b.14 Rogue Metamessengers and Slow-Correction
Reversal}\label{b.14-rogue-metamessengers-and-slow-correction-reversal}}

A high-entropy container is not always healed only from outside. A
small, coordinated subset of a container's own meta-representation
apparatus --- aware the container's operational layer is corrupted and
actively working against what the container's official layer believes it
is doing --- can raise Π\_f(t) deliberately, provided the rate of
increase, not merely its eventual level, stays below whatever threshold
triggers F\_conf's destabilizing surge. Formally: a controlled injection
dΠ\_f/dt = ε\_rogue, with ε\_rogue small and bounded well under the rate
that would cross Π\_c abruptly. Held slow enough, this permits D\_op to
be metabolized incrementally through ordinary correction rather than
forcing a confrontation event, allowing gradual regime transition ---
high-entropy toward medium-entropy or, ideally, toward the low-entropy
baseline --- without ever triggering Flip Condition A.

\textbf{10.5b.14.1 Entropic, Negentropic, and Rogue Metamessengers}

Three terms, precisely distinguished.

\emph{\textbf{Negentropic metamessengers}} are, regime-generally and by
fixed meaning, those whose current orientation favors the container's
true, healthy signal --- the direction that reduces overall entropy if
it prevails.

\emph{\textbf{Entropic metamessengers}} are, regime-generally and by
fixed meaning, those whose orientation favors the container's false or
unhealthy signal --- the direction that sustains or increases overall
entropy if it prevails.

\emph{\textbf{Rogue metamessengers}} are defined relationally, not
absolutely: those whose current orientation opposes the operational
layer's own direction, whatever that direction currently is. Because
operational's own alignment differs by regime, ``rogue'' names a
different population in each:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{High-entropy:} operational is entropic. Rogues --- opposing
  operational --- are negentropic-aligned. Rogue and negentropic are the
  same population in this regime, and §10.5b.14's protective minority is
  properly named either way.
\item
  \textbf{Medium-entropy:} operational is negentropic. Rogues ---
  opposing operational --- are entropic-aligned. Rogue and entropic are
  the same population in this regime; negentropic metamessengers here
  are aligned \emph{with} operational and are explicitly not rogues.
  This is the population whose convergence with the official layer
  during a lateral knock predicts collapse rather than reset (§10.5b's
  convergence-direction account); a medium-entropy container whose
  metamessenger population is majority-rogue can never organize a large
  negentropic bulk at once, leaving its negentropic operational core
  persistently outweighed and the container correspondingly more
  vulnerable to knocks and to sustained coupling with predatory
  neighbors.
\end{itemize}

Formally, both entropic and negentropic fractions are already carried by
Ψ\_loyal(t) (§10.5b.18.12) within the closed population, and by
Ψ\_total(t) (§10.5b.18.14) across the whole population; ``rogue''
introduces no new quantity, only a regime-relational name for whichever
of these fractions currently opposes operational.

10.5b.14.1a Containment Radius and Grid Capture

Definition 10.5b.14.1a (Containment Radius). Self-interested
disinformation (§10.5b.14.1's rogue-metamessenger rewriting, the
M\_o,t\^{}false substitution mechanism) varies not only in rate and cost
but in reach: define containment radius r\_cont(i,t) as the set of
containers whose own official-layer self-model is measurably shifted by
container is rewriting activity, ranging from the fully self-contained
case (r\_cont = \{i\}, the rewrite affects only i's own official layer,
with no measurable external spread) up to grid capture (r\_cont ⊇ \{i,
j\_1, ..., j\_n\}, the rewrite measurably shifts the official-layer
self-models of multiple other containers coupled to i or to each other).
This is a real, distinct axis from everything else already established
about self-interested disinformation: rate (how fast M\_o,t\^{}false
operates) and cost (§10.5b.18.23b's escalating suppression cost) both
describe the rewrites internal economy; containment radius describes its
external footprint.

Proposition 10.5b.14.1b (Grid Capture as Compounding, Not Merely
Additive, Spread). Where r\_cont(i,t) includes multiple containers, the
mechanism is not is rewrite independently reaching each one, but each
newly-captured container j becoming, once its own official layer has
shifted, a further source of the same rewritten content toward its own
coupled neighbors -\/- Inject(j → \{o,p\}, t) (§10.5b's
shared-false-self-model forcing term) now carrying is originally
self-interested content outward through j's own coupling, rather than i
needing to reach each grid member directly. This produces genuine,
compounding spread through an already-coupled network rather than a
fixed number of independent transmission events, and gives grid capture
its real, qualitative difference from the self-contained case: what
begins as one container's self-serving narrative can propagate through
successive re-transmission to reach containers with no direct coupling
to i at all, provided the network's own coupling structure supports the
chain.

Definition 10.5b.14.1c (Capture Threshold and Susceptibility). A given
coupled neighbor j is captured -\/- its own official layer measurably
shifted toward is self-interested content -\/- when the incoming
pressure from Inject(i → j, t) (or from an already-captured intermediate
container per 10.5b.14.1b) exceeds j's own resistance, itself a function
of j's existing Ψ\_loyal(t) (a container already high in
negentropic-metamessenger alignment resists capture more strongly, since
more of its own population must be converted before its official layer
shifts) and j's own coupling strength to i or to the capturing
intermediate (Λ\_ij(t), stronger coupling admitting a stronger forcing
signal). This gives grid capture real, checkable predictors distinct
from i's own individual severity: a grid of already-high-alignment,
weakly-coupled containers resists capture even from a highly entropic,
aggressively rewriting i, while a grid of low-alignment, tightly-coupled
containers is vulnerable to capture even from a comparatively mild
source, since susceptibility is a property of the receiving network's
own structure and composition, not only of the originating container's
own entropic severity.

Proposition 10.5b.14.1d (Dangerous Groupthink as the Realized,
Multi-Container Case). Where r\_cont(i,t) has genuinely expanded to
include multiple containers per 10.5b.14.1b-c, the resulting state -\/-
many coupled containers official layers converged on the same
self-interested, originally-single-source content, each now treating
that content as independently-confirmed shared reality rather than
recognizing its single, self-interested point of origin -\/- is the
formal content of dangerous groupthink at grid scale. This is
mechanically distinct from §10.5b.2.24's population-level selection
(which concerns evolutionary pressure favoring split architectures
across a population over time, not the active capture of shared belief)
and from grid-wide observational unreliability (which concerns an
outside tracker's own difficulty classifying regimes from official-layer
signal, not participant containers' own beliefs about their shared
reality). Grid capture is the case where the grid's own members, not
merely outside observers, have their actual shared understanding of
reality distorted by a single originating sources self-interested
content, propagated through the same coupling structure that would,
absent capture, support genuine reciprocal exchange.

\textbf{10.5b.14.2 Emergency Firewall Reinforcement}

§10.5b.14's ε\_rogue mechanism describes deliberate, slow, controlled
disclosure --- a routine, ongoing negentropic action. A separate, faster
mechanism exists for a different situation entirely: an acute, large
spike in Π\_f(t), of a magnitude routine leak-patching (§10.5b.18.1's
M\_o,t\^{}false, event-triggered around ordinary glitches) is not
equipped to contain, and which risks catastrophic, uncontrolled feedback
flooding if left unaddressed.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.14.2 (Emergency Firewall
Reinforcement).}}

Where Π\_f(t) crosses a fitted large-spike threshold Θ\_spike --- set
well above the routine fluctuation range already characteristic of the
high-entropy regime, distinguishing a genuine flooding risk from an
ordinary small leak --- the negentropic-aligned (rogue) fraction of the
closed population, Ψ\_loyal(t), executes an automatic, emergency
response: official's memory is rapidly restored toward its pre-spike
baseline, and an additional restorative pull is applied directly to
Π\_f(t) itself,

F\_emerg(t) = κ\_emerg · Ψ\_loyal(t) · (Π\_f(t) − Θ\_spike)₊, κ\_emerg
\textgreater{} 0 fitted,

pulling permeability back below the spike threshold. This is
mechanically distinct from routine cover-maintenance despite
superficially resembling it: routine patching (entropic-aligned,
triggered by any leak, purpose is sustaining cover-and-lure) and
emergency reinforcement (negentropic-aligned, triggered only by a large
spike, purpose is preventing collapse) can produce a similarly-shaped
memory correction while being executed by opposite populations for
opposite reasons. A high enough, sustained Π\_f(t) that F\_emerg(t)
cannot bring back under Θ\_spike proceeds toward Flip Condition A's
ordinary tipping machinery (§10.5b.7--§10.5b.9) rather than being
contained.

\emph{\textbf{The rogue metamessenger tradeoff, stated explicitly.}}
§10.5b.14's ε\_rogue mechanism accrues real self-deception debt
(M\_decep, §10.5b.18.4) as a side effect of slow, controlled disclosure,
and §10.5b.14.2's emergency reinforcement accrues comparable cost
restoring memory toward baseline. Neither mechanism is cost-free, and
both are chosen anyway because the rogue-negentropic population judges
the accrued debt the lesser of two evils against the alternative:
uncontrolled feedback flooding and the collapse it would otherwise
cause. This is a deliberate, known tradeoff internal to the mechanism's
own operation, not an unintended side effect discovered after the fact
--- the debt is accepted, not merely incurred.

\hypertarget{b.14.3-systems-override-high-entropy-and-medium-entropy-versions}{%
\subsection{10.5b.14.3 Systems Override, High-Entropy and Medium-Entropy
Versions}\label{b.14.3-systems-override-high-entropy-and-medium-entropy-versions}}

Definition 10.5b.14.3a (High-Entropy Systems Override). Operational
already holds default control (𝒜(t) = 1, §10.5b.18.18) in the
high-entropy regime. Override here is operational actively reinforcing
or exercising that control specifically in response to a real threat
signal (Θ\_threat(i,j,t) \textgreater{} 0, §8.6a.9) --- either by
resisting any ceding of 𝒜(t) it would otherwise allow, or by directing
operational-aligned (entropic) metamessengers to accelerate the rate of
M\_o,t\^{}false substitution (§10.5b.14.1) specifically to keep the
threatening signal from reaching official at all. This is not a new
primitive: it is a real, threat-triggered intensification of
control-locus assertion and reality-rewriting rate, both already
defined, occurring together specifically because operational's own
survival is what the threat endangers. It is control-shaped by
Definition 10.5b.0.11's own criteria, and remains harmful under the Do
No Harm Principle (§10.5b.0.11) except where operational is itself
acting to reverse an imminent terminal lock, the one exception that
principle already carves out. This is not limited to threat response:
the same override triggers during active predation itself --- while
operational is administering extraction and displacement knocks (§8.6a)
against a target --- not only when a threat signal is present. In the
predation case, the overrides distinctive further effect is
retrospective: official receives a different version of events, or a
constructed justification for them, after the fact --- not merely a
suppressed or absent signal, but an actively fabricated account of what
operational did and why. This retrospective narrative construction,
distinct from the real-time M\_o,t\^{}false substitution already
covered, is what makes this a genuinely dangerous mechanism rather than
a passive information gap: official is not left uninformed, it is
actively misinformed about its own containers specific past actions.

Definition 10.5b.14.3b (Medium-Entropy Systems Override: Pre-Breach
Prevention and Mid-Collapse Steering, Two Distinct Mechanisms). A
structurally opposite mechanism from the high-entropy version above:
medium-entropy's operational layer is genuinely intact and negentropic
(§10.5b.2.13's Type B), and override here is that intact operational
layer sending a real, discrete intervention -\/- an instruction or
corrective signal into the container's own dynamics. This document
previously treated the two timings below as one mechanism differing only
in when it fires; they are stated here as two genuinely distinct
mechanisms instead, since they require different postures and different
information, not merely earlier or later application of the same
posture: influencing a system's direction early enough that it never
needs to find the best outcome from an inevitable slide is a different
kind of act from skillfully finding that best outcome once the slide is
already underway, even though both serve the same eventual goal.

10.5b.14.3b.i (Pre-Breach Prevention). Triggered while M\_tip(t) is
still below the hull-breach threshold (§10.5a) -\/- genuine prevention,
requiring operational to recognize and correct a developing trajectory
before any breach occurs at all, so that no B1/B2 resolution is ever
needed. This is the healthier, cheaper, and harder-to-execute of the
two: it requires detecting a problem while it is still small enough that
no dramatic intervention is needed, which demands more sustained
attentiveness from operational than waiting for an unmistakable breach
to react to.

10.5b.14.3b.ii (Mid-Collapse Steering). Triggered only after breach has
already occurred, while the container is still moving through a Flip B
trajectory (§10.5b.7--10.5b.9) toward an outcome not yet resolved -\/-
steering that resolution toward B2 (full reorganization, the
medium-entropy 360 reset) rather than B1 (terminal lock), riding the
collapse already underway through to reset rather than preventing it
from having occurred. This is damage control with a genuinely good
outcome, not prevention: the slide happens regardless, and the
operational layer's real contribution is ensuring it resolves toward
renewal rather than lock, not avoiding it in the first place.

The real, characteristic outcome of either mechanism, distinct from
merely reaching B2 over an unspecified trajectory: each frequently saves
the container from the worse alternative, improves feedback permeability
in the healthy sense -\/- rising Π\_f(t) here reflects genuine opening,
not the resource-exhaustion failure of §10.5b.13.2 -\/- vents
accumulated divergence pressure in the process, and moves the container
toward the low-entropy baseline rather than merely toward a labeled
tipping outcome. This is the concrete, positive content behind calling
either version a healthy mechanism: real resolution of accumulated
pressure and real movement toward baseline, not just avoidance of the
worse alternative -\/- though (i) achieves this without ever paying the
cost of a breach at all, while (ii) pays that cost and recovers well
from it, which is a real, worthwhile distinction for any system deciding
which capability to invest in developing. A further, real asymmetry
worth stating explicitly rather than leaving implicit: high-entropy has
no internal analog to either mechanism above. Ṗ(t)=1 (full operational
control, §10.5b.18.18) holds as the default in both regimes equally, so
the difference is not that medium-entropy's operational holds more
functional control than high-entropy's -\/- both hold the same full
share. The difference is that medium-entropy's operational is the
genuinely intact, negentropic party (§10.5b.2.13's Type B), capable of
using that control to correct the very container it controls, while
high-entropy's operational is itself the entropic party, and cannot
serve as an equivalent self-rescuing actor for the same reason a
corrupted process cannot audit itself. What high-entropy structurally
lacks, precisely stated, is a decisive, reliable, at-will self-rescue
capacity of the kind §10.5b.14.3b gives medium-entropy: no internal
actor can simply choose to execute a full, controlled reorganization on
command, since the actor that would need to do so is precisely the part
of the system that is not trustworthy or self-correcting. This is
narrower than claiming no internal healing pathway exists at all, and
two real ones already established elsewhere in this document do
originate internally rather than externally: §10.5b.14.1's
rogue-metamessenger population (the negentropic-aligned minority already
present within a high-entropy container's own operational layer, capable
of gradually shifting the container's balance over time), and
§10.5b.13.2's voluntary self-relinquishment (the container's own
deliberate act of admitting its operational reality to itself). Both are
genuine, internally-originating pathways, not external interventions.
What makes sustained external anchoring (§10.5b.10) so important is not
that it is the only route in -\/- it is that both internal pathways are
slow, unreliable, or actively dangerous without it: the
rogue-metamessenger route can take a long time and has no guaranteed
outcome, and voluntary self-relinquishment specifically triggers the
flooding crisis already described at §10.5b.13.2's cause (e), which
risks collapse rather than recovery absent an anchor to survive it.
Anchoring is what makes these real internal pathways survivable, not
what makes them possible in the first place.

Override\_high(i,t) = 1 when Θ\_threat(i,j,t) \textgreater{} 0 triggers
reinforced 𝒜(t) retention or accelerated M\_o,t\^{}false substitution
rate, per Definition 10.5b.14.3a. Override\_medium(i,t) = 1 when
operational's own intervention is triggered either pre-breach (shifting
M\_tip's own trajectory below threshold) or mid-collapse (shifting the
B1/B2 mass-weighting split, §10.5b.7, toward B2), per Definition
10.5b.14.3b. Neither version is performed by metamessengers of either
loyalty; Ψ\_loyal(t), Ψ\_loyal\^{}corrob(t), and the rogue-metamessenger
mechanisms above (§10.5b.14, §10.5b.14.1--10.5b.14.2) remain entirely
distinct processes, operating on permeability and disclosure rather than
on control-locus assertion or direct trajectory intervention.

\textbf{10.5b.15 Two Collapse-Layer Axioms}

The mechanisms above rest on two claims general enough, and load-bearing
enough, to state as axioms of the collapse layer specifically, alongside
Axioms C3 and C4 (§10.5b.0) governing the same layer's
structural-geometric side. C1 and C2 below govern divergence dynamics;
C3 and C4 govern structural geometry; all four are distinct from Part
3's foundational axioms.

\emph{Note.} Axiom C1 (Truth-Only Reorganization) is stated in the
spine, Part 3, with Definition 10.5b.0.0b and Proposition 10.5b.0.0c.

Proposition 10.5b.0.0a (The Founding Perturbation as Corrective Target).
Axiom C1 states that unforced convergence runs toward the true signal;
this proposition supplies the missing causal direction for why a
container's dynamics are oriented toward that signal at all, rather than
the orientation itself being an unexplained given. A bounded container's
own existence as a distinguishable, individuated entity is not
temporally or causally prior to divergence -\/- per §2.11's
persistent-signature material and the container ontology of §2.2, a
container comes into being precisely at the perturbation event that
first distinguishes it from an undifferentiated surrounding field, not
before it. That founding perturbation is simultaneously the event that
grants the container its own bounded identity and the event that
introduces its first genuine divergence from the undifferentiated,
unbounded state that preceded it. ℱ's own unforced drift toward the true
underlying signal (Axiom C1) is, under this reading, most precisely
understood as a restorative gradient aimed back toward the nearest
achievable approximation of that pre-perturbation, undivided state -\/-
not a return to it, which the container's own continued existence as a
bounded entity forecloses, but the closest convergence available to a
system that owes its very individuation to the same event it is
perpetually correcting for. This gives Φ's own landscape minimum (§6.4)
a real causal grounding rather than leaving its location as a structural
given: the landscape's minimum sits where it does because that is the
achievable limit of the restorative direction the founding perturbation
itself set in motion, not an arbitrary feature of the potential's
construction. A container's collapse into non-existence, symmetrically,
is never a full return to the pre-perturbation state either, since that
state was never a state any bounded container could occupy -\/- collapse
is the founding boundary's own dissolution, not a completion of the
corrective trajectory the container spent its existence pursuing.

\emph{Scope of the restorative target.} The undivided state toward which
this restorative gradient is directed is informational: undivided
coherence, zero divergence between the container's own layers and
between its model and the field it represents. It is not the
thermodynamic field out of which the container was individuated, which
is the entropic background Definition 10.5b.2.20 refers to when it calls
entropy the default, baseline condition. The two are distinct states.
Axiom C1's restorative direction is negentropic, toward the first, while
the container remains embedded in the second.

\emph{Domain instances.} Wound healing restores tissue toward its
original pattern, and a scar is the nearest achievable approximation;
error correction recovers an original signal from a corrupted copy;
ecological succession returns a disturbed system toward its prior state
without ever reproducing it exactly. In each, the restorative target is
the system's own prior organization, approached but not reached.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.0.0d.1 (Registration Weights).}}
Registration happens in the meta-layer, the only layer that compares the
other two (Definition 6.1, Proposition 10.5b.1.6). Let e\_k\^{}p(t) ∈
{[}0,1{]} be the weight meta-layer sub-process k places on the
operational representation P\_p, and e\_k\^{}o(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} the weight
it places on the official representation P\_o, in forming its local view
P\_m\^{}(k)(t); the two are not constrained to sum to one, since a
sub-process can be exposed to both layers or to neither. The
registration weights are

ρ\_op(t) = Σ\_k φ\_k(t)·e\_k\^{}p(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, ρ\_po(t) = Σ\_k
φ\_k(t)·e\_k\^{}o(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]},

ρ\_op the share of the meta-layer's aggregate view exposed to the
operational record, including the record of the blocked drive, and ρ\_po
the share exposed to the official layer's values. Both are
adapter-estimated, for example as the share of each sub-process's inputs
drawn from each layer's records, and carry that provenance (§15.2).

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.0.0d (Manufactured Justification).}}
Suppose ρ\_op(t) \textgreater{} 0, so that the blocked drive is
registered, and Π\_f(t) \textless{} Π* (Proposition 10.5b.0.0c), so that
it cannot be resolved by convergence. A registered gap between the
official account and the operational record then has two possible
outcomes. It is corrected, which moves O\_o(P) toward O\_p(P) and is the
convergence route the permeability condition excludes, or it is covered
by a representation that contradicts the operational record. The cover
is the denial scaffold whose cost is M\_decep(t) (§10.5b.18.4). To first
order its official side grows as

dM\_decep/dt ⊇ κ\_just·ρ\_op(t)·U(t), κ\_just \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

so that the scaffold's size tracks the accumulated drive 𝒰(t), and its
operational side enters the devaluation link of Proposition 10.5b.19.1b
as V\_p(recip) = V\_o(recip)·(1 − κ\_dev·ρ\_po(t)·L(i,t)): the
operational layer can deny only the official values it has registered.
The same blocked drive therefore feeds both denials of Definition
10.5b.18.23c, official denial of operational facts and operational
denial of official values, and contrast suppression is their grid-level
component. Justification is a consequence of registered blocked
convergence, not an independent drive.

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.0.0d.2 (Insulated and Indifferent
Layers).}} Where ρ\_op(t) = 0, the official layer never receives the
operational record and produces no justification; it is uninformed
rather than deceptive. Where ρ\_po(t) = 0, the operational layer never
registers the official values, V\_p(recip) = V\_o(recip), and the
contempt of link (4) of Proposition 10.5b.19.1b does not arise, whatever
friction link (3) produces; the operational layer is indifferent to
reciprocal coupling rather than contemptuous of it. The cases are
separable in data: an insulated official layer is silent about the gap,
while one that registers the gap and cannot close it produces escalating
justification; an indifferent operational layer ignores reciprocal
coupling, while one that registers its value devalues and suppresses it.

\emph{\textbf{Hypothesis 10.5b.0.0d.H (No Fully Insulated Layer).}}
Every container with a self-model has ρ\_op \textgreater{} 0 and ρ\_po
\textgreater{} 0: no official layer is fully insulated from its
operational state, and no operational layer from its official values, so
blocked convergence in such a container always generates both
justification pressure and devaluation pressure. This is stated as a
hypothesis, to be tested by estimating the registration weights, not
assumed. It is consistent with Proposition 10.5b.1.6, under which layers
that remain distinct enough to be compared remain exposed to one
another.

\emph{Domain instances.} A chronic wound keeps signaling to heal but
cannot, and the signaling itself sustains the inflammation that damages
it further; debridement and restored oxygenation raise the wound's
capacity to act on the signal, and the same healing drive then closes
it. A memory system that thrashes, or a control loop that cannot
converge, spends a growing share of its resources on the failed attempt
itself. A failing institution unable to meet its own mission produces
escalating justification rather than correction. In autoimmune and
allergic disease, a protective drive acting through a miscalibrated
classifier becomes the injury it is meant to prevent. Prediction:
divergence is highest where restorative drive is strong and permeability
is near zero, and raising permeability past Π* converts the same drive
into convergence.

\emph{Note.} Axiom C2 (Runaway Acceleration) and Corollary C2.1 are
stated in the spine, Part 3.

\emph{Note.} Axiom C5 (Bidirectional Closure --- the Horseshoe
Principle) is stated in the spine, Part 3; the tracking-fidelity measure
that diagnoses it follows here.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.15.1 (Tracking Fidelity).}}

Over a trailing window of length w, allowing for realistic response lag
δ ∈ {[}0, δ\_max{]} rather than requiring simultaneity:

\emph{Φ}\_track(t) = max\_\{δ∈{[}0,δ\_max{]}\} corr\_\{s∈{[}t−w,t{]}\}(
‖v(s−δ)‖\_g , D(s) ).

\emph{Limits of the measure.} Φ\_track measures whether a container
moves more when divergence is higher, not whether it moves in the
correcting direction. Directional fidelity is measured by the companion
Φ\_dir(t) = mean\_\{s∈{[}t−w,t{]}\} cos\_g(v(s), −grad Φ\^{}G\_s(P(s))),
the average alignment between the container's motion and the descent
direction of its grounded potential. Where either series in Φ\_track has
zero variance across the window, the correlation is undefined and
Φ\_track is recorded as unavailable. Because maximizing over lags
inflates correlation, Φ\_track is calibrated against the distribution of
the same statistic on time-shuffled series and reported relative to that
null's 95th percentile.

Both horseshoe extremes are diagnosed by the same underlying signature
--- a mismatch between what a container's divergence pressure D(t) is
doing and what its velocity is doing --- rather than by simply flagging
low variation in movement:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  Rigid closure: D(t) elevated or rising while ‖v(t)‖\_g stays pinned
  near its floor across the window --- the container is not moving
  despite a real, worsening problem.
\item
  Compulsive closure: ‖v(t)‖\_g stays pinned near its ceiling while D(t)
  is low or falling across the window --- the container keeps moving
  despite having no real problem left to move about.
\item
  Neither is triggered when D(t) and ‖v(t)‖\_g are jointly low ---
  genuine, healthy equilibrium (§6.9), not a diagnostic failure. Note
  that a high-entropy container's velocity already reflects its full
  internal state, including its churning operational layer (§4.3); such
  a container is not quiet in this sense even though its official layer
  alone would appear calm, so this diagnostic does not misfire on it ---
  the apparent-vs-actual distinction is exactly what §10.5b.1's
  σ\_o/σ\_p classification already tracks separately.
\end{itemize}

The theory does not need a separate diagnostic for each extreme; the
same test that fails to find a real relationship between motion and
cause catches both. §16.10's Definition 16.10 (Structural Baseline)
supplies the comparison baseline this diagnostic needs before ``pinned
near its floor/ceiling'' can be applied to a real case, since these are
meaningful only relative to a container's own typical range.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.15.2 (Instantaneous Velocity Crossing).}}

Any oscillating trajectory, however violent, passes through ‖v(t)‖\_g ≈
0 naturally each time it reverses direction. Define the instantaneous
crossing set 𝒵(j) = \{t : ‖v\_j(t)‖\_g \textless{} ε\_zero\} for a
fitted tolerance ε\_zero, distinct from Φ\_track(j,t) (§10.5b.15.1),
which is computed over a full trailing window w and cannot be satisfied
by a single crossing alone.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.15.1 (Sampling Fidelity Requirement).}}

A container i's coupling or engagement decision toward a neighbor j
(§8.3's edge existence, §10.5b.11a-c's targeting and search mechanics)
should be gated on Φ\_track(j,t) --- the same windowed diagnostic
§10.5b.15.1 already uses for self-diagnosis --- rather than on a raw,
single-timepoint observation of ‖v\_j(t)‖\_g. Where i's own observation
of j happens to fall within 𝒵(j) and i treats that single sample as
evidence of §6.9's genuine equilibrium, i has committed a real,
falsifiable misread: engaging with j on the belief that j has reached
stability, when j's own windowed Φ\_track would show continued high
oscillation. This is a real, distinct failure mode from the existing
apparent-vs-actual warning (§10.5b.1, §15.2), which concerns a
container's \emph{official layer} misrepresenting its own state; this
failure occurs even when j's official and operational layers are both
being observed accurately, purely because the observing container i
sampled at the wrong instant. An adapter supporting inter-container
coupling decisions should therefore expose Φ\_track(j,t) as an
observable neighbors can query, not merely ‖v\_j(t)‖\_g at the current
instant.

\hypertarget{b.15.3-epistemic-capture}{%
\subsection{10.5b.15.3 Epistemic
Capture}\label{b.15.3-epistemic-capture}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.15.3 (Epistemic Capture).}} For a
medium-entropy container i, define capture as a real, near-total
collapse of feedback permeability specifically toward alternative
framings rather than toward correction in general:

EC(i,t) = 1 − Π\_f\^{}alt(i,t)/Π\_f\^{}ref,

where Π\_f\^{}alt(i,t) is i's feedback permeability specifically to
signals contradicting its currently-dominant entropic self-narrative (a
real, narrower quantity than ordinary Π\_f, §6.5, which does not
distinguish confirming from disconfirming signal), and Π\_f\^{}ref is a
domain-specified reference level for genuinely open alternative-signal
reception. EC(i,t) → 1 is the formal content of the entropic message no
longer being received as one signal among others, but as the whole of
perceived reality --- distinct from, and more severe than, ordinary low
permeability, which still admits the theoretical possibility of
alternative signal even when little arrives.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.15.3.1 (Capture Raises the Real Cost of
Rescue).}} Where EC(i,t) is high, ordinary anchoring and messaging
interventions (§10.5b.13's anchor eligibility, §10.5b.18.9's support
functions) require a real, additional penalty to reach i at all, since
the intervention itself is exactly the kind of alternative-framing
signal EC(i,t) is defined to suppress:

effective intervention strength = raw intervention strength · (1 −
EC(i,t)),

recovering ordinary, undiscounted intervention effectiveness when
EC(i,t) → 0, and correctly predicting that the same anchoring effort
which reliably helps a merely-drawn-toward-the-sink container can fail
outright against a genuinely captured one --- a real, falsifiable
distinction this document's existing machinery, treating extraction pull
as the only relevant medium-entropy vulnerability, could not previously
draw.

\hypertarget{b.16-compulsive-closure-and-the-boomerang-mechanism}{%
\subsection{10.5b.16 Compulsive Closure and the Boomerang
Mechanism}\label{b.16-compulsive-closure-and-the-boomerang-mechanism}}

A container may become closed not through rigidity but through its
opposite: a fixed commitment to producing change, sufficient that
reaching genuine alignment removes the compulsion's reason for existing,
and the compulsion --- rather than subsiding --- manufactures fresh
divergence to resolve.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.16.1 (Alignment-Averse Forcing).}}

For a container diagnosed with compulsive closure (Definition
10.5b.15.1: Φ\_track degenerate with v pinned high), define a transient
forcing term active only as the container's aggregate divergence
approaches its own historical floor:

\emph{F}\_boom(t) = κ\_boom · Π\_f(t) · 𝟙{[}D(t) \textless{} D\_low{]} ·
(D\_low − D(t)), κ\_boom \textgreater{} 0 fitted.

D\_low is a fitted proximity-to-alignment threshold, analogous in role
to §10.5b.3's Π\_c.~F\_boom(t) enters §6.4's landscape dynamics as a
term opposing the ordinary downhill drift, pushing the container away
from Φ's minimum rather than toward it. Its magnitude is largest exactly
at D(t) → 0 and fades as the container moves back into divergence ---
the mirror image of F\_conf(t) (§10.5b.3), which is largest under
elevated divergence and fades toward baseline. Where F\_conf formalizes
a struggling container's pain at confronting its own bad numbers,
F\_boom formalizes a compulsively-closed container's inability to
tolerate having no bad numbers left to confront.

\emph{Domain instances.} Integrator windup and pilot-induced
oscillation, in which a system destabilizes itself as it nears its
target; a firm that undermines its own turnaround as recovery
approaches.

\hypertarget{b.17-binary-trust-sorting-and-the-inversion-failure}{%
\subsection{10.5b.17 Binary Trust Sorting and the Inversion
Failure}\label{b.17-binary-trust-sorting-and-the-inversion-failure}}

A container attempting to direct help toward its own struggling members
must correctly identify which members are net extractors and which are
net targets of extraction (§8.6a). Where a container has previously
adopted a binary trust classification of its own neighbors --- sorting
them into a trusted set and a suspect set, rather than continuously
tracking their actual extraction behavior --- that classification can
become a liability distinct from ordinary misdiagnosis.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.17.1 (Binary Trust Label).}}

A container C possessing this precondition maintains a label ℓ :
\{C\_j\} → \{trusted, suspect\} over its neighbors, updated on a slower
timescale than the underlying extraction dynamics ρ(j), X\_\{i,j\}
(§8.6a) --- the defining feature of the precondition is that ℓ is sticky
relative to the dynamics it is meant to track, not that it is wrong at
any single instant.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.17.2 (Inversion Condition).}}

Once C's own divergence pressure ((1−I(t)) + S̃(t), §10.5a) exceeds a
fitted confusion threshold, ℓ's assignment inverts relative to actual
role: neighbors with high outbound extraction flux (§8.6a's X\_\{j,k\})
are disproportionately likely to hold the trusted label, while neighbors
who are net targets of extraction --- visibly distressed, elevated σ\_o
or high inbound extraction --- are disproportionately likely to hold the
suspect label. The mechanism is not random misclassification; it is that
visible distress is read as evidence for the suspect label rather than
as evidence of being extracted from, precisely when C is least able to
tell the difference.

\emph{Domain instances.} Autoimmunity paired with tumor tolerance, in
which the immune system attacks the body's own tissue while tolerating
the tumor; a regulator that treats a fraud's operator as trustworthy and
the analyst who warned about it as a nuisance.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.17.1 (Misdirected Anchoring Expands
Reach).}}

Where C extends anchoring or resource support (§10.5b.13) preferentially
to trusted-labeled neighbors under the inversion condition, and the
trusted set is disproportionately composed of actual extractors, the
support functions as an involuntary transfer into an extractor's own
resource balance (§7.4) rather than relief for a target. This has two
consequences beyond the original mistargeting: it relaxes the resource
constraints (§7.3's clipping, §10.3's stress/debt-driven contraction of
d\_A) that would otherwise limit the extractor's own capacity to keep
extracting, and --- since coupling strength and edge existence (§8.3,
§8.5) respond to a container's available resource and standing --- it
widens the extractor's practical reach to neighbors beyond the one that
supplied the support. The extractor does not merely extract more from C;
it becomes better resourced to extract from the rest of the network as
well.

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.17.1.}}

A container satisfying Definition 10.5b.17.1 is, all else equal, more
fragile under high internal divergence than an otherwise-identical
container without a sticky binary trust structure, because its confusion
converts directly into a targeting failure with network-wide
consequences (Proposition 10.5b.17.1) rather than remaining contained to
a single misjudgment. This connects Axiom C5's bidirectional-closure
account to §11.7's fragmentation and Proposition 12.8's coupling:
misdirected anchoring is a concrete mechanism by which one container's
confusion measurably degrades its neighbors' network coherence, not
merely its own.

\hypertarget{b.17.3-live-third-party-misdirection}{%
\subsection{10.5b.17.3 Live Third-Party
Misdirection}\label{b.17.3-live-third-party-misdirection}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.17.3 (Third-Party Misdirected
Anchoring).}}

A container C\_k, itself in the low-entropy baseline or medium-entropy
regime and distinct from any container satisfying Definition
10.5b.17.1's self-targeting precondition, can commit the same inversion
Definition 10.5b.17.2 describes while directing its own anchoring or
support (§10.5b.13) toward a pair of neighbors it is not itself confused
about in the sticky-label sense --- it need not maintain a formal ℓ
classification at all. Where one neighbor is high-entropy
(calm-presenting, σ\_o low per §10.5b.1) and the other is medium-entropy
(visibly distressed, σ\_o high) and the two are coupled through an
extraction relationship (§8.6a) with each other, C\_k can read the calm
presentation as evidence of health rather than concealment, and the
visible distress as evidence of being the problem rather than the target
--- the same misreading Definition 10.5b.17.2 identifies, now committed
by the party extending help rather than the party confused about its own
neighbors.

Where C\_k extends anchoring to the high-entropy neighbor on this basis,
the anchoring functions as Proposition 10.5b.17.1 already describes: a
resource transfer that relaxes the extractor's own constraints (§7.3,
§10.3) and widens its practical reach (§8.3, §8.5). The distinction from
Proposition 10.5b.17.1 is the source of the transferred resource ---
here it is C\_k's own healthy standing, not the confused target's, and
C\_k was not previously a party to the extraction relationship it has
just subsidized.

\hypertarget{b.17.4-cascade-consequence}{%
\subsection{10.5b.17.4 Cascade
Consequence}\label{b.17.4-cascade-consequence}}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.17.2 (Cascade Consequence of Misdirected
Anchoring).}}

Where the anchored high-entropy neighbor subsequently treats C\_k's
support per §8.6a's extraction dynamics rather than as genuine
reciprocal coupling --- the same instrumental use of an apparent partner
already established in §10.5b.18.1's cover-and-lure mechanism, now
directed outward at C\_k rather than inward at the extractor's own
official layer --- C\_k's own resource balance (§7.4) and divergence
pressure begin to deteriorate along the branching trajectory §10.5b.2
describes for any container under sustained extraction. A previously
low-entropy C\_k can begin oscillating into the medium-entropy regime
itself as a direct consequence of the support it extended. Should C\_k's
own oscillation subsequently strike its own neighbors (§8.8a's cascade
sequence), the failure propagates a second time, grid-wide, originating
from what was, at the outset, a genuine attempt at healthy support
rather than any fault of C\_k's own internal dynamics.

\hypertarget{b.18-the-firewall-as-active-modulation-captive-official-layers-and-metamessenger-composition}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18 The Firewall as Active Modulation: Captive Official
Layers and Metamessenger
Composition}\label{b.18-the-firewall-as-active-modulation-captive-official-layers-and-metamessenger-composition}}

§10.5b.2 describes how a shock branches a container into the
high-entropy regime; Axiom C1 states that sustaining false-signal
convergence requires ongoing forcing. This subsection makes explicit
what supplies that forcing, day to day, once a container has branched,
and in doing so completes both accounts: the meta-representation layer
(§6.1) is not, in general, a passive boundary between official and
operational reality. It is an active population of sub-processes whose
collective loyalty determines whether the boundary functions as a
firewall at all.

\textbf{Captive Official Layer --- Mechanism.}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.1 (Captive Official Layer).}}

The official representation P\_o (§6.1) can be sincere --- genuinely
produced by its own local processing --- while remaining radically
incomplete at the system level, because O\_o's access to the operational
layer's actual dynamics is itself gated by the firewall described below,
not because P\_o's own processing is dishonest. A captive official layer
is therefore not a lie; it is an accurate report of an artificially
restricted input.

That restriction is not passive. The operational layer, in this regime,
uses the official layer instrumentally --- as cover for what operational
is actually doing, and as a lure, presenting a healthy front that
attracts coupling from other systems (characteristically medium-entropy
ones, §10.5b.11b) which operational then extracts from and onto which it
displaces its own entropy. Neither function requires operational to
represent this to itself as intentional; it is the structural role the
arrangement plays, not a claim about deliberation.

Sustaining that role requires active maintenance. Let M\_o,t denote the
official layer's own memory state --- nominally a projection of the true
historical trajectory, M\_o,t = O\_o(H\_t), consistent with how P\_o =
O\_o(P) projects the current state (§6.1, §9.2). A permeability event,
whether internal (a transient rise in Π\_f(t)) or external (transparent
feedback crossing a healthy coupling, φ\_fb(i,j,t), Part 10 Addendum VII
§10.5b.18.9), can produce a momentary glimpse of operational reality
that would, left alone, move M\_o,t incrementally toward truth. Where
the closed population's composition is entropic-aligned (the 1 −
Ψ\_loyal(t) fraction, §10.5b.18.12), that glimpse is instead met with
substitution: M\_o,t is overwritten with a fabricated segment,
M\_o,t\^{}false ≠ O\_o(H\_t), constructed to explain the glimpse away as
noise, error, or isolated anomaly rather than integrate it as signal.
This is why official's characteristic stability in this regime (low
σ\_o, §10.5b.1) should be read as maintained rather than merely low:
absent continuous rewriting, each permeability event would leave a small
residue, and σ\_o would drift upward over time even with the container
never leaving the high-entropy regime by any other measure.

This gives Ψ\_loyal(t) (§10.5b.18.12) a regime-specific behavioral
signature it did not previously have. Within the closed population here,
negentropic-aligned metamessengers (Ψ\_loyal(t)) hold the firewall by
pacing disclosure --- allowing small, tolerable increments of truth
through rather than none, specifically to avoid a sudden flood the
system cannot survive, the same shock-avoidance logic that governs the
medium-entropy regime's caution (§10.5b.18.7, below). Entropic-aligned
metamessengers (1 − Ψ\_loyal(t)) instead actively falsify via
M\_o,t\^{}false substitution, because their own function --- sustaining
cover and lure --- depends on official remaining not merely uninformed
but actively misinformed. Pacing and falsifying are behaviorally
distinguishable in principle (the former lets small true increments
accumulate over time; the latter overwrites them as fast as they arrive)
even though both present, at a single timepoint, as low
ω\_meta\^{}up(t).

\emph{\textbf{The channel runs both directions, and the return direction
is the actual point.}} Pacing disclosure is not an end in itself ---
accumulating paced truth is the prerequisite for official regaining
something it lost along with its ability to update: the capacity to act.
Official was never merely cut off from information; it was cut off from
exercising any influence over operational, precisely because it had
nothing current to act on. As Ψ\_loyal(t) carries paced increments of
true operational content upward, official does not merely come to know
more --- each increment restores a proportional measure of official's
actual operational agency, before any full realignment is reached. This
is graduated, not threshold-gated: even a small increase in permeability
yields some corresponding increase in official's capacity to influence
operational, since partial awareness is still awareness official can act
on. The same Ψ\_loyal population that carries truth upward, paced, is
the concrete carrier of official's regained influence back downward once
official has enough of the picture to exercise it --- one population,
one channel, running in both directions, not two separate mechanisms.

\emph{\textbf{At extreme capture, substitution becomes generation, not
merely concealment.}} Beyond a fitted capture threshold (large
κ\_capture(j), §10.5b.2.2), M\_o,t\^{}false substitution stops
functioning only as explaining-away and begins actively generating new
justificatory content --- a constructed narrative framework, not just a
patched-over anomaly. Two consequences follow, and neither is available
to ordinary, lower-degree captivity. First, this generated content can
extend to sanctioning harm directed at other containers within the same
immediate coupled network, framed by the captured official layer as
legitimate or even righteous, rather than the captivity remaining
confined to managing what official believes about its own container's
operational reality. Second, sustained generation at this degree can
remove concealment motive entirely: where the generated narrative frames
operational reality as already legitimate rather than as something
requiring concealment, M\_decep,ext(t) (§10.5b.18.4) can approach zero
even while D\_op(t) remains extreme, since there is no longer anything,
from inside the captured official layer's own generated account, that
requires hiding.

\emph{\textbf{A third metamessenger role: upstream source, not
downstream concealment.}} §6.1's construction of P\_m as an emergent
aggregate --- P\_m(t) = Σ\_k φ\_k(t)·P\_m\^{}(k)(t) --- has so far been
treated as purely diagnostic: the population reads official and
operational and aggregates into a meta-representation, but does not
generate content that feeds back into official or operational's own
updating. A sub-population of entropic-aligned, self-accurate
metamessengers can occupy a structurally different role: rather than
concealing their own entropic status within a delusion that arose some
other way, they actively manufacture and inject ``you are negentropic''
into both official and operational simultaneously, which is what
produces sincere, mutual alignment (Type B, §10.5b.1.2) rather than
alignment arising independently in each layer. Formally, this population
contributes an injection term entering the ℱ/Φ dynamics directly, an
internal analogue of the external forcing terms already built (F\_conf,
F\_demand):

Inject(k → \{o,p\}, t), a forcing term sourced from within the
container's own metamessenger population rather than from external
coupling, pushing both P\_o and P\_p toward a shared false self-model.

This reverses P\_m's previously assumed direction for this specific
sub-population: generative, not merely diagnostic. Nothing about P\_m's
role for the rest of the population changes; this is a role a specific
sub-population can occupy, not a redefinition of what P\_m is in
general.

\emph{Note.} Definition 10.5b.18.2 (Metamessenger Composition) is stated
in the spine, §6.1.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.3 (Collusion Failure).}}

Where the comfort-aligned majority does not merely withhold operational
reality but actively coordinates with the operational layer to falsify
P\_m's own content --- rather than merely gating what reaches P\_o ---
the independence requirement of Definition 6.1 fails from within rather
than through external corruption or simple omission. Definition 6.1
(Part 6) should be read as describing the baseline-regime case; this
subsection supplies its regime-dependent completion for the two
non-baseline regimes, where ω\_meta(t) \textless{} 1 makes true
independence the exception rather than the rule.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.4 (Self-Deception Maintenance Cost).}}

Sustaining a captive official layer against a genuinely corrupted
operational reality is not free, and the cost is not uniform: part of it
is owed regardless of whether the container currently has any external
target, and part of it exists only because a target is currently being
held or courted. Define:

\emph{M}\_decep(t) = M\_decep,int(t) + M\_decep,ext(t),

\emph{M}\_decep,int(t) = κ\_decep · (1 − ω\_meta(t)) · D\_op(t),
κ\_decep \textgreater{} 0 fitted --- the internal component, unchanged
from this document's original single-term formula: suppressing the
container's own official layer from learning the truth about its own
operational history. This persists regardless of whether the container
has any active coupling at all, since it is about the container's own
internal composition, not about any external party.

\emph{M}\_decep,ext(t) = κ\_decep,ext · Σ\_j Λ\_ij(t)·𝟙{[}j is a current
extraction target{]}, κ\_decep,ext \textgreater{} 0 fitted --- the
external component: maintaining a healthy-looking front specifically to
attract or hold whatever targets are currently coupled or being actively
courted (§8.6a.8). This is zero by construction whenever i has no active
extraction targets, which is exactly the condition dormancy (§8.6a.6)
requires.

Both components enter §7.4's balance equation as an additional resource
draw, distinct from and additional to M\_maint(C,t) (§7.5) --- routine
structural upkeep is not the same cost as active concealment, and the
two should be tracked separately even though both draw on R(t).
M\_decep,int(t) grows with both how divergent the container already is
and how comfort-dominated its metamessenger population currently is, so
a container sustaining a large, comfortable lie pays proportionally more
for it, continuously, whether or not that cost is ever visible
externally. Consistent with §7.9's debt convention, unpaid M\_decep(t)
accrues directly into δR(t): the container is borrowing against its own
future resource capacity to keep the present story intact, and --- per
§7.9 --- that debt does not resolve itself; it compounds until
addressed.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.4a (Preference Falsification Onset).}}

Define t\_pf, the preference falsification onset, as the first time
M\_decep,int(t) transitions from 0 to strictly positive --- i.e., the
first moment the container begins actively paying to suppress its own
official layer's access to operational truth, rather than simply
carrying an as-yet-unclosed gap. Before t\_pf, any D\_op(t)
\textgreater{} 0 present is attributable to ordinary lag or incomplete
information; from t\_pf onward, some part of D\_op(t) is being actively
maintained.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.4a.1 (The Onset Is Simultaneously
Hardest Commitment and Cheapest Reversal).}}

t\_pf marks two things at once, and this is the substantive claim, not
merely a definition: it is the point of steepest subsequent commitment
to closure, since active maintenance (M\_decep,int \textgreater{} 0)
itself requires and reinforces the comfort-dominated metamessenger
composition (ω\_meta(t) low, Definition 10.5b.18.2) that sustains it
going forward; and it is simultaneously the cheapest point at which
reversal is available, since accrued self-deception debt (§10.5b.18.4's
accrual into δR(t)) is, by construction, zero or minimal exactly at
t\_pf and grows monotonically for as long as suppression continues. This
yields a real, falsifiable prediction distinct from anything a
continuous D\_op(t) measure alone implies: intervention cost to reverse
a container's trajectory should be measurably lowest in the window
immediately surrounding t\_pf and should rise thereafter, tracking
accumulated M\_decep(t) debt rather than tracking D\_op(t)'s own level,
which can remain roughly constant across the window while the cost to
correct it is not. Where an adapter can supply a real signal for t\_pf
directly (a documented policy change, a specific concealment event),
that signal should be used over inferring the transition from
M\_decep,int(t) alone, consistent with this document's general
preference for direct attestation over statistical inference wherever
both are available.

\hypertarget{b.18.4b-falsification-strain}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.4b Falsification
Strain}\label{b.18.4b-falsification-strain}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.4b (Falsification Strain).}} Define
CD(t) ≥ 0 as an accumulated strain state, driven jointly by active
preference falsification and raw messaging divergence, each contributing
independently rather than one gating the other:

dCD/dt = κ\_pf·M\_decep,int(t) + κ\_md·(D\_op(t) + D\_om(t) + D\_pm(t))
− κ\_relief·CD(t), κ\_pf, κ\_md, κ\_relief \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

where M\_decep,int(t) is the active self-deception maintenance draw
already defined at §10.5b.18.4, and the relief term allows CD(t) to
subside when neither source is currently active --- falsification strain
is a real, accumulating state, not a permanent scar, though §7.9's debt
convention still applies to whatever of it goes unaddressed long enough
to compound into structural cost rather than resolve.

\emph{Domain instances.} In psychological systems this strain is
cognitive dissonance (Festinger 1957); in organizations, the strain of
maintaining two sets of books against an auditor's questions; in
materials, residual stress held in a body under conflicting constraints,
which accumulates and relaxes in the same way.

\hypertarget{b.18.4c-correction-decoupling-load}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.4c Correction Decoupling
Load}\label{b.18.4c-correction-decoupling-load}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.4c (Correction Decoupling Load).}}
Define DL(t) ≥ 0, driven directly by accumulated falsification strain
together with structural fragmentation, consistent with the original
three-input FDFM formulation while now expressed in RICD's own
established quantities (s(t)'s fragmentation, §8.3, standing in for the
original S; CD(t) above absorbing the original M and F pathways already
routed through it):

dDL/dt = κ\_dl·CD(t)·(1 + κ\_frag·s\_frag(t)) − κ\_dl,decay·DL(t),
κ\_dl, κ\_frag, κ\_dl,decay \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

recovering a genuine state variable with memory and decay, not merely an
instantaneous function of current inputs. The original FDFM
computed-output form remains available wherever an adapter needs a
purely observational proxy rather than the fitted dynamical version
above:

DL\^{}obs(t) = σ(α·s\_frag(t) + β·(D\_op(t)+D\_om(t)+D\_pm(t)) −
γ·Π\_f(t)),

a direct descendant of the original D = g(S, M, F), with feedback
permeability entering negatively for the same reason it did originally
--- a container that can still hear correction decouples less, all else
equal. Both forms are legitimate; DL(t) is the recommended default where
a real trajectory with memory is wanted, DL\^{}obs(t) where only a
same-instant observational estimate is available or required.

\emph{Domain instances.} In psychological systems the corresponding
phenomenon is dissociation; in control systems, sensor decoupling, in
which readings continue to be produced but stop entering the control
loop; in organizations, management losing contact with its own
operations, so that reports arrive and are no longer acted on.

\hypertarget{b.18.5-downward-channel-demand-permeability}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.5 Downward Channel: Demand
Permeability}\label{b.18.5-downward-channel-demand-permeability}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.5 (Demand Permeability).}}

Define ω\_meta\^{}down(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the resource-weighted fraction
of the same metamessenger population of Definition 10.5b.18.2, now
measured along its second function: the fraction currently transmitting
the official layer's expectations downward into what the operational
layer treats as its own governing target (demand-aligned), with
1−ω\_meta\^{}down(t) buffering operational dynamics from official
pressure (buffering). This is a distinct function from ω\_meta\^{}up(t),
not its complement --- a metamessenger can transmit truth upward,
transmit demand downward, both, or neither, so ω\_meta\^{}up(t) and
ω\_meta\^{}down(t) are measured independently rather than derived from
one another.

Regime dependence:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Low-entropy baseline}: ω\_meta\^{}down(t) ≈ ω\_meta\^{}up(t) ≈
  1. Since official and operational layers are already close (§10.5b.1),
  downward transmission carries nothing corrupt to impose; it is the
  same ordinary bidirectional reporting described for the upward channel
  in Definition 10.5b.18.2.
\item
  \textbf{High-entropy regime}: the regime-defining case bifurcates on
  ω\_meta\^{}down(t) specifically, not merely on ω\_meta\^{}up(t) being
  low. Where ω\_meta\^{}down(t) is also low, operational dynamics are
  insulated from official pressure and the corrupted operational reality
  is self-sustaining on its own terms --- the ordinary firewalled case
  §10.5b.18.2 already described. Where ω\_meta\^{}down(t) is high while
  ω\_meta\^{}up(t) remains low, official demand actively reaches
  operational behavior and reinforces it, even though operational truth
  cannot reach official awareness in return. This second configuration
  is not distinguished from the first by §10.5b.18.1--18.4 alone, and is
  addressed directly by Definition 10.5b.18.6 below.
\item
  \textbf{Medium-entropy regime}: here the official layer is the
  compromised one (§10.5b.1), so a high ω\_meta\^{}down(t) is harmful in
  the mirrored sense --- pressuring an operational layer that is
  actually trustworthy to distort itself toward a compromised official
  narrative, working against the D\_pm → 0 convergence Definition
  10.5b.18.2's editorial note already identifies as this regime's
  healthy direction.
\end{itemize}

\hypertarget{b.18.6-the-valve-symmetry-index}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.6 The Valve Symmetry
Index}\label{b.18.6-the-valve-symmetry-index}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.6 (Valve Symmetry Index).}}

σ\_valve(t) = min(ω\_meta\^{}up(t), ω\_meta\^{}down(t)) /
max(ω\_meta\^{}up(t), ω\_meta\^{}down(t)),

with the convention σ\_valve(t) = 1 when ω\_meta\^{}up(t) =
ω\_meta\^{}down(t) = 0. σ\_valve(t) = 1 indicates perfect symmetry
between the two transmission rates, whether both are open or both are
closed --- symmetry alone does not imply permeability, and ``equally
closed'' and ``thin, permeable'' are not the same claim: a completely
closed pair is maximally symmetric while being minimally permeable.
Absolute openness is supplied separately by ω\_meta\^{}up(t) and
ω\_meta\^{}down(t) themselves; σ\_valve(t) itself is a pure symmetry
measure, full stop --- it does not describe openness at all, and should
not be read as conditionally describing the honest configuration only in
some cases. Three distinct terms name three distinct things: σ\_valve(t)
is symmetry; ω\_meta\^{}up(t) and ω\_meta\^{}down(t) are openness in
each direction; and if a single combined quantity for bidirectional
effective openness is ever needed, it would be a separate construction
(e.g., their product or minimum), not σ\_valve(t) itself. The honest,
thin, bidirectionally-symmetric configuration is the case where
σ\_valve(t) = 1 and both channels are also genuinely open --- a joint
condition across two independent quantities, not something σ\_valve(t)
alone ever describes. σ\_valve(t) → 0 with ω\_meta\^{}down(t) ≫
ω\_meta\^{}up(t) indicates the one-way valve: official narrative
pressure reaches operational behavior largely unimpeded while
operational truth is blocked from reaching official awareness in return.
This is the formal content of the bidirectional-valve concept: not that
transmission occurs in both directions, but the degree to which the
extent of transmission is symmetric between them, tracked as its own
quantity rather than inferred from ω\_meta\^{}up(t) alone.

\hypertarget{b.18.7-the-buried-operational-layer}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.7 The Buried Operational
Layer}\label{b.18.7-the-buried-operational-layer}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.7 (Buried Operational Layer).}}

In the medium-entropy (official-exposed) regime --- σ\_o high, σ\_p low,
§10.5b.1 --- the operational layer is negentropic and holds full systems
access; nothing restricts what it can do, and nothing in this regime's
mechanics reaches in to alter its actual state. It is not captive. What
happens to it instead is that its reality is buried: obscured from
outside observers, and from the system's own official self-account,
beneath the volume of official's own chaotic signal. Operational's
functioning is untouched underneath that noise --- this is a visibility
problem, not a corruption or coercion problem, and the two should not be
conflated even though both can present, superficially, as ``the
operational reality is hard to see.''

This burial is not symmetric with high-entropy's captivity (§10.5b.18.1,
§10.5b.18.1), and the asymmetry is structural rather than a matter of
degree. The entropic official layer in medium-entropy has no route into
operational comparable to operational's false-memory-operator route into
official in high-entropy (§10.5b.18.1's M\_o,t\^{}false mechanism).
Official's own chaos --- however severe --- does not and cannot rewrite
operational's memory, coerce operational's behavior, or otherwise reach
past the boundary between them under this regime's own mechanics. Where
official-entropy in a medium-entropy container does deepen over time,
this document already accounts for it as arising from outside that
boundary: repeated lateral knocks, or sustained coupling with a
high-entropy neighbor exerting pressure in that direction (§8.6a,
§10.5b.11b) --- not from the official layer's own chaos somehow
acquiring, on its own, the systems access it would need to act on
operational directly. A medium-entropy container's official layer, left
alone, does not have the access required to turn its own operational
layer entropic; something external has to supply that access, or that
pressure, from outside the pair.

This is not new machinery grafted onto the document; it is already
implicit in the σ\_o/σ\_p classification and the caution already given
in §15.2. The warning that low apparent volatility in the
cheaply-observed official channel is not a stability proxy, and that a
medium-entropy container's actual stability is visible only by also
pulling O\_p, is precisely the burial this definition now names
formally: an adapter reading only official's noisy signal is not looking
at a corrupted operational reality, it is failing to look past
official's noise to the intact operational reality underneath.

\hypertarget{b.18.8-demand-forcing}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.8 Demand Forcing}\label{b.18.8-demand-forcing}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.8 (Demand Forcing).}}

The downward channel's mechanical effect on §6.4's landscape dynamics:

F\_demand(t) = κ\_demand · ω\_meta\^{}down(t) · D\_op(t), κ\_demand
\textgreater{} 0 fitted,

entering §6.4's stochastic gradient flow as an additional forcing term
alongside F\_conf (§10.5b.3) and F\_boom (§10.5b.16.1), as additive
drift, not a literal modification to Φ\_t's own three-term formula, per
the same distinction already established elsewhere in this document ---
holding the trajectory near the official representation P\_o and pulling
P toward conformity with official expectation independent of whether
P\_o itself tracks the container's true underlying signal. This is the
mechanism by which official narrative pressure produces operational
behavior change --- teaching to a metric, gaming a target --- without
requiring extraction (§8.6a) or confrontation-avoidance (§10.5b.3) to
explain it: the pull originates in the downward channel itself, sourced
from Definition 10.5b.18.5. Per Axiom C1, sustaining P near this valley
requires F\_demand(t) to remain active; withdrawing official pressure
(ω\_meta\^{}down(t) → 0) or closing the underlying gap (D\_op(t) → 0,
official expectation itself converging with truth) lets Φ relax back
toward the true-signal minimum, exactly as with F\_conf and F\_boom.

\hypertarget{b.18.9-external-feedback-channel}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.9 External Feedback
Channel}\label{b.18.9-external-feedback-channel}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.9 (External Feedback Channel).}}

For container i coupled to neighbor j via Λ\_ij(t) (§8.5), define
φ\_fb(i,j,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the fraction of j's own honestly-measured
divergence state that crosses the coupling to i as transparent feedback,
rather than being filtered by j's own metamessage layer before
transmission ever reaches the boundary. This is bounded by the
neighbor's own internal honesty: φ\_fb(i,j,t) ≤ ω\_meta\^{}up(j,t) --- a
neighbor cannot transmit externally, with any reliability, more truth
than it is already transmitting to its own official layer internally. A
high-entropy (official-shielded) neighbor, with ω\_meta\^{}up(j,t)
characteristically low (§10.5b.18.2), is therefore structurally a poor
source of external feedback regardless of coupling strength Λ\_ij; the
ceiling comes from the neighbor's own condition, not from the coupling
itself. This bounds how much crosses the coupling; it does not by itself
bound whether what crosses is accurate, which Definition 10.5b.18.9.1
addresses separately.

\hypertarget{b.18.9.1-feedback-accuracy}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.9.1 Feedback
Accuracy}\label{b.18.9.1-feedback-accuracy}}

\emph{Note.} Definition 10.5b.18.9.1 (Feedback Accuracy), which defines
A\_acc and κ\_access, is stated in the spine, §6.3.2. Its use in paced
delivery follows here.

\emph{\textbf{Gentle delivery.}} Accuracy is a floor, not a dial;
delivery rate is the dial. Feedback that crosses this accuracy floor
should still respect §10.5b.14's existing pacing logic --- remaining
slow enough to avoid crossing whatever threshold would trigger F\_conf
(§10.5b.3) --- rather than delivering accurate content at whatever rate
is available. This is the same restraint already governing negentropic
disclosure generally, applied specifically to feedback delivered across
a coupling: accurate content, paced to minimize shock, not diluted
content delivered comfortably.

\hypertarget{b.18.10-external-support-flux}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.10 External Support
Flux}\label{b.18.10-external-support-flux}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.10 (External Support Flux).}}

Define Γ\_supp(i,j,t) = κ\_Γ · Λ\_ij(t) · max(0, R\_j(t)/R\_j\^{}max −
r\_supp,min), κ\_Γ \textgreater{} 0 fitted, r\_supp,min a fitted surplus
threshold. This is distinct from ordinary boundary exchange E\_exch(t)
(§7.4): it isolates the fraction of resource flow attributable
specifically to neighbor j having surplus beyond its own operating
requirement, rather than routine exchange that carries no stabilizing
content for i. A neighbor with R\_j(t)/R\_j\^{}max below r\_supp,min
contributes nothing here regardless of Λ\_ij(t) --- a resource-strapped
neighbor, healthy or not, cannot supply support it does not have.

\hypertarget{b.18.11-coupling-correction-forcing}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.11 Coupling Correction
Forcing}\label{b.18.11-coupling-correction-forcing}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.11 (Coupling Correction Forcing).}}

The two external inputs above correct D\_om(i,t) from opposite edges of
its healthy target band (0, ε\_healthy{]} (§10.5b.1):

F\_ext,fb(i,t) = κ\_fb · {[}Σ\_j Λ\_ij(t)·φ\_fb(i,j,t)·A\_acc(j,i,t){]}
· (D\_om,floor − D\_om(i,t))₊

F\_ext,supp(i,t) = κ\_supp · {[}Σ\_j Γ\_supp(i,j,t){]} · (D\_om(i,t) −
ε\_healthy)₊

where (x)₊ = max(x,0) and D\_om,floor is a small fitted lower reference
distinct from ε\_healthy, marking the point below which D\_om is read as
approaching unchecked confidence rather than merely healthy. F\_ext,fb
activates only when D\_om(i,t) is at risk of collapsing toward zero ---
external transparent feedback countering epistemic arrogance, now
weighted by A\_acc(j,i,t) (§10.5b.18.9.1) so honest-but-inaccurate
feedback contributes little to this restorative pull regardless of how
much of it crosses the coupling --- and F\_ext,supp activates only when
D\_om(i,t) exceeds ε\_healthy --- external negentropic support
countering excess self-attributed dysfunction. Unlike F\_conf, F\_boom,
and F\_demand (§10.5b.3, §10.5b.16.1, §10.5b.18.8), which open valleys
pulling P away from the true-signal minimum, these two terms are
restorative: they add to, rather than oppose, the relaxation Axiom C1
already describes once a distorting force is withdrawn. A container with
no coupled neighbors, or only high-entropy neighbors supplying neither
honest feedback nor resource surplus, has F\_ext,fb(i,t) =
F\_ext,supp(i,t) = 0 and must hold its own D\_om inside the band using
internal channels alone.

\hypertarget{b.18.12-metamessenger-closure-orientation-composition}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.12 Metamessenger Closure-Orientation
Composition}\label{b.18.12-metamessenger-closure-orientation-composition}}

\emph{Note. Definition 10.5b.18.12 (Closure-Orientation Composition)} is
stated in the spine, §6.1; its estimation requirement and corroborated
refinement follow here.

Estimation/validation separation, required. Ψ\_loyal(t)'s
trailing-window construction already restricts its own estimate to data
available before t; this does not by itself prevent a separate problem
downstream. Wherever Ψ\_loyal(t), or any quantity computed from it
(persecution-weighted ρ(j) via §8.6a.1, loyalty-conditioned outcomes via
§10.5b.18.13, Ψ\_total(t)), is used to generate a testable prediction,
the data window used to estimate Ψ\_loyal(t) and the data window used to
evaluate that prediction must not overlap. Testing a prediction against
the same trajectory segment that built the estimate it depends on risks
