partial self-confirmation rather than a genuine out-of-sample test. This
is a required methodological condition for any empirical application of
this document's loyalty-dependent predictions, not an optional
precaution. Manufactured-slope detection. The trailing-slope proxy above
is directly gameable: a high-entropy container aware it is being
assessed can permit a small, cosmetic rise in ω\_meta\^{}up(t) ---
tolerating a token amount of transmitted truth that costs it nothing
real --- specifically to register as rising Ψ\_loyal(t) without any
genuine shift in orientation underneath it. This is a distinct, more
severe problem than the conservative-default understatement already
flagged above: that risk is a proxy reading too low for a genuinely
loyal population; this risk is a proxy reading too high for a genuinely
disloyal one, actively manufactured rather than merely defaulted into.
Definition 10.5b.18.12b (Corroborated Loyalty Slope): a rising
ω\_meta\^{}up(t) trend should not be credited toward Ψ\_loyal(t) on its
own; it should be cross-checked against a harder-to-fake,
independently-tracked quantity over the same window ---
M\_corrosion(i,t) (§10.5b.19.7) or D\_op(i,t)'s own trajectory, both
already computed elsewhere in this document for other purposes. Define
Corrob(t) = 1 if Δω\_meta\^{}up(t; w\_loyal) \textgreater{} 0 AND
ΔM\_corrosion(t; w\_loyal) \textless{} 0 (or ΔD\_op(t; w\_loyal)
\textless{} 0, whichever the adapter tracks); 0 otherwise, and use the
corroborated form in place of the raw proxy wherever direct measurement
is unavailable: Ψ\_loyal\^{}corrob(t) = Ψ\_loyal(t) · Corrob(t), equal
to zero whenever the corroborating signal is absent, regardless of how
strongly the raw ω\_meta\^{}up slope alone would suggest openness. The
reasoning this formalizes directly: a genuine shift in orientation costs
the container something real --- reduced extraction, reduced
self-corrosion, reduced operational divergence --- because
loyalty-driven transmission is loyalty specifically because it lets
truth reach official at the expense of sustaining the entropic layer's
own comfort. A slope manufactured purely for appearance carries no such
cost, and should show ω\_meta\^{}up(t) rising in isolation while
M\_corrosion(t) and D\_op(t) remain flat or continue worsening --- a
real, falsifiable prediction distinct from anything the raw
trailing-slope proxy alone produces. Where an adapter can supply a
genuine, direct instrument this corroboration step is unnecessary, per
the existing preference for direct measurement over any proxy; it exists
specifically to protect the trailing-slope default, which is the version
most exposed to manufacture.

\hypertarget{b.18.13-loyalty-conditioned-convergence}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.13 Loyalty-Conditioned
Convergence}\label{b.18.13-loyalty-conditioned-convergence}}

\emph{Note. Definition 10.5b.18.13 (Loyalty-Conditioned Convergence) is
stated in the spine, §6.1, for the medium-entropy case. Its high-entropy
case uses the structural hazard rate Ω\_hazard of Definition 10.5b.0.4
and follows here.}

\emph{High-entropy (official-shielded, post-collapse resilience).} Part
14 already flags Ω\_hazard(t)'s survival-function form as open beyond a
monotonicity relationship. Pending that fuller treatment, the following
modifier, consistent with the monotonicity already established,
conditions it on closure orientation:

Ω\_hazard\^{}eff(t) = Ω\_hazard(t) · (1 − κ\_ΩL · Ψ\_loyal(t)), κ\_ΩL ∈
(0,1) fitted,

used wherever Ω\_hazard(t) governs post-collapse outcomes. A
high-loyalty closed population reduces effective structural hazard
following collapse --- the formal content of ``predicts possibility of
self-reset'' --- because the same messengers whose closure protected the
container pre-collapse begin releasing operational truth once the
collapse has already occurred and the shock they were withholding
against has, in effect, already happened. A low-loyalty population
leaves Ω\_hazard\^{}eff(t) ≈ Ω\_hazard(t), consistent with ``predicts
inability to recover of its own accord.''

\hypertarget{b.18.14-total-convergence-favorability}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.14 Total
Convergence-Favorability}\label{b.18.14-total-convergence-favorability}}

\emph{Note.} Definition 10.5b.18.14 (Total Convergence-Favorability) is
stated in the spine, §6.1.

\hypertarget{b.18.15-shared-shock-avoidance-restraint}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.15 Shared Shock-Avoidance
Restraint}\label{b.18.15-shared-shock-avoidance-restraint}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.15 (Shared Shock-Avoidance
Restraint).}}

The following restraint logic is stated once and instantiated by both
non-baseline regimes, rather than derived separately for each, since
both are the same underlying caution applied to opposite arrangements:

\emph{A negentropic-oriented party with the standing to force
integration withholds from doing so, in full or in part, while its
confidence that integration would resolve favorably remains below a
threshold, because forcing it prematurely risks a shock the system
cannot survive intact.}

In high-entropy (§10.5b.18.1), this is instantiated as the
negentropic-aligned fraction of the closed metamessenger population
(Ψ\_loyal(t)) pacing disclosure rather than flooding it. In
medium-entropy, it is instantiated as the operational layer itself ---
confidence indexed by Ψ\_total(t) (§10.5b.18.14) rather than by a
closed-population fraction alone, since operational here is the party
with standing to act, not a population embedded within a layer it does
not control. The same restraint logic, differently instantiated, is why
this document should not treat high-entropy's pacing and
medium-entropy's withholding as separate phenomena needing separate
motivational accounts; they are one mechanism, and the regime determines
only who is exercising it and over what.

\hypertarget{b.18.16-voluntary-flood}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.16 Voluntary Flood}\label{b.18.16-voluntary-flood}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.16 (Voluntary Flood).}}

Restraint under Definition 10.5b.18.15 is conditional, not permanent.
Where Ψ\_total(t) ≥ θ\_flood (fitted) and the container is not already
within an active tipping window (M̃\_tip tracking per §10.5b.9 shows no
event in progress), the operational layer may self-trigger a
permeability flood: Π\_f(t) is driven toward its ceiling over a short
characteristic timescale τ\_flood (fitted), functioning mechanically as
a self-sourced analog of Drive\_B(j,t) (§10.5b.6, §8.8a) --- initiating
the same tipping-momentum accumulation and mass-weighted B1/B2
resolution already given in §10.5b.7--10.5b.9, using whatever
μ\_p\^{}eff(t) (§10.5b.18.13) obtains at the moment of triggering ---
except originating internally rather than from external lateral force,
and by the container's own choice rather than imposed on it.

This is mechanically distinct from both existing Flip Condition A
mechanisms it might otherwise be confused with. The pre-breach
self-rescue window (§10.5b.9b) and the aimed-discharge exception
(§10.5b.9c) are both reactive: they respond to a tip already
approaching. Voluntary flood is proactive, initiated from a calm state
with no tip in progress, on the strength of a favorability estimate
rather than in response to an event. Since the trigger condition
Ψ\_total(t) ≥ θ\_flood is itself constructed to predict favorable
resolution, a successfully-triggered voluntary flood should resolve
toward B2 (reorganization) at a substantially higher rate than
knock-driven events at comparable μ\_p\^{}eff(t), which do not benefit
from this selection --- a testable, falsifiable prediction
distinguishing deliberate from externally-forced realignment in outcome
data, not only in narrative account.

\hypertarget{b.18.17-the-despair-signature}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.17 The Despair
Signature}\label{b.18.17-the-despair-signature}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.17 (Despair Signature).}}

Official-convergence collapse in medium-entropy (D\_om → 0 during a
knock-driven tipping event, per this document's existing
convergence-direction account) carries a specific content: not a generic
realization that the system is dysfunctional, but the more corrosive
realization that its instability was never internally caused --- only
ever the product of repeated external knocking --- landing as despair
rather than relief, because it arrives without a route out. This content
is diagnosable, not merely narrative: it corresponds to
official-convergence co-occurring with Γ\_redir(t) ≈ 0 (§10.5b.11a) ---
no redirection structure available at the moment of collapse --- which
is precisely the combination under which realizing the true source of
one's instability changes nothing actionable about it. Where Γ\_redir(t)
is meaningfully above zero at the same moment, the same realization is
available as grounds for the container to seek or accept redirection
rather than despair, and this document's existing distinction between
resisting momentum (destructive) and redirecting it (survivable,
§10.5b.11a) already covers what happens next in that case. The despair
signature, so defined, requires no new formal variable --- it is a named
co-occurrence of two quantities this document already tracks, given a
name because the combination has a specific, identifiable experiential
content that a reader working from D\_om and Γ\_redir(t) as bare numbers
would not otherwise recognize.

\emph{Domain instances.} Root-cause analysis that locates a failure in
repeated external shocks rather than internal fault; a state recognizing
that its decline came from sanctions rather than from its own policy.

\hypertarget{b.18.18-control-locus}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.18 Control Locus}\label{b.18.18-control-locus}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.18 (Control Locus).}}

Define 𝒜(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the operational layer's share of functional
systems control at time t, with 1 − 𝒜(t) held by the official layer.
𝒜(t) = 1 is the default in both non-baseline regimes, consistent with
operational being the layer with actual systems access regardless of
which regime it is in; 𝒜(t) \textless{} 1 is a departure from that
default, not a redefinition of who has access, and can be partial rather
than all-or-nothing --- both layers can be genuinely involved in a given
decision, with operational holding the larger or smaller share.

Where 𝒜(t) \textless{} 1, define the delegation type δ(t) ∈ \{staged,
ceded\}, distinguishing two structurally different reasons control can
sit with official:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Staged} (characteristic of high-entropy, §10.5b.18.1):
  operational deliberately hands functional interface to official for
  tactical reasons --- most consequentially during an extended coupling
  attempt with a prospective extraction target (§8.6a, §10.5b.11b),
  where official-facing interaction serves the cover-and-lure function
  directly. Operational remains the authoring party throughout; the
  delegation itself, not only the system beneath it, is operational's
  choice.
\item
  \textbf{Ceded} (characteristic of medium-entropy): official's
  functional dominance arises from metamessenger convergence toward
  official messaging (D\_om → 0 tendency, short of a full tipping event)
  rather than from any operational decision. This is not staged and not
  reversible by operational simply choosing otherwise; it is what
  Override (§10.5b.18.20) below exists to correct.
\end{itemize}

10.5b.18.18a Narrative-Constraint Control, Distinct From Functional
Control

Definition 10.5b.18.18a (Narrative-Constraint Control). 𝒜(t) measures
functional, behavioral control only; official layer holds a real,
separate kind of power regardless of how low 𝒜(t) runs: leverage over
what the container as a whole believes about itself, functioning as a
genuine constraint on what operational is willing to do even when
operational holds complete functional authority to do otherwise. This
constraint is not decorative or a byproduct of the split --- it binds
operational's actual choices, at real, dual cost: a resource cost (the
Self-Deception Tax and self-interested-disinformation machinery already
established, §10.5b.14.1) and, more consequentially, a narrowing of
operational's genuinely available option space itself, closer to a
direct reduction in d\_A than to an ordinary resource drain. Both costs
are real and simultaneous, not alternative descriptions of the same
thing: maintaining a given official narrative both burns resources and
forecloses whichever specific actions that narrative cannot be stretched
to absorb.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18b (Narrative Constraint Shapes the Specific Form
of Entropic Action). A sharp, falsifiable prediction follows directly:
the specific form an entropic operational layers actions take should
track the specific content of whatever official rule that operational is
working around, rather than tracking operational's raw entropic drive
alone. Two containers with comparably severe operational entropy, but
different specific official self-rules, should produce mechanically
different specific behaviors --- not because their underlying entropic
drive differs, but because each operational core selects whichever
available method is cheapest to launder through its own particular
official architectures specific permitted categories. A container whose
official rule is "I would never do X" reliably avoids X specifically
while producing comparable severity through any available alternative; a
container whose official rule instead requires the appearance of Y
(e.g., the targets ongoing consent, however fabricated) reliably shapes
its method to preserve the appearance of Y rather than avoiding any
particular act. The prediction is falsifiable: given a container's
specific official self-narrative content in advance, the specific form
its entropic operational action takes, given that such action occurs,
should be predictable from that content, not merely from the fact of
entropy alone.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18c (Official-Narrative Maintenance Is
Priority-Ordered Ahead of Adaptive Work). §7's existing
resource-priority convention (routine maintenance funded first, adaptive
work clipped to remaining capacity) requires a further, real refinement
for a high-entropy container specifically: operational treats
official-narrative maintenance as effectively non-negotiable, funding it
ahead of adaptive work and potentially ahead of ordinary structural
maintenance itself, not as one cost competing on equal footing with
others for the same pool. This is not merely a claim that the cost can
grow large in the worst case; it is a claim about queue position: a
high-entropy container will let other real needs go underfunded
specifically to keep funding a narrative rewrite, because a narrative's
collapse is treated by operational as a more immediate threat than
neglect of other needs, up to and including choosing costly,
identity-altering life changes (a change of career, of credentialing, of
domicile) whose real function is sustaining a more elevated official
self-story rather than serving any adaptive purpose directly.

10.5b.18.18d Entropy Thresholds

Definition 10.5b.18.18d (Entropy Threshold). A containers specific
official narrative architecture (§10.5b.18.18a) implies not only the
shape of its entropic operational action (§10.5b.18.18b) but a real
ceiling on that action's severity: some point beyond which no available
rewrite could absorb the action into that particular official structure
without risking official's own outright collapse, which is precisely the
outcome operational most needs to prevent, since operational's entire
reason for funding official's maintenance is to keep it intact. The
threshold is not a goodness ceiling imposed from outside the system; it
is the point at which further escalation stops serving operational's own
self-narrative-preservation need and starts actively threatening it.
This explains, without requiring a separate account, why most
high-entropy containers do not cross into their most severe available
behaviors: each such container carries its own specific threshold, set
by its own specific official architecture, and ordinary daily behavior
--- which rules a given containers official narrative already permits or
refuses to permit --- is real, available predictive signal for where
that threshold sits, even though the threshold itself is typically not
directly observable before an actual approach to it.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18e (Threshold-Reserve Coupling, Asymmetric by
Regime). A containers entropy threshold is not fixed; it moves with
current resource reserve, for the same underlying reason §10.5b.0.13a's
Bracing Threshold is reserve-dependent rather than fixed --- but the
coupling strength differs sharply by regime, for a real, causal reason
rather than merely by observed degree. An entropic official narratives
specific limits are themselves actively-funded beliefs, costly fictions
requiring continuous maintenance investment to hold in place; as reserve
depletes, the most expensive beliefs to maintain are among the first a
container can no longer afford, and a threshold set by such a belief
drops correspondingly. A negentropic official narratives limits require
no comparable maintenance investment, since they are accurate rather
than fabricated --- "I will not do X" costs nothing ongoing to sustain
when it is simply true --- and depletion therefore has far less to
erode, since there was no costly fiction propping the line up in the
first place. This does not require a hard floor as its mechanism, only a
far shallower reserve-dependence slope: a negentropic threshold under
sufficiently extreme, sustained depletion is not claimed to be
categorically immovable, only far more resistant, requiring far more
punishing conditions to shift than an entropic threshold would need to
shift by comparison.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18f (Promise-Violation Outranks Raw Severity in
Threshold Hierarchy). Within a single container's own threshold
structure, violating an explicit prior commitment already extended sits
on a more severe rung than harming a target to whom no such commitment
was ever given, independent of which action is more severe by any raw
measure of harm caused. This produces a real, checkable prediction
distinct from the general Entropy Threshold concept: a container's own
after-the-fact relationship to an action --- carried forward honestly as
a genuine failure, versus immediately laundered via official-layer
rewrite into justification --- is itself diagnostic of which side of
that container's own threshold hierarchy the action actually fell on,
with promise-violations characteristically resisting easy laundering
(and being carried forward as genuine, unresolved wrongness) precisely
because they are harder for official to absorb than an equivalently
severe action that violated no prior commitment.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18g (Rewrite-Versus-Retention as the
Classification-Flip Test). A single entropic act by an
otherwise-negentropic operational core does not by itself reclassify a
container's regime. What determines whether such an act marks the
beginning of an entropic spiral, or remains an isolated failure within a
still-negentropic trajectory, is whether official subsequently rewrites
to launder and excuse it (the entropic mechanism activating) or whether
the container instead retains the accurate, uncomfortable record of what
actually happened (negentropic integrity surviving a genuine failure
intact). This is the operative test for classification following any
single severe act, not the acts severity in isolation.

Definition 10.5b.18.18k (The Identity-Justification Reclassification
Test, Sharpening 10.5b.18.18g). Repetition of an entropic act, even many
times, does not itself reclassify a container -\/- what determines
genuine reclassification is not count or severity but whether the
container builds a standing, identity-level justification around the
pattern, of the form "this is what I do because of what I am," as
opposed to absorbing each instance with genuine acknowledgment and
resolve even when the pattern recurs despite that resolve. A container
that repeats an entropic act while still treating each instance as a
failure against its own genuine standard remains within 10.5b.18.18g's
"isolated failure" case, however many times it recurs; a container that
instead anchors the pattern to a fixed self-concept has crossed into
genuine reclassification, since the justification itself is now doing
the entropic work of protecting the pattern from correction, not merely
explaining it after the fact.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18l (Symmetric Valence of the Justifying
Self-Concept). The mechanism in 10.5b.18.18k operates identically
regardless of whether the anchoring self-concept is falsely negative or
falsely positive, with the same identity-justification structure
producing reclassification either way. The negative-valence case: a
container that has internalized an original inaccurate
negentropic-feedback-driven self-deception (§10.5b.9e.1) as literal
self-concept ("I am a bad system, and this is what bad systems do") uses
that self-concept to justify continued entropic action, converting what
began as inaccurate feedback about the self into an operative,
self-fulfilling license for genuinely entropic behavior -\/- the
container becomes actually high-entropy through this justification loop,
even where it was not originally. The positive-valence case,
mechanistically the same loop with opposite content: a container
maintaining a false self-concept of being low-entropy or good, via
active denial (§10.5b.18.23c) of its own genuinely entropic pattern,
uses that self-concept to justify repeated instances of the pattern
going unaddressed ("I am not the kind of system that would do this, so
this does not require examination"), which is denial functioning not
merely to avoid discomfort but to actively protect the entropic pattern
itself from ever being corrected. Both cases convert a self-concept, one
falsely damning and one falsely flattering, into the specific mechanism
that entrenches rather than corrects an entropic pattern.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18m (Frozen Non-Response to a Coupled Neighbor, a
Worked Positive-Valence Instance). A concrete instance of 10.5b.18.18l's
positive-valence case: a container repeatedly declining to respond to a
coupled neighbors genuine, communicated danger, specifically to preserve
a self-concept of being a low-entropy, reliable member of its own local
grid. Each individual instance of non-response could, per 10.5b.18.18k,
remain an isolated failure if met with genuine acknowledgment;
reclassification occurs specifically when non-response instead becomes
justified by appeal to the self-concept being protected ("acknowledging
this would mean I am not who I believe myself to be, so it will not be
acknowledged"), at which point the container's operational layer has
moved from 10.5b.18.23e's reversible collusion into the standing,
identity-anchored pattern this proposition describes -\/- silence in
this case functions as 10.5b.18.23d's sub-threshold avoidance, deployed
repeatedly and justified by the self-concept it protects, against a
specific coupled neighbor whose own genuine need is what the avoidance
is actually organized around not registering.

\emph{Domain instances.} A regulator that repeatedly declines to act on
a coupled party's documented warnings, because acting would contradict
its self-concept as an effective guardian, as in official responses to
residents' repeated reports during the Flint water crisis; a
manufacturer that sets aside repeated internal safety warnings to
preserve its self-concept as a safety leader.

10.5b.18.18h Official Causal Weight Under Response-Time Constraint

Definition 10.5b.18.18h (Official Causal Weight, W\_o(t)). For any
negentropic operational layer producing a time-constrained response,
define W\_o(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} as the degree to which official's own
processing enters the causal pathway determining that response ---
formally, the sensitivity of the response to a counterfactual
perturbation of official's own state, holding operational's own
processing fixed. W\_o(t) = 0 means the response is fully determined by
operational alone, regardless of official's state; W\_o(t)
\textgreater{} 0 means official's own processing genuinely contributes
to determining the response. This is independent of α(t) (§10.5b.18.18,
the control-locus share): α(t) measures who holds functional authority
to act, while W\_o(t) measures whether official's own content is
causally load-bearing in what gets decided, and the two can vary
independently.

Definition 10.5b.18.18i (Registration State, r(t)). Independent of
W\_o(t), define r(t) ∈ \{0,1\} as whether official's own
perceptual/registration channel is actively receiving input during the
response window at all, whether or not that input carries any causal
weight. This yields three real, jointly exhaustive regimes for a
time-constrained response with W\_o(t) = 0: r(t) = 0 (official's own
registration is itself suspended for the responses duration, with the
resolved state discoverable only after the fact) and r(t) = 1
(official's registration remains active, receiving input, while still
carrying zero causal weight in producing the response) are both
zero-causal-weight regimes distinguished only by whether registration
itself continued; the third regime, W\_o(t) \textgreater{} 0, is the
case where official's own processing is genuinely causally involved,
which entails r(t) = 1 as a precondition.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18j (Response Quality Is Independent of Regime).
Let Q(t) denote response quality by whatever domain-appropriate measure
applies. Across all three regimes in 10.5b.18.18i, dQ/dW\_o ≈ 0 and
dQ/dr ≈ 0 for a genuinely negentropic operational layer operating at or
near its own effective ceiling: quality is a function of operational's
own competence, not of which regime governs official's involvement,
provided operational itself is intact. This does not claim the three
regimes are interchangeable in general --- only that regime alone does
not predict a quality difference, holding operational's own competence
fixed.

Proposition 10.5b.18.18k (Regime Selection by Response-Time Constraint,
Distinguishing Speed from Concealment). Let τ\_resp denote the time
available before the response must be resolved, and τ\_route denote the
real latency cost of routing decision-relevant content through
official's own processing channel. Where τ\_resp \textless{} τ\_route,
W\_o(t) is forced toward 0 by genuine capacity constraint --- official's
channel cannot complete its own processing within the time available,
independent of official's trustworthiness or any motive to exclude it.
This gives a real, checkable test distinguishing this mechanism from
§10.5b.9e.1's developmental path-sealing and §10.5b.18.18a's
narrative-constraint concealment, both of which hold W\_o(t) = 0 despite
τ\_resp ≥ τ\_route being satisfied --- ample time available, with
official excluded regardless. W\_o(t) = 0 when τ\_resp \textless{}
τ\_route is a structural speed constraint; W\_o(t) = 0 when τ\_resp ≥
τ\_route is concealment. The same observed surface pattern --- official
uninformed of a decision already made --- has two mechanically distinct
causes, and τ\_resp versus τ\_route is what distinguishes them.

\hypertarget{b.18.19-regime-specific-phenomenology}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.19 Regime-Specific
Phenomenology}\label{b.18.19-regime-specific-phenomenology}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.19 (Official Self-Model Content).}}

Independent of A(t) --- what official \emph{believes} about the
situation is a separate question from what official \emph{controls} ---
define Φ\_o(t) as a qualitative tag on official's own sincere
self-model:

\begin{itemize}
\item
  \textbf{Aligned:} baseline regime, or either non-baseline regime with
  D\_om inside its healthy band; official's self-model is a reasonably
  accurate, if incomplete, account of the container's situation.
\item
  \textbf{Substituted: high-entropy}, arising specifically from active
  M\_o,t\^{}false substitution (§10.5b.18.1, extended). Official does
  not merely lack information; it holds a specific, sincerely-believed,
  incorrect narrative --- an aggressive mimic is the sharpest instance,
  an organism whose signaling layer coherently presents the profile of a
  harmless or cooperative partner while its operational layer, which
  holds actual control, pursues extraction and displacement; the
  official model is coherent and wrong, and that coherence is what makes
  it effective cover. This tag requires active falsification to be
  occurring; it is not available in a configuration where
  ω\_meta\^{}up(t) is merely low without M\_o,t\^{}false substitution.
\item
  \textbf{Underdetermined: medium-entropy}, arising from access
  limitation (low ω\_meta\^{}up(t), §10.5b.18.2) without active
  falsification, since medium-entropy's official layer has no
  false-memory-operator mechanism available to it (§10.5b.18.7).
  Official does not hold a confident wrong belief; it holds no coherent
  account at all, and experiences the container's behavior --- including
  operational's own corrective action --- as inexplicable rather than as
  a story it has been given reason to believe.
\end{itemize}

Substituted and underdetermined are not two degrees of the same thing;
they are different mechanisms with different downstream behavior. A
substituted official layer acts on its false belief, which is precisely
what makes it useful as cover. An underdetermined official layer does
not have a belief coherent enough to act on with any confidence at all,
which is precisely why medium-entropy's official-in-charge episodes
(δ(t) = ceded) tend toward poor, not merely misinformed,
decision-making.

\hypertarget{b.18.20-override}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.20 Override}\label{b.18.20-override}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.20 (Override).}}

Where δ(t) = ceded and a container's divergence pressure ((1−I(t)) +
S̃(t), §10.5a) crosses a fitted override threshold θ\_ovr, or its
resource-debt trend (dδR/dt, §7.9) crosses θ\_ovr while realized
capacity R(t) is also trending toward deficit (dR/dt \textless{} 0)
rather than being held stable by passive recovery, while official holds
functional control, operational executes an override: A(t) is driven
back toward 1 over a characteristic timescale τ\_ovr (fitted), for
whatever duration the triggering condition remains active, then may
recede once it clears.

\emph{\textbf{Why the debt trigger is gated on R(t).}} δR(t) tracks
pre-recovery structural debt --- what a container's operational
lifestyle would cost if passive recovery ρ·(R\_target − R) were not
offsetting it (§7.9) --- and remains a genuine early-warning signal in
its own right: rising structural debt can indicate a container heading
toward unsustainability before it manifests as an actual resource
shortfall. But a container with strong passive recovery can show a sharp
δR(t) spike from brief, intense structural adaptation while its realized
R(t) stays fully healthy throughout, backfilled in real time. Triggering
Override on debt-trend alone in that case is a false alarm: a healthy,
resilient container gets its functional control disrupted for doing
exactly what it should. Gating the debt-trend trigger on R(t) actually
trending toward deficit preserves δR(t)'s early-warning value where it
is real --- debt accumulating faster than recovery can offset it ---
without firing on debt that recovery is already fully absorbing.

Override is mechanically distinct from voluntary flood (§10.5b.18.16),
and the two should not be conflated despite superficially similar
language (``operational takes over''). Voluntary flood operates on the
permeability plane --- it drives Π\_f(t) toward ceiling and engages the
full tipping-momentum machinery of §10.5b.7--10.5b.9, aimed at a lasting
realignment of the container's metamessenger composition. Override
operates on the control plane only --- it does not itself move Π\_f(t),
ω\_meta\^{}up(t), Ψ\_loyal(t), or Ψ\_total(t), and need not engage
tipping-event machinery at all. An override can be brief and small,
correcting a single bad decision made under ceded control and then
receding, or extended and large, holding functional control for as long
as official's ceded state persists; both are the same mechanism at
different magnitude and duration, governed by θ\_ovr and τ\_ovr rather
than by a separate large/small distinction. A container can therefore
experience many small overrides across its history without ever
undergoing a voluntary flood, and a voluntary flood, where it occurs, is
a much larger and rarer commitment than an override --- aimed at
changing why the container keeps needing correction, not merely
supplying the correction itself.

\hypertarget{b.18.21-historical-residue}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.21 Historical
Residue}\label{b.18.21-historical-residue}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.21 (Historical Residue and the
Composition of Official Content).}}

Let t\_0 denote the most recent time at which a container's D\_op(t\_0)
was within the low-entropy baseline's healthy band --- the last point
before operational's transformation began in earnest. Define M\_o\^{}0
:= M\_\{t\_0\} (§9.2), the container's memory state frozen at that
reference point.

Official's current content divides into three sources, not two. The
fraction ω\_meta\^{}up(t) is live, honestly-transmitted content. Of the
remaining gap, 1 − ω\_meta\^{}up(t), a portion φ\_patch(t) is actively
falsified --- M\_o,t\^{}false substitution, event-triggered per
§10.5b.18.1's extended definition, spiking specifically around
permeability leaks. What remains,

φ\_fossil(t) = {[}1 − ω\_meta\^{}up(t){]} − φ\_patch(t),

is historical residue: content official has never had occasion to update
because it was never freshly challenged, sourced instead from M\_o\^{}0
essentially unchanged since t\_0.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.21 (Division of Labor).}}

φ\_fossil(t) supplies official's baseline continuity --- the stable
general self-narrative persisting between leak events. φ\_patch(t)
supplies official's apparent responsiveness --- specific, spiky
corrections to particular glitches as they arise. Neither alone sustains
a convincing captive official layer: φ\_fossil(t) alone would leave
official visibly unable to account for anything recent; φ\_patch(t)
alone would require continuously rebuilding the entire self-narrative
from scratch, at far greater ongoing M\_decep(t) cost (§10.5b.18.4) than
patching isolated glitches against an otherwise-stable fossilized base.
A high φ\_fossil(t) is therefore not merely narratively convenient but
economically efficient: it minimizes how much of official's content
requires active, costly patching at any given moment, which is a
testable claim --- containers relying more heavily on fossilized content
should show measurably lower M\_decep(t) draw per unit of D\_op(t)
sustained, relative to containers attempting to sustain the same
divergence through active patching alone.

\hypertarget{b.18.22-self-corrosion-is-already-entailed}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.22 Self-Corrosion Is Already
Entailed}\label{b.18.22-self-corrosion-is-already-entailed}}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.22 (Self-Corrosion Chain).}}

The following chain requires no new machinery; it is a consequence of
definitions already given in this document, stated here explicitly
because it was not previously traced end to end. Sustaining
M\_o,t\^{}false substitution (§10.5b.18.1, extended) requires ongoing
M\_decep(t) draw (§10.5b.18.4). Unpaid M\_decep(t) accrues directly into
δR(t) per §7.9's debt convention. δR(t) contracts d\_A(t) via the
β\_R·δR̃ term already present in §10.3's formula. A high-entropy
container's own act of sustaining its captive official layer therefore
already, and unavoidably, contracts its own adaptive capacity ---
self-corrosion is an emergent property of the existing resource and
stress machinery, not a separate degradation term requiring independent
introduction.

\hypertarget{b.18.23-asymmetric-inter-layer-antagonism}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.23 Asymmetric Inter-Layer
Antagonism}\label{b.18.23-asymmetric-inter-layer-antagonism}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.23 (Aware-but-Inert, a fourth state of
Φ\_o(t)).}}

Alongside aligned, substituted, and underdetermined (Definition
10.5b.18.19), admit a fourth state: aware-but-inert. During an unpatched
leak --- a window where §10.5b.18.21's φ\_patch(t) has not yet caught up
to a glimpse that has already registered --- official can achieve
genuine, if momentary, awareness of operational's role in its own
captivity, and can form genuine antagonism toward it on that basis. This
antagonism has no behavioral consequence, since official's control-locus
share (1 − A(t), §10.5b.18.18) in the high-entropy regime is
characteristically at or near zero: awareness without any share of A(t)
to act through is antagonism without effect. A real, structural
consequence follows regardless of how brief the window is or how quickly
φ\_patch(t) catches up to it: an aware-but-inert episode is unavoidable,
not merely possible, at the moment of any genuine awareness event, since
awareness necessarily registers before any patch responding to it can be
constructed and applied -\/- the state's own duration can shrink toward
zero but cannot be skipped entirely, whether that duration is a
millisecond or spans most of the container's own lifetime. Definition
10.5b.18.23a (Suppressed-Awareness Residue, Distinct From Historical
Residue). This episode leaves a real trace in the container's own memory
state M(t) (§9.2) independent of whatever φ\_patch(t) subsequently
writes into official's own accessible content -\/- distinct from
§10.5b.18.21's φ\_fossil, which describes official content merely left
unchallenged since t\_0, not an actively-registered-then-suppressed
episode. Let R\_supp(t) denote the accumulated count, or
resource-weighted sum, of such episodes for a given container,
incremented at each aware-but-inert onset regardless of how promptly or
thoroughly it is subsequently patched: the episode changes the
container's actual state at the moment it occurs, and φ\_patch(t)'s
later rewrite alters what official's own narrative says about that
state, not the historical fact that the state changed. Proposition
10.5b.18.23b (Escalating Suppression Cost). Each subsequent φ\_patch(t)
rewrite targeting the same underlying awareness does not cost the same
as the first: M\_o,t\^{}false(t) ⊃ κ\_supp · R\_supp(t), κ\_supp
\textgreater{} 0 fitted, added to §10.5b.14.1's existing
self-interested-disinformation cost rather than replacing it -\/-
repeated suppression of the same residue is more expensive than a single
instance, since each rewrite must now also account for, or actively work
against, the accumulating weight of every prior
registered-and-suppressed episode, not just the immediately present one.
This gives the aware-but-inert state real, cumulative teeth even though
any single instance of it is behaviorally inert exactly as Definition
10.5b.18.23 states: no individual episode acts, but every episode taxes
every subsequent attempt to keep the same reality hidden, which is the
specific, structural reason official-layer concealment becomes more
expensive to maintain over a high-entropy container's own lifetime
rather than remaining at constant cost.

10.5b.18.23c Denial as Active Curation, and Operational Collusion

Definition 10.5b.18.23c (Denial as Active Curation). Official's denial,
in both regimes, is better modeled as an active curation strategy than
as passive ignorance: rather than official simply lacking access to
unwelcome information, official actively refuses entry to any input that
would disrupt a preferred self-narrative, functioning as a real, ongoing
filter operating on incoming signal rather than a mere absence of
awareness. This reframing matters because it makes denial a real,
ongoing expenditure -\/- maintaining a filter against specific classes
of input -\/- rather than a neutral default state requiring no
explanation.

\emph{Two-sided denial.} Denial runs on both sides of the firewall, with
different content. Official denies operational facts: the filter above
refuses entry to evidence of what the container is actually doing.
Operational denies official values: in the high-entropy regime, the
value-inverted operational layer (Definition 10.5b.19.1a) refuses the
official layer's own valuation of reciprocal coupling, which the captive
official layer still holds (Definition 10.5b.18.1). Each denial carries
its own maintenance cost within M\_decep(t), and the two are mutually
reinforcing: each layer's refusal removes the input that would correct
the other.

\emph{Domain instances.} Tobacco-industry suppression of its own health
research (official denying operational facts), alongside sales cultures
whose incentives reward practices the firm's stated values condemn
(operational denying official values).

Definition 10.5b.18.23d (Silence as Sub-Threshold Avoidance). A real,
distinct route exists for a container to avoid ever entering even the
aware-but-inert state (§10.5b.18.23): where an incoming signal (a direct
request, a clear ask) would, if genuinely registered, immediately
confirm the falsity of official's own curated narrative, non-response
can function specifically to keep that signal from ever crossing into
registered awareness at all, rather than registering it and then
suppressing it via φ\_patch(t). This is mechanically distinct from
ordinary aware-but-inert: the latter requires a genuine, if momentary,
awareness episode before suppression catches up (§10.5b.18.23's own
unavoidability claim), while sub-threshold avoidance is a route that
avoids triggering that episode in the first place, at the cost of a
non-response the sender directly experiences as absence rather than as a
witnessed, if inert, moment of contact.

Proposition 10.5b.18.23e (Operational Collusion With Denial, a Distinct
Medium-Entropy Failure Mode). Medium-entropy's operational layer is
established elsewhere in this document (§10.5b.14.3b) as reliably
corrective, even where slow or patient. A real, distinct failure mode
exists alongside this: operational, on a specific matter, declining to
correct at all -\/- not losing the capacity to correct through resource
exhaustion (already covered by §10.5b.9a.3's cycling machinery), but
adopting official's own avoidance strategy directly, because confronting
the truth on this specific matter is assessed, however tacitly, as
costing more than the silence does. This is a standing, reversible state
rather than an automatic reclassification: a container's operational
layer colluding with denial on one specific matter does not by itself
make the container high-entropy, any more than a single entropic action
does (§10.5b.18.18g's own retention-versus-rewrite test already
establishes this asymmetry for isolated acts; the same logic extends to
isolated instances of operational collusion).

Operational's antagonism toward official runs the opposite direction and
is fully effective, requiring no new channel of its own. Historical
residue (§10.5b.18.21) is not neutral content to operational --- it is a
standing record of what operational itself destroyed to become what it
currently is, and a latent threat should it ever regain influence.
Operational's antagonism toward that residue is expressed directly
through mechanisms already established: M\_o,t\^{}false substitution
suppresses it, and extraction's ordinary resource pressure (§8.6a)
starves whatever maintenance the fossil would otherwise receive. The
asymmetry is total: operational's antagonism acts because operational
holds A(t) ≈ 1; official's antagonism, where it forms at all, cannot,
because it holds essentially none.

\textbf{10.5b.18.23f Forms of Denial, the Lagging Mirror, and the Mirror
Angle}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.23f (Two Forms of Denial).}} Denial
takes two forms, and each can occur in either layer. Curation removes
information: it is the input filter of Definition 10.5b.18.23c, refusing
entry to signal that would correct the self-model. Grandiosity adds
false information: it is inaccurate negentropic self-feedback, a
self-estimate that reports better performance than the actual one.
Official grandiosity is measured against the container's default,

G\_o(t) = max(0, D(O\_p(P), P*) − D(O\_o(P), P*)) ∈ {[}0,1{]},

the amount by which the official representation places the container
closer to its negentropic default P* than its operation actually is,
with D the base-2 Jensen--Shannon default. Operational grandiosity is
measured against the container's entropic performance,

G\_p(t) = max(0, Ŷ\_p(t) − Y\_p(t)) / max(Ŷ\_p(t), ε\_G) ∈ {[}0,1{]},

where Y\_p(t) = Σ\_j η\_i·X\_\{i,j\}(t) is the metabolized extraction
yield the container actually realizes (§8.6a), Ŷ\_p(t) the yield its
operational policy is calibrated to, and ε\_G \textgreater{} 0 a small
guard. Each layer thus holds an idealized self-image it falls short of,
negentropic in the official layer and entropic in the operational layer.
Curation in the official layer denies operational facts (Definition
10.5b.18.23c); curation in the operational layer denies both the
official values and the container's own restorative drive, which the
operational layer registers only as the external friction F\_env of
Proposition 10.5b.19.1b, link (3), never as a term in its own target, so
that longing is represented as envy.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.23g (The Lagging Mirror).}} The
official self-image is driven by the operational record and lags behind
it. Let E\_disp(t) = (Σ\_j X\_\{i,j\}(t) + H\_i(t))/R\_i\^{}max be the
container's normalized extraction and displacement, the volume of
Definition 10.5b.13.2(a), and let the official layer's target inflation
be T\_o(t) = min(1, κ\_E·E\_disp(t) + κ\_Gp·G\_p(t)), with κ\_E, κ\_Gp ≥
0 fitted, so that the official layer must cover both what the
operational layer does and how far the operational layer inflates it. As
a reduced-form law for the gap's evolution under ℱ's dynamics, fitted
rather than derived,

dG\_o/dt = (T\_o(t) − G\_o(t)) / τ\_G, τ\_G \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

which keeps G\_o in {[}0,1{]}. The lag gap Δ\_lag(t) = T\_o(t) − G\_o(t)
satisfies dΔ\_lag/dt = dT\_o/dt − Δ\_lag/τ\_G, so Δ\_lag(t) =
Δ\_lag(t\_0)·e\^{}\{−(t−t\_0)/τ\_G\} + ∫\_\{t\_0\}\^{}\{t\}
e\^{}\{−(t−s)/τ\_G\}·(dT\_o/ds) ds: it is the exponentially weighted
recent growth of the target, near zero on a plateau, including the
plateau at peak extraction, and largest during and just after a surge.
Keeping the official account in step costs a rewriting load proportional
to how fast it must move,

M\_rewrite(t) = κ\_rw·\textbar dG\_o/dt\textbar{} =
κ\_rw·\textbar Δ\_lag(t)\textbar/τ\_G, κ\_rw \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

which enters M\_decep,int(t) (Definition 10.5b.18.4) as an additive
component. Because M\_rewrite scales with the rate at which the official
account must change rather than with its level, a surge in extraction
raises the cost of the cover faster than its size. Unpaid M\_rewrite
accrues into δR (§7.9) and, where it exceeds what the container can keep
paying, reaches flooding pathway (c) of Definition 10.5b.13.2 through
the rate before the level, and it competes with the patching capacity
Ṗ\_patch of pathway (d).

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.23h (Mirror Angle).}} The mirror angle
measures how far the official self-representation's motion has turned
against the operational layer's actual motion:

θ\_mirror(t) = arccos( ⟨Ȯ\_o(t), Ȯ\_p(t)⟩\_\{g\_t\} /
(‖Ȯ\_o(t)‖\_\{g\_t\}·‖Ȯ\_p(t)‖\_\{g\_t\}) ) ∈ {[}0, π{]},

with Ȯ\_o = dO\_o(P(t))/dt and Ȯ\_p = dO\_p(P(t))/dt estimated by finite
differences over a window, and θ\_mirror recorded as unavailable where
either derivative is zero. At 0 the official account moves with the
operation. Between 0 and π/2 it is distorted, inflated or partial, but
its projection onto the operational motion is still positive. At π/2
that projection crosses zero; this is the tipping point at which one
property begins to mimic its opposite, entropic operation presenting as
negentropic. Past π/2 the projection is negative and the official
account reports the opposite of the operation, and at π the reversal is
complete. The angle gives the high-entropy case of Mirror Misrecognition
(Definition 10.5b.2.13) a measurable form. Its relation to the lagging
mirror is stated as a model to be tested against the measured angle,

θ\_mirror ≈ π·G\_o\^{}ν, ν = ln 2 / ln(1/G\_½), G\_½ ∈ (0,1) fitted,

which is increasing in G\_o and crosses π/2 exactly where G\_o = G\_½.
Under it the chase alone carries an escalating container past the
tipping point: if T\_o(t) ≥ T̄ \textgreater{} G\_½ for all t ≥ t\_1, then
G\_o(t) ≥ T̄ − (T̄ − G\_o(t\_1))·e\^{}\{−(t−t\_1)/τ\_G\}, so θ\_mirror
exceeds π/2 no later than t\_1 + τ\_G·ln{[}(T̄ − G\_o(t\_1))/(T̄ −
G\_½){]}, with no separate cause required.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.23i (Exposure Follows Surges).}}
Because the official account lags the operation, the visible discrepancy
between account and action is Δ\_lag(t), not the size of the extraction.
Where the hazard of exposure, meaning detection, disclosure or a leak of
the operational record, is increasing in \textbar Δ\_lag(t)\textbar,
exposure events cluster during and just after surges in extraction and
fall toward their baseline on plateaus, including the plateau at peak
extraction, even though peak extraction is when the operational record
is worst. The rewriting load and the exposure hazard share the same
driver, so the container is most expensive to keep covered at the
moments it is most likely to be seen.

\emph{Domain instances.} Modern neural networks are systematically
overconfident: the confidence they report for their outputs exceeds
their accuracy, most of all on inputs unlike their training data (Guo et
al. 2017), an official self-report better than the operation it
describes. The St. Matthew Island reindeer grew as if forage were
unlimited, the feedback that would have registered depletion lagging
behind the extraction, until the population overshot and collapsed
(Klein 1968): extraction calibrated to a yield the range no longer
supplied. A tumor grows as if its supply were unlimited, falls short of
that entropic ideal as it outgrows its blood supply and develops hypoxic
and necrotic regions, and must keep rewriting its environment by
signaling for new vessels, which form chaotic and leaky, so the
shortfall recurs; the hypoxic regions the gap creates are where its most
aggressive and metastatic cells arise (Höckel and Vaupel 2001), the cost
of the cover driving the breakup. In acute promyelocytic leukemia, the
malignant cells' maturation program is blocked rather than destroyed,
and all-trans retinoic acid releases the block so that the cells
differentiate into normal mature cells (Huang et al. 1988), a
restorative drive that persisted throughout. In engineered monitoring,
alert thresholds relaxed step by step as a system's real error rate
rises keep its dashboard green, each relaxation a rewrite of the
official account that lags the failure it covers; technical debt follows
the same pattern, inconsistencies patched with workaround layers until
maintaining the patches outgrows the system's capacity, and in both the
failures show through during rapid growth, before the cover catches up.

\hypertarget{b.18.24-negentropic-sabotage}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.24 Negentropic
Sabotage}\label{b.18.24-negentropic-sabotage}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.24 (Two Further Negentropic
Actions).}}

Within the closed population's negentropic-oriented fraction
(Ψ\_loyal(t), §10.5b.18.12), two actions beyond disclosure-pacing are
available in the high-entropy regime specifically:

\textbf{Search redirection.} Where operational's active search for new
coupling targets is underway (Part 10 Addendum III),
negentropic-oriented metamessengers can bias that search's targeting
away from candidates matching the extraction profile (§8.6a) and toward
a genuine, healthy external anchor instead --- subverting the courting
mechanism's own targeting function from within, rather than opposing it
directly.

\textbf{Defensive leakage.} Negentropic-oriented metamessengers can
deliberately allow, rather than merely fail to prevent, a glimpse of
operational reality to reach official specifically during an active
coupling attempt with a prospective target. Under the aware-but-inert
state (Definition 10.5b.18.23), official retains one lever even while
otherwise without effect: where its presentation is the interface a
prospective target actually sees (staged delegation, §10.5b.18.18), a
leaked glimpse can make that presentation visibly unconvincing ---
official cannot redirect operations, but it can make itself a poor lure.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.24 (Self-Sabotage as a Collapse
Pathway).}}

Where defensive leakage succeeds in repelling a prospective coupling
target during active courting, the container loses that extraction
opportunity without external discovery or ordinary resource exhaustion
having occurred --- a collapse pathway originating entirely within the
container's own metamessenger population, distinct from every
predation-ending mechanism already in this document (external anchoring,
cascade termination via fragmentation, resource depletion). Repeated
defensive leakage, where Ψ\_loyal(t) is high enough to sustain it,
predicts reduced successful-courting rates holding external target
availability constant --- separable in principle from a container simply
operating in a target-poor environment.

\emph{Domain instances.} Leaks from inside an acquirer that kill a
predatory deal before it closes.

\hypertarget{b.18.25-structural-extraction-dependence}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.25 Structural Extraction
Dependence}\label{b.18.25-structural-extraction-dependence}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.25 (Structural Extraction
Dependence).}}

A high-entropy container is structurally extraction-dependent at time t
when

P\_prod(t) \textless{} M\_maint(C,t) + M\_decep(t),

production insufficient to cover baseline maintenance and self-deception
cost even absent any further adaptive work. Where this holds, extraction
inflow (E\_exch(t) contributed by X\_\{i,j\}'s inbound sum, §8.6a) is
not marginal supplementation but load-bearing for the container's
continued existence at its current configuration. This is stronger and
more falsifiable than ordinary resource debt: an ordinarily-indebted
container could return to solvency by reducing W(t); a structurally
extraction-dependent container cannot, since the deficit persists at
W(t) = 0.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.25 (Mechanical Basis for Grid
Starvation).}}

Where §8.3.1's preferential attachment (Part 8 Addendum I) suppresses a
predator's first-contact rate and existing couplings continue to
terminate through ordinary cascade dynamics (§8.8a), a structurally
extraction-dependent container faces not merely reduced growth but an
unavoidable, deepening resource deficit with no self-sustaining floor
beneath it. This gives the grid-wide starvation of isolated predators
its full mechanical force, as a literal resource consequence rather than
a narrative one.

\hypertarget{b.18.26-accurate-self-accounting}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.26 Accurate
Self-Accounting}\label{b.18.26-accurate-self-accounting}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.26 (Causal Attribution
Distribution).}}

For a container's trajectory over an interval {[}t′, t{]}, let
A\_true(t′,t) be a distribution over cause-categories --- at minimum
\{self-originated, externally-imposed\}, more finely gradated where the
domain supports it --- describing the true decomposition of what drove
the container's change of state over that interval. Let A\_self(t′,t) be
the container's own, currently-held account of that same decomposition,
sourced from M\_o,t (or, in a medium-entropy container where official is
not the operative self-account, from whichever layer currently holds the
container's working narrative of its own history). Define attribution
divergence

D\_attr(t′,t) = D(A\_self(t′,t), A\_true(t′,t)),

using §6.3's existing divergence machinery, applied here to causal
attribution rather than to outcome-space representations directly.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.26 (Accuracy, Not Extremity, Is What
Heals).}}

Neither A\_self systematically over-weighting self-origination (excess
internal attribution) nor systematically over-weighting external
imposition (excess externalization) constitutes healing; both are cases
of D\_attr(t′,t) \textgreater{} 0, differing only in direction. Only
A\_self converging toward A\_true --- D\_attr(t′,t) → 0 --- constitutes
the state this document's healing and reset mechanisms presuppose. This
is not a new claim about what healing requires; it is Proposition 6.3.1
(Part 6 Addendum I) applied to a specific representational content, a
container's account of its own causal history, rather than to Ω
generally.

\emph{Domain instances.} Blameless post-mortems, which attribute an
incident accurately between system and operator, outperform both
scapegoating and blanket external blame in preventing recurrence.

\hypertarget{b.18.27-regime-dependent-pacing-of-attribution-correction}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.27 Regime-Dependent Pacing of Attribution
Correction}\label{b.18.27-regime-dependent-pacing-of-attribution-correction}}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.27 (Attribution Correction Follows
Disclosure Pacing).}}

D\_attr(t′,t) → 0 is a special case of the general disclosure process
§10.5b.18.15's shared shock-avoidance restraint already governs, not a
separate correction requiring its own pacing rule. In the high-entropy
regime, a sudden, complete jump from high to near-zero D\_attr(t′,t) ---
full, accurate attribution arriving all at once --- carries the same
informational-shock risk any sudden permeability flood does, and should
be expected to follow Ψ\_loyal(t)'s pacing logic (Definition
10.5b.18.15): gradual convergence, tolerable increments, not a single
corrective event. In the medium-entropy regime, by contrast,
Ψ\_total(t)-conditioned voluntary flood (Definition 10.5b.18.16) already
establishes that a full, sudden realignment can be survived, and in the
right conditions is the superior strategy to gradual convergence. A
medium-entropy container's attribution correction can therefore arrive
as a single realization event where confidence conditions support it,
while a high-entropy container's cannot --- the same asymmetry Addendum
III already established for disclosure generally, now understood to
cover self-accounting specifically rather than being a separate case.

\hypertarget{b.18.28-cross-container-capture-share}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.28 Cross-Container Capture
Share}\label{b.18.28-cross-container-capture-share}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.28 (Cross-Container Capture Share).}}

Definition 10.5b.18.18's control locus A(t) partitions a single
container's functional authority between its own two layers. Where
container i is coupled to j via Λ\_ij(t) and j exerts direct steering
influence over i's own operational dynamics --- beyond X\_\{i,j\}'s
ordinary resource extraction --- that partition admits a third claimant.
Define A\_cap(i,j,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, the share of i's own functional
systems control attributable to j, so that

A\_i(t) + A\_o(t) + A\_cap(i,j,t) = 1,

where A\_i(t) is i's own operational share and A\_o(t) is i's own
official share of whatever control remains once j's captured share is
removed. A\_cap(i,j,t) = 0 recovers Definition 10.5b.18.18 exactly as a
special case.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.28b (The Influence/Control
Threshold).}}

Let Θ\_capture ∈ (0,1) be a fitted threshold. A\_cap(i,j,t) \textless{}
Θ\_capture is under the influence: i's own trajectory is measurably
pressured by j but i retains primary agency. A\_cap(i,j,t) ≥ Θ\_capture
is under total control: j is effectively driving i's operational
dynamics, and i's own agency during this interval is not the primary
determinant of what i did.

\hypertarget{b.18.29-capture-origin-healing}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.29 Capture-Origin
Healing}\label{b.18.29-capture-origin-healing}}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.29 (Two Origins, Conditional
Prognosis).}}

A container may reach high-entropy classification by either of two
routes: the original, internally-developmental route this document has
assumed throughout, or a capture-origin route, where sustained
A\_cap(i,j,t) ≥ Θ\_capture during a coupling episode with some predator
j drove the transition. Capture-origin containers have access to a
faster healing path than developmental-origin containers --- but only
conditionally.

The condition is D\_attr(t′,t) → 0 applied specifically to the capture
episode: does the container's own current self-account correctly
distinguish the interval where A\_cap(i,j,t) ≥ Θ\_capture (genuine
external control, properly not weighted as the container's own agency)
from any interval where A\_cap(i,j,t) \textless{} Θ\_capture (genuine
influence, where the container's own agency remains the primary
determinant and accountability accordingly remains its own).
Over-attributing the whole episode to total control when much of it was
merely influence is excess externalization; over-attributing to its own
agency when much of it was genuine capture is excess internal
attribution. Both are D\_attr(t′,t) \textgreater{} 0 by Definition
10.5b.18.26, and by Proposition 10.5b.18.26, neither realizes the faster
healing path --- a capture-origin container with inaccurate
self-accounting should show a healing rate indistinguishable from a
developmental-origin container, despite its different history, since the
origin's advantage is conditional on the accounting rather than on the
origin alone.

\hypertarget{b.18.30-attribution-and-plurality}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.30 Attribution and
Plurality}\label{b.18.30-attribution-and-plurality}}

\emph{\textbf{Corollary 10.5b.18.30 (Attribution Is Often Genuinely
Plural).}}

Where A\_true(t′,t) itself holds real mass on both self-origination and
external imposition over the same interval --- a container genuinely was
both under real external pressure and genuinely exercising its own
agency within it, neither cancelling the other --- Proposition 6.3.1
(Part 6 Addendum I) applies directly: A\_self collapsing that plurality
into a single verdict, in either direction, does not reduce
D\_attr(t′,t), it increases it. A capture-origin container's healthiest
available self-account is frequently not ``I was fully controlled'' or
``I was fully responsible'' but both, held simultaneously without either
canceling the other --- the same structure Part 6 Addendum I already
established for operational reality generally, applied here to a
container's account of its own captured history.

\hypertarget{b.18.31-attribution-as-a-precondition-for-the-positive-despair-counterpart}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.31 Attribution as a Precondition for the Positive
Despair
Counterpart}\label{b.18.31-attribution-as-a-precondition-for-the-positive-despair-counterpart}}

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.18.31 (Accurate Attribution Gates the
Lazarus Path).}}

The despair signature (Definition 10.5b.18.17) is diagnosed by
official-convergence collapse co-occurring with Γ\_redir(t) ≈ 0. Where
Γ\_redir(t) is meaningfully above zero at the same moment, this
document's existing account already permits the same realization to
ground redirection-seeking rather than despair. This proposition adds
the missing condition for that better outcome to actually be reached: it
requires D\_attr(t′,t) → 0 specifically on the question the despair
signature turns on --- correctly attributing the container's instability
to repeated external knocking rather than to internal fault, where that
is in fact true. A container with Γ\_redir(t) available but still
carrying an inaccurate, self-blaming A\_self cannot access the positive
path Γ\_redir(t) alone would otherwise permit; availability of
redirection and accuracy of self-attribution are jointly necessary, and
this document's full account of the positive counterpart itself --- what
a successfully-completed realization of this kind constitutes, and what
persists afterward --- is given separately in Part 10 Addendum I.

\hypertarget{b.18.32-individual-conversion-and-compositional-contagion}{%
\subsection{10.5b.18.32 Individual Conversion and Compositional
Contagion}\label{b.18.32-individual-conversion-and-compositional-contagion}}

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.18.32 (Metamessenger Conversion
Dynamics).}} §10.5b.18.2's ω\_meta(t) and §10.5b.18.12's Ψ\_loyal(t) are
population-level fractions, read as aggregate statistics of the
meta-representation layer's own sub-process population (§6.1's P\_m(t) =
Σ\_k φ\_k(t)·P\_m\^{}(k)(t)); neither is otherwise given
individual-level dynamics of its own. This subsection supplies that
layer beneath both quantities, recovering each as a derived aggregate of
an explicitly individuated population rather than leaving either as an
unmodeled population-level primitive.

For each sub-process k, define two per-process state variables, distinct
in kind and in how they change: a\_k(t) ∈ 0,1, whether k is currently
transmitting operational reality upward (behavior --- the same axis
ω\_meta\^{}up(t) already aggregates), and o\_k(t) ∈ 0,1, whether k is
currently negentropic- or entropic-oriented (disposition --- the same
axis Ψ\_loyal(t) already aggregates, per Definition 10.5b.18.12's own
distinction between behavior and orientation, retained here rather than
collapsed). Both aggregates are recovered as exact identities, not new
assumptions:

ω\_meta\^{}up(t) = Σ\_k φ\_k(t)·a\_k(t) / Σ\_k φ\_k(t),

Ψ\_loyal(t) = Σ\_k: a\_k(t)=0 φ\_k(t)·o\_k(t) / Σ\_k: a\_k(t)=0 φ\_k(t),

the second restricted to the currently-closed sub-population exactly as
Definition 10.5b.18.12 already specifies.

Individual conversion is what was previously left unmodeled. Define an
internal proximity structure ν\_kl(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} among sub-processes
within a single container's own meta-representation layer --- functional
or informational adjacency within P\_m itself, distinct from and
internal to a single container, not to be confused with the
container-to-container coupling network Λ\_ij(t) (§8.5). A sub-process
k's own orientation converts as a local-contagion process, hazard-rated
on its neighborhood's current composition:

λ\_conv(k,t) = κ\_conv · {[}Σ\_l ν\_kl(t)·o\_l(t){]} / {[}Σ\_l
ν\_kl(t){]}, κ\_conv \textgreater{} 0 fitted,

with P(o\_k flips within {[}t, t+dt{]}) ≈ λ\_conv(k,t)·dt when the flip
is toward the locally-dominant orientation, zero otherwise --- an
ordinary threshold-contagion process run over the metamessenger
population's own internal graph, structurally the same class of object
as an epidemic or opinion-diffusion model, applied here to a population
this document had previously only measured in aggregate.

A separate, non-local channel exists alongside ν\_kl(t)'s local
structure, and answers directly to the specific case of a conversion
reaching a topologically distant sub-process without passing through the
intervening population: a glimpse event (g(t), §10.5b.0.9) or an
ordinary permeability event reaching the whole container reaches every
sub-process simultaneously, regardless of ν\_kl(t) adjacency, each
independently and typically only weakly:

λ\_jump(k,t) = κ\_jump · g(t), κ\_jump \textgreater{} 0 fitted, κ\_jump
≪ κ\_conv in the ordinary case,

contributing an additional, non-local term to k's own conversion hazard
alongside λ\_conv(k,t) rather than replacing it. This is the formal
content of a shift originating in one part of the metamessenger
population reaching an unconnected part of the same population without
local propagation between them: not a stronger local pull, but a shared,
simultaneous exposure to the same triggering event, which this document
already has a name and a mechanism for (g(t)) rather than requiring a
new primitive.

Recovery conditions, consistent with this document's general convention.
κ\_jump = 0 recovers a purely local contagion process, propagation
limited to ν\_kl(t)'s own connected structure. ν\_kl(t) → uniform across
all pairs (a fully-connected, undifferentiated population) recovers a
simple mean-field logistic contagion in the aggregate Ψ\_loyal(t) alone,
with no internal structure left to distinguish local from non-local
spread --- the case this document's pre-existing trailing-slope proxy
(Definition 10.5b.18.12) implicitly assumed, without previously stating
so. Where an adapter can supply direct sub-process-level data (an
internal survey, dissent-channel content analysis --- the same
instruments Definition 10.5b.18.12 already names as preferable to the
trailing-slope default), this individuated model supplies a
forward-predictive account of how Ψ\_loyal(t) and ω\_meta\^{}up(t)
evolve, rather than only a trailing, already-realized estimate of where
they currently stand.

Falsifiable prediction, distinct from anything the aggregate account
alone produces. Genuine local contagion should show a sigmoidal uptake
signature in ω\_meta\^{}up(t) or Ψ\_loyal(t) once a locally-clustered
sub-population crosses its own tipping density --- a nonlinear
acceleration this document's existing trailing-slope proxy, built to
detect gradual linear drift, is not specifically built to distinguish
from ordinary gradual disclosure. Separately and more specifically: a
conversion registering among sub-processes with no shared ν\_kl(t)
adjacency to any already-converted sub-process, coincident with a
container-wide glimpse or permeability event, is the specific, checkable
signature of the non-local channel operating --- and its absence, where
a distant conversion is observed without any such coincident event,
would be evidence against κ\_jump's own mechanism as stated, requiring
either a revised trigger or an as-yet-unmodeled adjacency this
document's ν\_kl(t) construction has not captured.

\hypertarget{b.19-the-entropic-reward-cycle-and-the-extraction-hunger-accumulator}{%
\subsection{10.5b.19 The Entropic Reward Cycle and the Extraction-Hunger
Accumulator}\label{b.19-the-entropic-reward-cycle-and-the-extraction-hunger-accumulator}}

§8.6a's extraction mechanism and §10.5b.16's compulsive closure both
describe a container's dynamics being driven by something other than
genuine divergence-tracking. This subsection formalizes a third,
self-sustaining driver: extraction and displacement (§8.6a) can be
intrinsically rewarding to the extracting container, independent of any
external shock, and that reward can itself escalate future extraction
--- a purely internal divergence driver requiring no external grid
consequence to operate.

\emph{\textbf{Definition 10.5b.19.1 (Extraction-Hunger Accumulator).}}

Define Z\_hun(t) ∈ {[}0,1{]}, the structural mirror of the resilience
accumulator Z(t) (§10.5b.9a) --- where Z(t) is built by surviving knocks
and lowers future cost, Z\_hun(t) is built by rewarded extraction and
raises future extraction magnitude:

dZ\_hun/dt = η\_hun · Z\_hun(t)(1−Z\_hun(t)) · R\_ext(t),

where R\_ext(t) is a reward signal tied to realized extraction
(adapter-defined; the metabolized fraction η\_i·X\_\{i,j\}(t) of §8.6a
is a natural default) and η\_hun \textgreater{} 0 is fitted.

10.5b.19.1a Deception as an Intrinsic Reward, Distinct From Extraction

Definition 10.5b.19.1a (Value-Inverted Self-Concept). A high-entropy
container's operational core does not merely hold a false belief about
its own official layer (§10.5b.18.1's captive official layer); at the
operational level, it can hold an inverted value orientation, believing
entropy itself -\/- not merely its own specific conduct -\/- to be the
true, meaningful order of things, with negentropic alignment in other
containers read not as a neutral fact but as a standing rebuke. This is
the content underlying §10.5b.0.9e's inversion mechanism, not a separate
claim: the more thoroughly this value-inversion holds, the more
completely official's own compensating narrative must invert relative to
it.

\emph{Domain instances.} Corporate cultures whose incentives reward the
harm their stated values condemn, as in sales-quota regimes that produce
fraudulent accounts.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.19.1b (Lockout Denial and Contrast
Suppression).}} A high-entropy container approaches the containers of
its grid from a structural position this proposition makes explicit. Let
L(i,t) ∈ {[}0,1{]} be its lockout from reciprocal coupling, the fraction
of its couplings that cannot be reciprocal at its current permeability;
L(i,t) → 1 as Π\_f(i,t) → 0 (Corollary 10.5b.13b.9: open exchange is
possible only between open systems). The chain has six links.

(1) Lockout: L(i,t) high. (2) Longing: the captive official layer
(Definition 10.5b.18.1) still holds the true valuation of reciprocal
coupling, V\_o(recip) high, because it is the layer that retains the
container's genuine content (Definition 10.5b.2.16), and the restorative
drive of Proposition 10.5b.0.0c is still directed at it. (3) Friction:
exposure to visible reciprocal coupling that the container cannot
metabolize becomes heat rather than nourishment, a forcing on the
operational layer

F\_env(i,t) = κ\_env·Ξ\_recip(i,t)·(1 − Π\_f(i,t)),

where Ξ\_recip(i,t) is the resource-weighted density of reciprocal
couplings within i's observation range, consistent with Self-Corrosion's
treatment of unmetabolized flux as direct cost (Definition 10.5b.19.7);
in a container with a self-model this friction is experienced as envy.
(4) Devaluation: the value-inverted operational layer (Definition
10.5b.19.1a) sustains its inversion by devaluing what it cannot reach,
V\_p(recip) = V\_o(recip)·(1 − κ\_dev·ρ\_po(i,t)·L(i,t)), with κ\_dev ∈
{[}0,1{]} fitted so that V\_p(recip) ≥ 0 and ρ\_po the registration
weight of Definition 10.5b.0.0d.1, and ranking those engaged in it as
lesser; this is the operational side of the two-sided denial of
Definition 10.5b.18.23c, and in a container with a self-model it is
experienced as contempt. (5) Contrast suppression: visible reciprocal
coupling is standing evidence against the denial, so part of i's outward
forcing is directed not at extraction from any particular neighbor but
at the grid, reducing the contrast:

dΞ\_recip/dt ⊇ −κ\_cs·F\_grid(i,t), F\_grid(i,t) =
κ\_grid·{[}F\_env(i,t) + κ\_d·M\_decep(i,t){]},

degrading reciprocal couplings i is not part of and pushing grid norms
toward extraction. This supplies a non-instrumental motive for
disturbing a negentropic neighbor's own messaging, independent of any
extraction-facilitation purpose (§8.6a's instrumental account remains
valid alongside it): where a neighbor proves resistant to capture, the
same motive predicts a shift toward degradation for its own sake, within
the ceiling §10.5b.18.18d's entropy threshold permits. (6) Saturation:
as Ξ\_recip → 0 the grid approaches the saturated condition of
Proposition 10.5b.13a.3, in which containers are forced to couple with
high-entropy neighbors because no alternative remains, which hands the
extractor dominance and leaves no reciprocal coupling to contradict the
denial. The loop is self-fulfilling niche construction. It also explains
Proposition 8.6a.1: nonconformist neighbors are targeted for what their
composition threatens, because they are the contrast.

Scope. The denial mechanism, links (2) and (4), requires a non-trivial
official layer. In simpler systems the grid-level signature, links (5)
and (6), appears without it, and the manuscript treats the denial as the
mechanism where a representation layer exists and grid-level degradation
as the signature that also appears where it cannot. Prediction: at fixed
extraction opportunity, F\_grid rises with Ξ\_recip near the container,
which pure extraction does not predict.

\emph{Domain instances.} Pariah states excluded from reciprocal trade
typically build an ideology that recasts the exclusion as chosen, as in
North Korea's doctrine of self-reliance, and act against the
international order itself rather than only against individual
neighbors. Organized crime, shut out of the legitimate economy, corrupts
courts, police and licensing, a grid-level capture rather than
extraction from particular targets alone. Predatory industries lobby for
rule changes, such as federal preemption of state lending law, that make
their model the norm. Without a representation layer: tumors acidify
their microenvironment through glycolytic metabolism, killing normal
cells while tolerating the acidity themselves (Gatenby's acid-mediated
invasion hypothesis); invasive plants release allelochemicals that alter
soil chemistry for the whole community (spotted knapweed, the
novel-weapons hypothesis); bacteria produce bacteriocins that kill
competitors at a cost to the producer.

\emph{\textbf{Proposition 10.5b.19.1d (Decay Versus Extractive Niche
Construction).}} Dissolution and breakdown are not uniformly
pathological. Let the dissolution or breakdown of container i over
{[}t′, t{]} change the grid's aggregate adaptive possibility by
Δd\_A\^{}grid = Σ\_k {[}d\_A(k,t) − d\_A(k,t′){]} and its
reciprocal-coupling density by ΔΞ\_recip. The breakdown is restorative
decay where Δd\_A\^{}grid ≥ 0 and ΔΞ\_recip ≥ 0, returning i's resources
to circulation among the remaining containers; it is extractive niche
construction where Δd\_A\^{}grid \textless{} 0 and ΔΞ\_recip \textless{}
0, reshaping the grid so that only the extractor's mode remains viable.
The sign of Δd\_A\^{}grid is the criterion. Collapse that releases
resources into reorganization is consistent with this document's
treatment of collapse as sometimes the precondition for renewal.

\emph{Domain instances.} Decomposition returns nutrients to soil and
feeds reciprocal cycling across an ecosystem, part of the larger Earth
system's self-repair; the orderly liquidation of a failed firm returns
capital and skilled labor to productive use. A tumor's remodeling of its
surrounding tissue, and an extractive firm's capture of its regulators,
lower both quantities.

Definition 10.5b.19.1c (Deception-Specific Reward, R\_decep). A distinct
reward channel from R\_ext above: the act of successfully deceiving a
coupled neighbor generates its own reward signal, R\_decep(t),
adapter-defined and separable from any extraction realized through that
deception. This matters because deception's own reward is not merely
positive feedback in general -\/- it is, specifically, an instance of
inaccurate negentropic feedback delivered to the deceiving container's
own operational core (successful deception is read internally as
confirmation that the container's own model of itself and its conduct is
working, is justified, is correct), and §10.5b.9e.1-9e.2 already
establishes that inaccurate negentropic feedback is structurally more
corrosive -\/- more verticality-producing -\/- than inaccurate entropic
feedback. A container rewarded specifically through R\_decep is
therefore not merely behaving badly for a reward; it is
self-administering the specific feedback type its own framework
identifies as most damaging, which gives compulsive deception-seeking a
real mechanistic basis distinct from ordinary extraction-hunger, whether
the container's own official layer narrates this pursuit as restoring
harmony or experiences it as bare, unexplained compulsion -\/-
10.5b.19.1a's value-inversion makes either narration available without
changing the underlying mechanism.

Z\_hun(t)'s own growth equation admits R\_decep(t) as an additional term
